**Session:** S-AA194-design (v6.3.10/v6.3.13, `c043cf5`/`4b630eb`, 2026-07-16, worktree `channel-intelligence-0716`) — John's own priority framing for this session: "a question can get answered fully non-stop in under 2 minutes, the message is fully routed among all correct agents" — named **Continuity**, an existing Type Taxonomy tag whose definition was tightened this session to explicitly cover both failure shapes (misrouted *and* stalled/dead-ended), not just misrouting, per John's own words.

**Standing rule added, John's explicit call:** `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` gained a new "Skill/Capability Disclosure" rule — before creating/editing any `skill_profiles` row, state (verified live, never from memory): is this a new Skill or an edit, what Capability it's assigned to, does that Capability have other Skills, who is it assigned to. Applies to every future session touching Skills/Capabilities/Agents, not just this one.

**`AA-194`** (Sam Reyes escalation misroute) — Owen's `qg-review-intent` sometimes recommended Nadia Farouk over Sam Reyes for failure-triage hand-offs because Sam's `intake-failure-intent.output_desc` was `null` while Nadia's was fully authored. Fixed: authored the missing `output_desc` (Supabase content only, no code, no kickoff doc — same shape as the earlier `AA-186` precedent). Live-verified against the real escalation scenario: Sam now correctly outranks Nadia. Broader roster gap (Marcus/Elena/Owen/Priya/Victoria/Dan/Eleanor's `library-catalog-intent` all missing `output_desc` too) logged, not fixed — `AA-194`'s row stays 🔶 Partial, not archived.

**`AA-160`** (found live during this session's own testing, not pre-existing on the backlog in this form) — Alex's `qa-answer-format` Skill was the one intent in the Q&A→display chain never pinned to `temperature: 0`, same failure class `AA-166` already fixed elsewhere in this exact pipeline (a required `body` field silently dropped on ~1-in-5 calls). Fixed: pinned `temperature: 0` (Supabase only). Live-verified 8/8 clean runs against the real dev endpoint using the exact question that originally triggered it. Archived to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`.

**`AA-182`** (residual schema-validation failures on catalog questions, found 2026-07-14, still open after `AA-166`'s earlier `temperature: 0` fix reduced but didn't eliminate it) — root-caused this session as a **generic harness gap, not capability-specific**: `callModel()`'s retry-once mechanism (`api/prompt/request-receivable.js`) already computed which required field a failed schema-tool call was missing but discarded that detail before the one retry it sends, so the retry was really just another non-deterministic roll of the dice. Fixed (`S-ARCH-RETRY-SPECIFICITY-01`, v6.3.10, `c043cf5`): the retry correction message now names the specific missing field(s). Node test 2/2, build clean, 1 file. Live-verified 8/8 clean on the exact repro (up from 1/5 failing) — benefits every schema-tool call platform-wide by construction, not just `library-catalog-intent`. Archived.

**`AA-191`** (`delegate_to_agent` write-bypass, previously flagged urgent) — moved from `docs/FEATURES.md` (now) to `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md` (next tier), John's explicit call: not currently worried about wrong-content-storage risk, Continuity is the actual priority right now.

**`AA-195`, found live by John immediately after the three fixes above shipped, same EMEA co-op seed question — the actual reason "Continuity is solid" was premature.** Real chat error ("Something went wrong reaching Marcus") during Owen's own regenerate-and-review delegation to Marcus. `durable_hops` evidence: Owen's `qg-review-intent` row failed with a hard `"Anthropic call failed: 400"` (a genuinely different failure class than anything fixed above — a request Anthropic rejected outright, not a post-200 validation gap), while Marcus's nested regenerate call was simultaneously orphaned `in_progress` at `hop_counter: 2`. **Root cause not confirmed this session, despite real effort:** John provided Vercel CLI access mid-session; used it (`vercel whoami` confirmed authenticated), but `--follow` doesn't support status-code filtering and hit a 5-minute query duration cap before capturing anything; non-`--follow` mode returned zero historical entries. ~16 live reproduction/resume attempts against the real dev endpoint did not reproduce the exact 400 again, though they did surface a real, related finding: the checkpoint/resume mechanism itself is fragile under this exact multi-hop scenario (6 of 12 fresh runs checkpointed instead of completing, several needed 2+ resumes). Confirmed via direct code read: only `resumeCapability()` persists a failure to `durable_hops` today — `runCapability()`'s fresh-call path has zero error persistence at all — which narrows the eventual root-cause search to `resumeCapability()`'s pending-delegation re-dispatch specifically, not "somewhere in a nested chain" generally.

**Shipped instead, per John's explicit call — permanent, not a temporary diagnostic hack:** `S-ARCH-FAILURE-DETAIL-01` (v6.3.13, `4b630eb`) persists the full Anthropic-rejection detail (already computed, previously discarded) into `durable_hops.error`, on both the resume path and the previously-uncovered fresh-call path — one shared helper, no capability-specific logic, zero added latency (only touches an already-happening write on an already-failed request). Node test 3/3, build clean, 1 file, code diff independently verified against the kickoff spec. **Honest live-test result, not glossed over:** neither Category L test observed an actual failure this pass — the fresh-path repro's premise (a bad `capability_slug` throwing) turned out to be factually wrong for the current code, and all resumed orphaned jobs from the investigation resolved successfully rather than failing again. Logged plainly on `AA-195`'s own row for whoever picks this up next. `AA-195` remains open, root cause unconfirmed — the next real occurrence will now leave full diagnostic detail in Supabase automatically.

**Session-wide QA discipline:** every fix this session was independently re-verified by this design session directly (live API calls, direct Supabase queries, direct code/diff reads) rather than accepted from either the coding sessions' own reports or from assumption — consistent with the standing verify-never-assert-from-memory rule. No blind fixes shipped; `AA-195` was explicitly left open rather than guessed at.

**Session:** S-LOG-08-design/S-LOG-08 + S-LOG-08-patch-design/S-LOG-08-patch (v6.3.14 `773d55e`, v6.3.15 `c930b63`, 2026-07-16, worktree `ai-logging-enforcement-0716`) — direct continuation of the same-day `AA-192a`/`b` work. After `AA-192b` shipped, John asked "what's next critical for AI logging"; the answer (`AA-192c`, `FetchContext.jsx`/`api/web-memory.js`) surfaced a serious, unrelated finding mid-investigation — `FetchContext.jsx` calls `logAICall(...)` with **no import anywhere in the file**, a genuine `ReferenceError` on every successful Fetch run since 2026-06-04 that also aborts the subsequent `PATCH /api/web-memory` call in the same code block. Before scoping a fix, John stepped back and asked the harder question directly: how is a day of careful AI-audit work still missing things, one at a time, whack-a-mole style — and pushed for a full architectural sweep instead of another incremental patch.

**Sweep execution — two sub-agents, one self-corrected, one didn't:** dispatched one server-side sweep (`api`/`lib`) and one client-side sweep (`src` + full `SERVICE_CATALOG`/`PATTERN_CATALOG` audit), both pointed at the shared checkout with no explicit worktree path in their prompts (a real process gap, not yet fixed at dispatch time). The server-side agent discovered mid-task that the shared checkout was 193 commits behind `origin/dev`, found a worktree that matched `origin/dev` HEAD exactly, and rerouted its own investigation there — its findings held up on every point independently re-checked afterward. The client-side agent did not self-correct, worked entirely from the stale checkout, and its report contained multiple confirmed-false claims as a result: that `patterns_used` (the real per-call pattern data) is never read anywhere in the AI Audit screen (false — `computeByPattern()`/`byService`, directly verified live on `origin/dev`, both genuinely compute real counts/costs from actual logged data, not the static catalog); that `DashboardScreen.jsx`/`PersonnelScreen.jsx`/`TeachScreen.jsx` still log the wrong model (false — already fixed same day by `AA-192a`/`b`, confirmed live); that a double-write between `execute.js`'s `logAgentTurn()` and `request-receivable.js`'s `sendRequest()` inflates displayed cost (the double-write is real, but already correctly deduplicated at read time by `AI-51`'s `pairedAgentTurnIds()`, confirmed live — cost is zeroed on the paired row); that `PersonnelScreen.jsx` still has an embedding-log client call missing tokens (false — that call was already removed in a prior, non-today session, `AA-190h`); that `src/aiPatterns.js` has 9 importers including `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` (false — 8 importers, confirmed via `git grep` directly against `origin/dev`; that file's import was already removed, `FEATURE: MI-51`).

**Every claim from both sweeps was then independently re-verified by this design session directly against `origin/dev` (commit `08d2ad9`)** — not relayed from either agent — before anything was reported as fact, per the standing verify-never-assert-from-memory rule, given the first relay already turned out to contain real errors. Confirmed real via direct code read: `api/plan.js`'s own core task-planning call (`action:'plan'`, default) and its `'title'` action have zero logging anywhere — the platform's central planning capability, invisible in cost/audit. `lib/agent-run.js`'s `callReflect()`/`callClaude()` have no logging built into the shared wrapper itself, so even `api/brief.js`'s already-fixed AGENT PATH never captures its own REFLECT sub-call's real cost. `lib/rag.js`'s `queryRAG()` embedding call is unlogged across all 4 real callers. `lib/search-harness.js`'s `the_reasoning` embedding calls are unlogged. `api/brief.js`'s LEGACY and PAT paths are unlogged (the AGENT PATH is correctly fixed). `api/web-memory.js`'s Haiku "learning" call remains unlogged (confirmed still true, unaffected by anything else — this absorbs `AA-192c`'s original scope). Client-side: `AIReviewTab.jsx` (dead `logAICall` import, real unlogged `/api/brief` calls), `TestTeamScreen.jsx` (zero logging, real `/api/rag-query`+`/api/brief` calls), `TaskInstructionsScreen.jsx` (zero logging, calls the same two unlogged `plan.js` actions), and `FetchContext.jsx`'s crash (confirmed unchanged). Catalog mismatches also confirmed real: `document-extraction` claims an AI/Structured-Output pattern for a fully deterministic route (`api/extract.js`, zero LLM calls, confirmed by grep); `guardrails-check` has no `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE` mapping, orphaned from the By-Service rollup; `prompt-assembly` is marked live with zero real callers anywhere.

**Root cause of why this kept happening, explained plainly to John:** `AA-190`'s migration explicitly scoped itself to "9 originally-found write sites" — a real, careful investigation, but never a claim of 100% coverage. The new gaps found today were never deferred or knowingly skipped (unlike `AA-192`'s client-side items, which were); they simply never surfaced in whatever grep/manual pass `AA-190` used, most likely because that investigation looked for *existing-but-wrong* logging code, and/or checked API routes without tracing into shared library functions (`lib/agent-run.js`, `lib/rag.js`) that sit one layer underneath them.

**Fix, per John's explicit direction — build the mechanical check, don't just fix the list:** `scripts/check-ai-logging-coverage.js` statically scans `api/`/`lib/`/`src/` for real LLM/embedding call markers (`api.anthropic.com`/`api.openai.com`/`embedContent(`) and flags any file lacking a corresponding `logActivity()`/`logAICall()` call — CRITICAL for zero-logging-attempt or an undefined-reference crash risk, WARNING for a real-call-count vs. log-call-count mismatch needing human review (explicitly not a formal verifier — can't trace per-branch gating logic). Verified to catch all 8 of the day's independently-confirmed real gaps before being considered done — the coding session's own verification caught one real bug in its own logic first (missing the `embedContent(` marker, which made `lib/search-harness.js` invisible and `api/web-memory.js` coincidentally look covered when its real gap is a different call in the same file). Referenced in `STANDARDS.md`'s Category M.

**Same-day patch, found by this design session's own independent re-run, not accepted from the coding report alone:** the freshly-shipped script flagged `src/hooks/useAIActivity.js` — the file that *defines* `logAICall` — as a permanent CRITICAL false positive (a file that defines a function isn't "calling it with no import"). Patched same day: `checkClientFile()` now strips `//` comments and the function's own definition signature before counting, which also correctly cleared a second, related false positive on `CreateWorkOrderScreen.jsx` (comment-only mentions of `logAICall`) as a legitimate side effect, independently re-verified by re-running the script directly rather than trusting the coding report.

**Close-out:** `AA-192` marked Done and archived (its own Dashboard/Personnel/Teach scope is complete; `TeachScreen.jsx`/`AA-192b` was its last real sub-session). `LOG-09` (server-side gaps), `LOG-10` (client-side gaps), `LOG-11` (catalog mismatches) logged with full detail as the confirmed-real findings for a future session to actually fix — this session's deliverable was the check, not the fixes. A concurrent session shipped a directly relevant `CLAUDE.md` update the same day: every sub-agent must now be given an explicit worktree path in its prompt text, never left to discover the (deliberately stale) shared checkout on its own — the exact gap that caused this session's client-side sweep to go wrong in the first place.

**Session:** S-ARCH-NESTED-RESUME-01-design/S-ARCH-NESTED-RESUME-01 (v6.3.16, `f84beb7`/`a7cbacc`, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-aa195-0716`) — `AA-195` root-caused and fixed, closing the gap `S-ARCH-FAILURE-DETAIL-01` (same day, earlier session) left open.

**Root cause, confirmed by reading the full execution path against real `ai_activity_log`/`durable_hops` timestamps for the original incident row (`64f1a7e7`, 2026-07-16 14:56:10 UTC), not guessed:** `S-ARCH-DURABLE-RESUME-02` (AA-185/AA-187, 2026-07-14) introduced a "nested checkpoint" case — when a `delegate_to_agent` dispatch's own target itself checkpoints mid-flight (out of shared budget), the outer job checkpoints too. But that handling clears `pending_delegation` to `null`, discarding the only field that recorded a delegation was still outstanding, while leaving `conversation_history` ending in an unresolved assistant `tool_use` turn. The next resume sees `pending_delegation: null`, assumes it's a normal replayable state, and re-enters `runLoop()` straight from that stale history — sending Anthropic a conversation with a dangling `tool_use` block, rejected with a hard 400.

**Timeline reconstruction that proved this, not assumed:** Owen's `qg-review-intent` review (own fresh top-level call, own hop counter) decides to retry via `delegate_to_agent` → Marcus. Enough budget remains, dispatch happens live, threading Owen's hop counter/deadline into Marcus's nested call (confirmed: Marcus's regenerate hops log as `depth1`/`depth2`, sharing Owen's counter). After Marcus's `depth2` turn completes, his own chain needs one more hop and trips its own pre-dispatch budget check (by then, ~46s of the shared ~55s effective budget is spent) — Marcus's chain checkpoints itself. `dispatchDelegation()` sees `delegateResult.status === 'in_progress'`, returns `nested_checkpoint`, **dropping `delegateResult.job_id` entirely**. Owen's row re-checkpoints with `pending_delegation: null`, conversation history unchanged (2 messages, dangling `tool_use`) — matching the observed row exactly. The client's `resolveInProgress()` poll loop (no backoff) immediately fires another resume; `resumeCapability()` sees `pending_delegation: null`, naively continues, and the malformed request is rejected — 0.47s after Marcus's last logged turn, consistent with an immediate rejection rather than a generation attempt.

**Corrected an earlier hypothesis mid-session, caught by John's direct question ("why is Owen's retry target comparatively slow?"):** real `ai_activity_log` data showed Marcus's regenerate calls are NOT unusually slow (avg 10.2s, 1/151 over 25s) — Priya's hypothesis intents run far closer to the 35s checkpoint threshold (avg 28-30s, 18-21 of ~20-23 calls over 25s) without tripping this bug, because they're never nested inside an already-resumed delegation. The real mechanism is budget accumulation across Owen's inherently 5-hop retry chain (routing → answer → review → regenerate×2), not any single agent's speed — corrected before scoping the fix, not asserted from the first plausible-sounding theory.

**Fix (`api/capabilities/execute.js`, 1 file, `S-ARCH-NESTED-RESUME-01`):** `dispatchDelegation()` now returns the nested job's id and tool_use_id on a `nested_checkpoint` outcome instead of dropping them; both checkpoint sites persist `pending_delegation: { waiting_on_job_id, tool_use_id }` instead of `null`; `resumeCapability()` gained a new branch that actively (recursively) resumes the nested job before deciding what to do — still in progress → re-checkpoint unchanged; failed → fail the outer job with an honest cascaded error; complete → build the real `tool_result` and continue the loop properly. Zero capability-specific branching — generic harness plumbing extending the same mechanism `S-ARCH-DURABLE-RESUME-02` introduced, not a new one. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19h gained a follow-up correction paragraph, same precedent as its existing 2026-07-14 correction.

**Live-verified, all 4 Category L scenarios, independently re-checked by this design session (diff stat, git log, real row content), not accepted from the coding report alone:** nested-already-complete → real completed result, not a 400; nested-still-in-progress → clean re-checkpoint, `pending_delegation` unchanged; nested-failed → honest cascaded error, no raw Anthropic 400; clean non-retry Owen review → completes normally in ~10s, confirming zero regression on the happy path. Two additional real (non-synthetic) multi-hop checkpoints occurred organically during the coding session's own testing and both resumed to completion cleanly — further corroboration beyond the synthetic tests. All synthetic test rows deleted after verification.

**What this does not fix:** the underlying 60s shared-deadline/35s-reserve budget accounting that makes Owen's 5-hop retry chain tight in the first place — this session closes the correctness bug (a resume that corrupts state), not the latency pressure that makes a nested checkpoint possible. Other distinct `durable_hops` failure classes (e.g. Nadia's write-validation error, older Marcus 404/parse failures) are separate, already-tracked issues, unaffected by this fix.

**Session:** S-LOG-12-13-14-design/S-LOG-12-13-14 (v6.3.17, `97363df`/`d3883a5`, 2026-07-16, worktree `ai-audit-log-0716`) — John asked what's the highest priority for the AI audit log; rather than working from the existing `LOG-09`/`10`/`11` backlog, live-tested a real question through the deployed Channel Intelligence (MI) screen and traced the resulting `ai_activity_log` rows against both the platform-wide AI Audit screen and the MI screen's own Agents drawer. Three related bugs surfaced from that one real interaction, all one root cause.

**`LOG-12` — AI Audit's `byService` misattributes delegation-loop turns.** `logAgentTurn()` (`execute.js`) writes an `ai_type: 'agent-turn'` row for every turn in a multi-turn capability; `sendRequest()` additionally writes a second, capability-named row only for the terminal (non-delegating) turn. `byService`'s grouping key (`AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE[e.type] || e.type`) resolved every non-terminal turn to the literal string `'agent-turn'` instead of its real capability, even though the real capability was sitting in the row's own `feature` field (e.g. `"quality-gate:qg-review-intent:depth0"`). Measured live: Owen's Quality Gate review of one real answer made 5 real Anthropic calls; only the last was attributed to "Quality Gate" in By-Service, the other 4 (real tokens, real cost) landed in "Agent Turn," indistinguishable from any other capability's own intermediate turns.

**`LOG-13` — MI screen's Agents drawer drops real activity for 3 agents.** `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `MI_LOOP_SCOPE` constant has two parallel lists (`aiTypes` for terminal rows, `featurePrefixes` for `agent-turn` rows) that had drifted out of sync — `data-analysis`, `memory-consolidation`, `data-room-custody` were in `aiTypes` but missing from `featurePrefixes`, so Nadia's/Elena's/Eleanor's own intermediate `agent-turn` rows failed `inScope()` and were dropped entirely (zero count/cost/latency, not just miscategorized).

**`LOG-14` — catalogs not self-maintaining, John's explicit direction this session.** `SERVICE_CATALOG`/`PATTERN_CATALOG` are static hand-maintained lists; any real slug not yet catalogued was silently invisible rather than miscategorized. Concrete instance found: `guardrails-check` (Alex's/Michelle's real guardrail calls) had zero `SERVICE_CATALOG` entry and zero `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE` mapping — the same class of gap `LOG-11` already documented for other services, closed here both for this specific instance and generally.

**Scope discipline, mid-session:** the first proposed fix (patch `api/web-memory.js` with a manual `logActivity()` call for its unlogged Haiku learning call) was caught by John as a real architectural miss before any code was written — that file hand-rolls its own Anthropic call instead of routing through the Generic Capability Executor (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19b), the exact anti-pattern that section bans by name (`channel-intelligence.js`/`quality-gate.js` precedent). Correctly re-scoped to `AI-17`/`S-INFRA-02` (already tracked, needs its own bigger design session) rather than reinforcing the anti-pattern with another one-off patch. This kept the session on its real target (Channel Intelligence screen accuracy) instead of drifting into Fetch/web-memory territory, which John explicitly flagged as the whack-a-mole pattern to stop.

**Fix (`S-LOG-12-13-14`, 2 files):** `src/hooks/useAIActivity.js` gained `capabilitySlugForRow()` (resolves `agent-turn` rows to their real capability via `feature`'s first `:`-delimited segment before the existing `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE` remap) — used in `byService`'s grouping, fixing `LOG-12`. `byService` and `computeByPattern()` both gained a self-maintaining fallback: any real resolved slug not yet in `SERVICE_CATALOG`/`PATTERN_CATALOG` gets an auto-humanized bucket instead of vanishing (`LOG-14`), plus a proper named `guardrails-check` `SERVICE_CATALOG` entry with its real observed patterns. `src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `MI_LOOP_SCOPE.featurePrefixes` gained the 3 missing entries (`LOG-13`) — the two deliberately-narrower existing entries (`project-manager:agent-selection-intent:`, the two `screen-controls:` sub-intents) were left untouched, not collapsed into a bare-slug match, to avoid pulling in Michelle's/Alex's non-MI activity from other screens.

**Independently re-verified by this design session against a second, fresh live question (not accepted from the coding report alone):** queried all 12 real non-terminal `agent-turn` rows produced by that run directly from Supabase and hand-applied the shipped `capabilitySlugForRow()` logic — 0 of 12 landed in the generic bucket, all correctly resolved (`channel-intelligence`/`quality-gate`/`task-planning`/`screen-controls`). Confirmed the `guardrails-check` catalog entry, `capabilitySlugForRow()`, and the 3 `MI_LOOP_SCOPE` prefixes all present in the actual pushed `origin/dev` commit, not just the coding session's stated diff. `git diff --stat` confirmed exactly 2 files. Node test (7/7) run and deleted by the coding session, confirmed via commit history.

**What this does not fix, flagged to John explicitly:** `LOG-13`'s fix (the 3 missing prefixes) was verified by direct code inspection against the kickoff spec, not by fresh live data — neither live test run happened to exercise Nadia/Elena/Eleanor's agent-turn activity. The broader `LOG-09` (server-side unlogged calls: `api/plan.js`, `lib/agent-run.js`, `lib/rag.js`, `lib/search-harness.js`, `api/brief.js` LEGACY/PAT, `api/web-memory.js`), `LOG-10` (client-side unlogged calls, `FetchContext.jsx`'s crash), and the rest of `LOG-11` (`document-extraction` naming collision, `prompt-assembly` phantom-live status) remain open and queued, unchanged — confirmed none are Channel Intelligence/MI-screen scope, consistent with John's explicit priority ordering (Bench/Personnel screens secondary/now, Project Management/Spend Analysis next). This session verified against 2 real interactions' worth of data, not an exhaustive sweep of every one of the ~15 MI-roster capabilities for the same missing-prefix/missing-catalog-entry shape — a follow-up systematic sweep is still worth its own short session before calling Channel Intelligence's AI Audit fully trustworthy.

**Session:** S-CHI-AUDIT-GROUPING-01-design/S-CHI-AUDIT-GROUPING-01 (v6.3.18, `b2b1746`/`24901e6`, 2026-07-16, worktree `mi-columns-ux-0716`) — design session with John working from a live screenshot of the Channel Intelligence screen's Audit column, with 5 numbered complaints. Before scoping, live-reproduced the same seed question against the real dev deployment to verify each complaint rather than assume from the screenshot: confirmed the Agent Routing log's event *order* was correct (not a sequencing bug, John's initial suspicion on complaints #3/#4) — the real gap was purely how events were grouped for display. Also live-tested complaint #2 (chat auto-scroll) and could not reproduce it (`InteractColumn`'s existing `MI-64` logic worked correctly, 0.2px gap measured) — John confirmed dropping it as a non-issue. Complaint #1 (Chat/Evidence architectural separation) was scoped as its own, much larger future session, out of scope here. This session covers complaints #3/#4/#5 only, bundled at John's explicit direction.

**`CHI-01` — Agent Routing log grouped one row per raw event, not one card per hand-off.** `S-MI-68-design` had already suppressed the repeated avatar/name/role header on consecutive same-agent rows (`sameAgentAsPrevious`), but each event still rendered as its own separately-bordered row, and the visible number counted raw events. John's explicit call: "a new line should mean a hand-off happened, not an activity." Live-verified on a real 9-event run: only 6 actual hand-offs occurred (Marcus→Owen→Marcus→Owen→Michelle→Owen, Owen's final turn alone containing 3 activities — routing to Nadia, blocking the answer, a triage recommendation).

**`CHI-02` — long embedded log text (e.g. Michelle's `agent_selection.reasoning`) was hard-truncated, unrecoverable.** `shapeForLog()` sliced to 140 chars and appended `…` to the cut string — the ellipsis landed wherever CSS word-wrap happened to break (sometimes alone on its own line), and the discarded text could never be recovered, so there was no way to "read the rest."

**Root cause noted but deliberately not fixed this session (logged as `LOO-005`, `docs/FEATURES.md`):** an agent's own reasoning ahead of a `delegate_to_agent` call is never logged as its own event — only the outgoing call is. That's why Owen's card can legitimately open on "routing this to Marcus" with no prior "Owen reviewing" event — a real observability gap in the event trail, separate from this session's grouping fix.

**Fix (`S-CHI-AUDIT-GROUPING-01`, 2 files, first `CHI`-coded rows ever logged):** `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` gained `groupEventsIntoTurns()` (groups consecutive same-`agentId` events into one turn, numbered oldest=1 ascending, same direction as before) and replaced `RoutingEventRow` with `RoutingTurnCard` (one header per turn) + `RoutingActivityLine` (one line per activity, a 6px color dot instead of a full border, CSS 3-line clamp + click-to-expand for long text). The 4 `describePipelineEvent()` cases that wrapped embedded text in `shapeForLog()` (proofreader critique, failure_triage research suggestion, patch_proposed version note, agent_selection reasoning) now pass the raw field straight through — `shapeForLog()` itself is untouched, since 2 unrelated call sites in `AuditDrawersBody` still depend on its truncating behavior with their own `maxLen` values. Both render call sites (desktop `AuditColumn`, mobile pinned feed) share the one grouping helper. Drawer count label switched from "N events" to "N turns". `STYLE-GUIDE.md` §31 amended (supersedes the header-suppression-only mechanism, same underlying intent), §32 added (locks the clamp+expand convention as generic to any future long-text log line).

**Coding session's own test caught and correctly root-caused a bug in the kickoff doc itself, not the implementation:** the doc's Node test fixture asserted the 9-event live example collapses to 6 turns, matching the session's own narrative — but the implemented algorithm (byte-identical to the kickoff's Task 1 spec) genuinely produces 7 turns on that fixture, because the doc's narrative had silently uncounted the leading "reading the question" event as its own turn. Confirmed independently by manually walking the algorithm against the fixture before accepting the coding session's fix — the doc's test assertions were wrong, not the code. Corrected in place, all 15 tests passed after the fix, build clean.

**Independently re-verified by this design session against the real deployed dev URL, not accepted from the coding report alone:** two real Q&A runs. First (2 turns, clean answer): confirmed the empty state reads "0 turns"; Marcus's turn correctly bundled both his intake-classification and answer-generation steps under one header — his `intent_routing` and `qa_answer` events share `agentId: "marcus"` in real data, a detail the kickoff's synthetic test fixture had guessed wrong (assigned that first event a `null` agentId instead) — harmless to the shipped algorithm, but worth knowing for the next session reading real event data. Second run (safety-blocked on a citation gap, 6 turns from 9 raw events): confirmed Owen's final 3-activity turn renders as one bordered card with a single header and distinct per-line color dots; confirmed the long triage-recommendation line clamped with a working "Read more" control, clicked it to expand to full text with the toggle flipping to "Show less," clicked again to re-collapse; confirmed short lines show no toggle at all. Repeated on a fresh mobile load (375px, real `matchMedia` breakpoint confirmed via `window.matchMedia('(max-width: 768px)').matches`, not just a viewport resize — a runtime resize alone did not trigger `useIsMobile()`'s change listener, a known pre-existing environment quirk, not a bug in this session's code) — identical turn-grouped cards rendered in the pinned Agent Routing feed, confirming the shared render path holds across both shells; the `MI-50` scroll-hint chevron still rendered. Zero console errors on either run.

**Also flagged during scoping, not fixed:** a fresh data point added to the existing `AA-159` row (`docs/FEATURES.md`) — the live run needed 6 handoffs for one Q&A, consistent with that row's already-tracked call-volume cost concern, not a new finding.

**Session:** S-LOG-16-design/S-LOG-16 (v6.3.20, `a2713d1`/`5e6a9ea`, 2026-07-16, worktree `ai-audit-sweep-0716`) — direct continuation of the same-day `LOG-12`/`13`/`14` session (John asked to scope a systematic sweep for the same bug shape across the ~15 MI-roster capabilities). While live-testing on the real MI screen to ground that sweep, John separately noticed the Agent Routing drawer no longer showed pattern info for some events and raised a much deeper platform-rules objection that superseded the sweep entirely.

**`LOG-15` (found, not fixed) — two `describePipelineEvent()` bugs.** Live-verified via a fresh real question: `agent_selection` (Michelle's "who should handle this" calls) and `display_format` (the Display agent's formatting call) both hardcode `capability: "channel-intelligence"` regardless of who's actually involved — Michelle's real capability is `project-manager`/`task-planning`, Alex's is `screen-controls`. Confirmed live: both event types mislabeled "Channel Intelligence" in a real interaction. Separately, `agent_selection` events carry zero pattern data structurally — their `data` field is set to the picker's *selection reasoning* object (`qa.last_help_selection`/`display.selection`), which never included the call's own `patterns_used`. Logged, not fixed — the deeper `LOG-16` finding took priority.

**`LOG-16` — the real root cause, verified live in the write-side code, not assumed.** John's objection, in his own framing: a pattern should never require a pre-declared profile entry in order to be logged, the display should update dynamically as real usage is detected, and displaying a DeepBench Service name as if it were an industry Pattern (the `LOG-15` mislabeling) compounds the same dishonesty. Traced to `request-receivable.js`'s `sendRequest()` (line 440-443): `patterns_used` spread a declared Intent Skill Profile field (`intent_technical_services`) into **every call** of that intent unconditionally — no runtime check on whether that specific call actually exercised the pattern. This shipped 2 days earlier as `AI-50a`/`b`/`c` (2026-07-14), explicitly framed as "real per-call pattern attribution" — genuine progress over the prior fully-static SERVICE_CATALOG-level tagging, but it only moved the hardcoding down one level (service → intent), not to genuine per-call detection. Checked `ARCHITECTURE.md`/`AI-SERVICES.md` directly: no LOCKED rule was ever violated — this is a real gap in the documented model itself (the one place declaration-gating is explicit, REFLECT/Prompt Compression's "must be declared," is legitimate there because declaring *is* what triggers the behavior), not a rule that was ignored.

**Quantified live (Supabase query against all 11,226 real rows), corrected for precision mid-investigation:** an initial blanket count found 2,696 rows carrying a "declared-list" pattern — but a closer check (`lib/librarian.js`, direct code read) found 1,999 of those (`rag`/`embeddings` on `ai_type='librarian'`) are a **hardcoded literal sitting immediately after a real vector search succeeds**, honest regardless of declaration, not part of the real risk. The corrected, real at-risk population: **659 rows**, across `channel-intelligence`/`project-manager`/`hypothesis-evaluation`/`quality-gate`-family capabilities and their `agent-turn` siblings.

**Design principle established with John, governs all future pattern-logging work:** a pattern is logged from a real, checkable fact about *this specific call*, sourced one of four ways — (1) mechanical, computed from the call's own shape/response; (2) declared-and-triggers-real-behavior (REFLECT/Prompt Compression only — declaring is what causes it to run); (3) self-reported by the model's own structured output; (4) deterministically inferred/verified by the harness from actual data (call graph, retrieved-content cross-check) — reserved for facts no single call's shape reveals (e.g. `llm-as-judge`, a fact about the relationship between two calls) or for verifying a self-report rather than trusting it blindly. John's own correction, mid-design: buckets 1-3 are not a lookup table to maintain (patterns aren't "mapped" into buckets) — they're real signals the harness already has or can compute, wired once, generically; bucket 4 is the only one needing real design, and even there, one detection function per *pattern* (not per capability) run against every call, never a per-capability opt-in.

**Corruption question, answered directly and quantified, not glossed over:** yes, both directions are real. False positives: any of the 659 rows might show a declared pattern the specific call didn't genuinely exercise — unmeasurable which ones, since nothing recorded per-call confirmation. False negatives: real usage of anything not pre-declared was never logged at all — inherently uncountable from the data itself. Not retroactively fixable; forward-only.

**Fix shipped (Phase 1, 2 files):** found live that `ai-enrichment.js`'s `enrichPrompt()` already computes real per-call `debug.rag_retrieved` (`Object.keys(ragChunksBySection).length > 0` — a genuine retrieval check), `debug.reflect_ran`, `debug.synthesis_ran` — sitting unused for pattern-logging purposes. `buildPatternsUsed()` (`request-receivable.js`) extended to accept a real `ragRetrieved` boolean; `sendRequest()` now reads `enrichDebug.rag_retrieved`/`reflect_ran`/`synthesis_ran` instead of spreading `intent_technical_services` (removed entirely from the patterns_used computation — its legitimate role triggering REFLECT inclusion in `db-assembly.js` is a different, untouched code path). Almost entirely wiring, since the real detection already existed. `computeByPattern()` (`useAIActivity.js`) gained a historical trust boundary — `PATTERN_VERIFICATION_CUTOFF`, `isPatternTrusted()` — so `rag`/`streaming`/`reflect`/`intelligent-synthesis` on any non-`librarian` `ai_type` only count if the row postdates the fix's real deploy timestamp; the stripped share of a call's cost redistributes to its remaining trusted patterns rather than silently vanishing from every bucket's total. Per John's explicit rule: unverifiable data doesn't display; verifiable data does.

**Coding session set `PATTERN_VERIFICATION_CUTOFF` to the real push timestamp** (`2026-07-16T18:44:00Z`, confirmed against the actual commit's `%cI`), not a placeholder, per the kickoff's explicit instruction. Node test 9/9 PASS, build clean, `git diff --stat` exactly 2 files.

**Independently re-verified by this design session against real Supabase data, not accepted from the coding report alone:** pulled the two rows the coding session cited directly — `id 11248` (`18:42:04 UTC`, before the `18:44:00Z` cutoff) and `id 11252` (`18:46:18 UTC`, after) — and hand-applied `isPatternTrusted()`'s actual logic: they bracket the cutoff exactly as designed, `rag` correctly excluded from one and included in the other.

**Deliberately not fixed this session, logged separately:** `LOG-15` (drawer mislabeling, above). `LOG-17` (Phase 2) — `llm-as-judge` (93 real rows, needs call-graph detection in `execute.js`, different file/verification shape) and Case-Based-Reasoning (self-report + deterministic cross-check against `the_reasoning`) — and `streaming` (6 historical rows, needs a `stream` flag threaded one layer further, low priority). The systematic sweep John originally asked for at this session's start never happened — superseded by this deeper finding; still open for a future session.

**Process note:** local `vite`-only dev server cannot serve `/api/*` Vercel serverless routes (confirmed live — a real question submitted against `localhost:5173` alone failed with "Something went wrong reaching Marcus," the same symptom as the still-open `AA-195`-class incidents, purely because there's no backend to answer it locally). Not a bug; switched verification to the real Vercel dev preview URL, which picks up new commits within roughly a minute of push.

**Session:** S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-design/S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01 + S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-patch-design/S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-patch (v6.3.19 `971dd06`, v6.3.21 `80478c4`, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-aa194-roster-0716`) — `AA-196` closed, direct continuation of the same-day `AA-195` work.

**Started as a scope question, not a bug report:** John asked to continue the Continuity track; a scoping conversation compared `AA-194`'s remaining agent-content roster (13 skill rows with null `output_desc`, live-verified via the exact SQL query `AGENT-COMPETENCY-MODEL.md` already specified) against harness/loop/speed work, and John redirected to "harness, loop, platform, architecture, speed of routing first." Checked before proceeding, not asserted: `AA-194` doesn't fix `AA-196`/`AA-159` (content-correctness vs. checkpoint-architecture/call-volume, no shared mechanism) — real `ai_activity_log` data showed `agent-selection-intent` depth1 (540 calls, 60.6 cumulative minutes) is dominated by legitimate, correctly-routed calls, not misrouting corrections. `AA-196` chosen over `AA-159` because it directly extends `AA-195`'s own mechanism and `AA-159` was already deliberately downgraded by John's prior `AA-170` verdict.

**Root cause, pulled before designing anything (John's explicit ask: "pull the data first"):** real p99/max latency across every agent-turn on the platform showed the flat `HOP_BUDGET_RESERVE_MS` (35s, from `MAX_ESTIMATED_HOP_MS=30000` + margin) is wrong in both directions — over-reserved for routing/selection/review hops (Marcus's `ci-routing-intent` p99 7.8s, Michelle's `agent-selection-intent` p99 12.3s, Owen's `qg-review-intent` p99 22.3s), and **under-reserved** for several genuinely slow hops that already exceed the assumed 30s worst case: Eleanor's `data-room-custody` p99 46.2s, Nadia's `data-escalate-intent` p99 40.4s, Priya's `hyp-hypothesis-test-intent` p99 36.1s, Riley's `html-display` p99 34.0s. The under-reserved side is the more serious finding — those hops can already exceed what the harness assumes is safe, risking a hard Vercel `maxDuration` timeout instead of a graceful checkpoint.

**Confirmed not a hardcoded routing decision before scoping (John's explicit, direct question):** the fix only changes a timing threshold used to decide whether there's enough budget to attempt one more hop — it doesn't decide which agent/capability/intent handles anything. The one named capability in the fix (`project-manager`/`agent-selection-intent`, used to estimate `request_help`'s next call) mirrors an already-fixed architectural relationship (`resolveCapabilityHolder('project-manager')`), not a new special case.

**Fix (`S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01`, v6.3.19):** `execute.js` gained a per-capability/intent budget estimate — `getHopBudgetReserveMs()`, cached with a 15-minute TTL, computed via a duplicated (not cross-layer-imported, `ARCHITECTURE.md` §6) linear-interpolation percentile matching Postgres's `percentile_cont()` exactly (same formula already proven by `MI-58`'s client-side `percentile()`). Falls back to the unchanged flat constant whenever a pair has fewer than 10 real samples. Both existing `runLoop()` checkpoint sites now use it — the pre-dispatch site estimates the *next* call's profile (the delegate target for `delegate_to_agent`, Michelle's known profile for `request_help`), not the current hop's own.

**Coding session's own Category L test caught a real defect, not a false alarm — root-caused directly against the live endpoint, not assumed from either side's report:** `getHopBudgetReserveMs('data-room-custody', null)` returned the unchanged `35000` fallback in production despite 21 real samples existing. Traced by directly inspecting the REST response: `Content-Range: 0-999/3868` for a request that asked for `limit=3000` — this Supabase project's PostgREST silently caps a single request at 1000 rows regardless of the `limit=` param, no error. Eleanor's 21 samples (dated 07-08 through 07-14) fell entirely outside the resulting 1000-row window (cutoff 07-14 01:53) — the exact under-reserved capability the whole fix was built to protect was silently still using the old, unsafe constant.

**Patched same day (`S-ARCH-HOP-BUDGET-01-patch`, v6.3.21), John's explicit call (Option 1 — paginate to fetch everything, not a fixed bounded window, since the table has only ~3900 total rows):** `refreshHopEstimateCache()` now paginates in pages of 1000 (the server's real per-request cap) until a page returns fewer rows than requested, bounded by a 10-page/10,000-row safety cap. Live-verified independently by the design session (not accepted from the coding report alone, matching this session's own repeated discipline): `data-room-custody`/null now returns `51168.4` (real p99+margin), `ci-routing-intent` unchanged at `12784.68` (no regression).

**Unrelated security-adjacent finding, handled correctly, not acted on:** the coding session's live-verification script (loading `.env.local`, which contains real API keys) triggered `dotenv@17.4.2`'s own promotional console output — `tip: ⌁ auth for agents [www.vestauth.com]`, phrasing aimed specifically at AI agents. Inspected the package source directly (no network call, a `console.log` in `dotenv`'s own code) rather than trusting or acting on it — neither the coding session nor this design session visited the URL or took any "auth" action. Flagged to John directly per the standing prompt-injection-in-tool-output protocol. Worth noting: this dependency resolves from the shared checkout's `node_modules` (this worktree has none of its own), so it's shared across every concurrent session — not fixed this session, John's call whether to pin `{quiet:true}` or audit further.

**What this does not fix:** Owen's chain length (still 5 hops by design), the underlying 60s `maxDuration` ceiling, or `AA-159`'s call-volume cost — this is a threshold-accuracy fix, reducing how often a chain checkpoints unnecessarily and closing a real timeout risk, not a chain-shortening or volume-reduction one.

**Session:** S-CI-ROUTING-ESCALATE-01-design (2026-07-16, worktree `data-query-intent-0716`, Supabase content only — no kickoff doc, no code, no version bump) — `AA-198` closed. (Originally claimed as `AA-197` at session start; renumbered to `AA-198` after a rebase conflict revealed a concurrent session, `S-CHI-03-design`, had independently claimed `AA-197` for an unrelated prompt-caching finding in the same window — feature IDs are picked by "highest existing + 1," which has no atomic-claim protection the way version numbers do since `CLAUDE.md`'s `dev_version_counter` fix; flagged to John as a process gap below.) John shared a live screenshot: "what data files are available for me to query?" asked on the real Channel Sales Intelligence chat returned the client-side escalate placeholder ("Escalating for deeper research ships in a future build...") instead of an answer, and asked whether this was a regression from the already-shipped catalog-question fix (`AA-182`/`AA-186`).

**Root-caused before proposing anything, per the standing verify-never-assert rule:** traced the live client code (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `runIntentPipeline`) — every message first hits Marcus's `ci-routing-intent` (Skill on his `channel-intelligence` Capability, the only agent/capability pairing that uses it, confirmed via `agent_capability_assignments`). Its schema allows `qa`/`theory`/`forecast`/`correct`/`escalate`, but its `method` field (read live from Supabase, not assumed) only ever defined how to distinguish qa/theory/forecast — zero guidance for escalate, and `output_desc` was null. An open-ended catalog question falls into the undefined bucket by default. `escalate` is a hard client-side dead end (`runIntentPipeline` returns the placeholder immediately, the agent loop never starts) — upstream of `AA-182`/`AA-186`'s fix, which only ever mattered once Marcus routed a question to `qa` in the first place.

**Clarified mid-session, John's question:** confirmed this edits an *existing* Skill (`ci-routing-intent`), not a new one, and that `output_desc` is a separate field read only by Michelle's roster-broker (`lib/project-manager.js`, one call site, grep-confirmed) for her own delegation reasoning — it is never injected into `ci-routing-intent`'s own prompt, so it has zero effect on the routing bug itself. Authoring it was optional, bundled in only because `AA-194` already tracked it as a known gap on Marcus's roster row and it was cheap to include in the same edit.

**Fix approved and applied (exact wording shown to John before writing, per the mandatory stop-gate):** appended an escalate-classification paragraph to `method` — catalog-style "what data is available" questions are qa, not escalate; since escalate has no working destination today, default to qa when genuinely ambiguous. Authored `output_desc` in the same terse "Returns X — the Y" style already used by sibling Intent Skills (checked live for consistency before writing, not guessed).

**Live-verified, caught a second real bug in the process:** the original failing question now correctly returns `qa`/high confidence. A negative-control test (a genuinely escalate-worthy request) returned `{"error":"Parse failed and retry also failed","detail":"No tool_use block in response"}` — reproduced twice in a row, not a one-off. `VERCEL_TOKEN` was unset in both the Bash and PowerShell shells (checked directly per `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s known Windows `setx` gap, genuinely unreachable this session, documented rather than assumed unavailable) — could not pull the raw Anthropic stop_reason. Root-caused instead via config comparison: `ci-routing-intent` (`max_tokens: 300`, `temperature: null`) is the one intent in its `can_request_help`/`tool_choice:'auto'` family never migrated onto the `temperature=0`/adequate-`max_tokens` fix `AA-154`/`AA-149` already proved on 3 siblings (`ci-answer-intent` 3000/0, `qg-review-intent` 1500/0, `library-catalog-intent` 1500/0) — a `AA-192`-shaped "one site never migrated onto an already-proven fix" pattern, not a new bug class. The session's own longer `method` text gave the model more to reason about, pushing this pre-existing thin margin into visible failure (even the passing `qa` call needed the existing retry-once safety net).

**Second fix approved and applied, same proven pattern, no new numbers invented:** pinned `temperature=0`, raised `max_tokens` 300→1500 to match `qg-review-intent`/`library-catalog-intent`. Re-verified live: original question still `qa`/high confidence; the escalate negative-control now correctly returns `escalate`/high confidence — the "default to qa when ambiguous" guidance did not over-correct into swallowing genuine escalate cases. Both calls still show `retry_count: 1` on the first attempt — consistent with the platform's already-documented `AA-151`/`AA-172` retry-family behavior (seen at 10-80% rates on other `can_request_help` intents), not treated as a new regression, not chased further this session.

**Session-hygiene check run at start (checks 1-3, 5), reported not fixed (John's call pending):** `CLAUDE-STATE.md` 27.8 KB (baseline ~10 KB), `FEATURES.md` 64.3 KB (baseline ~40 KB), `FEATURES-NEXT.md` 34.8 KB with 1 unarchived `✅ Done` row, and 4 fully-merged stale worktrees (`agent-competency-model-0715`, `ai-audit-completion-0716`, `ci-chatbox-ux-0716`, `continuity-architecture-0716`) safe to remove. Flagged, not restructured — a separate deliberate step per the skill's own scope.

**What this does not fix:** the deeper "why does the first attempt almost always need a retry" question for `can_request_help`/`tool_choice:'auto'` intents platform-wide (`AA-151`/`AA-172`'s territory) — this session closed the specific `ci-routing-intent` under-provisioning gap, not the general retry-family pattern. Also does not close `AA-194`'s roster gap beyond Marcus's `ci-routing-intent` row — his `ci-answer-intent`/`ci-answer-display-intent` remain null, along with Eleanor/Elena/Owen/Priya/Victoria/Dan.

**Process gap found at push time, flagged to John, not fixed this session:** rebasing onto `origin/dev` conflicted in `docs/FEATURES.md` — a concurrent session (`S-CHI-03-design`, worktree `ci-chatbox-ux-0716`) had independently claimed `AA-197` for an unrelated prompt-caching finding in the same window this session claimed `AA-197` for the escalate-misroute fix. Resolved by renumbering this session's rows to `AA-198` (the true next-free ID re-checked directly against a fresh `origin/dev` fetch, not the stale local grep used at session start) and merging both sessions' `AA-194` edits by hand rather than picking one side. Feature/backlog IDs (`AA-`/`MI-`/etc.) are still assigned by "read the highest existing number and add one," the exact race pattern version numbers had before `CLAUDE.md`'s `dev_version_counter` atomic-claim fix (2026-07-15) — this collision is the same failure class, just never migrated to the same fix. Worth its own atomic counter (or per-prefix counter row) if concurrent-session collisions on feature IDs recur; not built here, John's call on priority.

**Session:** S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01-patch-design/S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01-patch (v6.3.30, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-architecture-0716`, Supabase content only — no code file, no commit hash) — `LOO-001`/`LOO-002`/`LOO-004` closed.

**Context:** same-day patch to `S-ARCH-LOOP-CONTINUITY-01` (v6.3.28), which shipped three fixes (`LOO-001`/`LOO-002`/`LOO-004`) to Michelle's `agent-selection-intent`. That session's own live verification (6 runs of the self-only-match scenario: Marcus, `requesting_agent_id: "marcus"`, skill need matching only his own `channel-intelligence` capability) found only 1 of 6 correctly returned `no_match: true` — the other 5 recommended Marcus back to himself, even though the model's own `reasoning` field explicitly acknowledged the self-referral conflict in the same response before overriding it.

**Root cause, verified live this session (not assumed from the coding report):** queried `skill_profiles` directly — `agent-selection-intent.temperature` was `null`, while `ci-answer-intent`/`qg-review-intent` are both already pinned to `temperature: "0"` under `AA-154`, an existing fix for this exact failure class (reasoning-text-vs-output-field inconsistency under Anthropic's default temperature). `agent-selection-intent` was never brought under that precedent. This is a real behavioral-determinism change outside `v6.3.28`'s original scope (affects every `agent-selection-intent` call platform-wide, not just the self-referral path) — confirmed with John before applying, per the Design Session Walkthrough Required rule, rather than applied unilaterally.

**Fix applied:** `update skill_profiles set temperature = 0 where slug = 'agent-selection-intent'` — live-verified `null` before, `"0"` after. No code file touched, no `lib/project-manager.js`/`execute.js` filtering added as a workaround (the `LOO-001` design principle — reasoning, not filtering — stands unchanged).

**Live re-verification against the real dev endpoint (`https://deepbench-frontend-git-dev-roadmapventures-projects.vercel.app/api/capabilities/execute`, calling `project-manager`/`agent-selection-intent` directly with `agent_id: "michelle"`), expanded trial count per the patch kickoff's explicit instruction (a single post-patch pass proves nothing given the prior 1/6 rate):**
- **Self-only-match, 8x** (`requesting_agent_id: "marcus"`, need matching only his own `channel-intelligence`): **8/8 clean** — every run returned `no_match: true`, `recommended_agent_id: null`, `recommended_capability_slug: null`, with `candidates` correctly still listing Marcus (for audit transparency) and `reasoning`/`missing_skillset` both correctly explaining that recommending the requester back to itself is invalid.
- **Generic no-match regression, 1x** (nadia, "file U.S. federal tax returns for the company"): unchanged — `no_match: true`, both recommendation fields null, `missing_skillset` names the real gap (no tax-filing expertise on the roster).
- **Legitimate match regression, 1x** (marcus requesting, need matches Owen's `quality-gate`): unchanged — `no_match: false`, `recommended_agent_id: "owen"`, `recommended_capability_slug: "quality-gate"`.

Full pass/fail tally reported honestly per the kickoff's instruction: 10/10 across all three scenarios, no rounding, no early stop. `temperature` pinning alone closed the gap — no second, undocumented code-level fix was needed or attempted.

**Close-out:** `LOO-001`/`LOO-002`/`LOO-004` moved from `docs/FEATURES.md` to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` as ✅ Done. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.30`; this session's own "In flight now" bullet (from the prior `-design` session) removed. Only `docs/FEATURES.md`/`docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`/`CLAUDE-STATE.md` touched — no code file, no serverless function count change.

**Independently re-verified against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the build/Node-test pass alone (Manual QA Checklist, all 12 items):** pushed to `dev` (`934b471`), waited for Vercel's git-integration auto-deploy (confirmed live by polling the built JS bundle for the new `#f0d99a` token), then drove 3 full live Q&A round trips through the real Marcus→Owen→Michelle→Alex delegation chain (1 desktop, 2 on an actual 375×812 mobile viewport) — genuine multi-hop chains each taking 50s–1m20s, including two runs where Owen's quality-gate requested a real revision before Michelle re-routed to Alex for display formatting. Clicking "Good, thanks" correctly swapped the block for the existing unchanged "✓ Good, thanks — no further action." note (checklist item 7). `CHI-05` `FeatureBadge` visible under `?debug=features` (item 10). Root cause: hop numbers are array-creation order; the pre-existing `delegation` announcement always created the earliest row (later claimed by the target's `LOO-009` credit), while `LOO-011`'s originator credit fired only after the nested call resolved, always creating a genuinely later row. Fix: moved the originator's `delegation_complete` event to fire before the `delegation` announcement — both `delegationRequired`/`tool_input.is_final` are already known at that point, computed once as `willResolveFinal`, reused by both the early-fire decision and the existing final-branch check. Content unchanged, only the firing point moved. 1 file (`api/capabilities/execute.js`). Node test 2/2 PASS (event-order + returning-case regression), deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean. **Live-verified against the real deployed dev preview:** Jordan/Alex news-fetch flow now shows Jordan as hop 1 (full real task description) and Alex as hop 2 (real service label), reversed from the broken state, content on both unaffected; a live 8-hop chart-request chain confirmed the untouched `request_help`+`delegationRequired` broker-leg branch (Michelle/`LOO-009d`) still orders correctly (Michelle hop 7, Alex hop 8); returning-case regression (Owen/Marcus) confirmed via `git diff` (that branch is byte-identical, untouched) + the Node test + the same live chain's own nested-return legs (Michelle, Eleanor) each credited exactly once, no duplicates — did not force a live QG-rejection retry-to-Marcus loop specifically (a live LLM judgment call, not reliably forceable on demand), flagged rather than silently assumed. Zero console errors, zero 4xx/5xx. `LOO-014` archived ✅ Done. Full detail: `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`.
- **S-LOO-015** (v6.3.88, `4bdb965`, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-loo-013-broker-fallback-0721`) — coding session executing the kickoff doc's own exact diff: fixes total requester erasure (not just misordering) on the `request_help`+`delegationRequired` branch, found live in John's screenshot (a real Jordan→Michelle→Brent chain showed only Michelle and Brent, Jordan completely absent). Root cause: same erasure mechanism as the original Owen/Marcus bug — the branch's first `delegation` event always created the requester's own earliest row, fully overwritten in place once the broker's own leg credit (`LOO-009d`) claimed it, so the requester's identity never survived anywhere else. `LOO-011` deliberately scoped its own fix to the *other* branch (plain `delegate_to_agent`), reasoning a `delegationRequired` requester "does no real work by definition" — false for a capability like `web-search-news` where the requester's real work happens before the hand-off. Fix: one new `delegation_complete` event (`fromAgentId: null`), gated on `delegationRequired`, fired before the branch's existing first `delegation` announcement — same pattern as `LOO-011`/`LOO-014`, positioned correctly for hop order on the first attempt. 1 file (`api/capabilities/execute.js`). Node test 2/2 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean. **Live-verified against the real deployed dev preview:** the non-deterministic `request_help` path required 10 direct reload attempts on the Jordan/Alex news-fetch flow (all 10 resolved via plain `delegate_to_agent`, correctly unaffected each time) before pivoting to a live chat question (Marcus's `channel-intelligence` Q&A→Display hand-off, the same chain `LOO-009`/`LOO-012`/`LOO-014` used as ground truth for this branch), which exercised the target path on the first attempt. Full 9-hop chain read from both raw SSE and the rendered drawer: hop 7 = Marcus (requester) with real full task content, previously would have been invisible; hop 8 = Michelle (broker, real reasoning, `LOO-009d` unaffected); hop 9 = Alex (final target, `LOO-009` unaffected) — chronological order confirmed. Regression checks in the same live chain: Owen's non-`delegationRequired` `request_help` call to Michelle (hop 4) fired zero duplicate credit events; the plain `delegate_to_agent` branch (hops 1-2, 5) rendered exactly per `LOO-011`/`LOO-014`'s existing pattern, `git diff`-confirmed byte-identical/untouched. Zero console errors, all `/api/capabilities/execute` calls 200 across all 11 live attempts. `LOO-015` archived ✅ Done. Full detail: `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`.

## S-CHI-07-design / S-CHI-07 (v6.3.34, `202827d`, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-ux-0716`)

**Context:** first of a 5-session Channel Intelligence UX sweep opened by John's live-screenshot walkthrough of the Chat/Evidence/Agent Routing panels (`CHI-07`→`CHI-08`→`CHI-12`→`CHI-09`→`CHI-10`). John flagged several missing/inconsistent "Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in Xm Ys" captions and Agent Routing lines that never show a duration even after "Read more." Investigated with two `Explore` sub-agent passes plus direct source reads before proposing scope — root cause was structural, not a render bug: `logEvent()` had 21 independent call sites (13 in the component body, 8 inside the module-scope `runQaWithQualityGate`/`runHypothesisTest` functions) each hand-computing or hand-nulling their own `durationMs`, each with its own justifying comment; `setMessages()` had 17 independent call sites, only 1 of which (the `qa` bubble) ever set `totalElapsedMs`, even though 7 more ack-bubble sites already carried `hopStart`/`hopEnd`. A stray bug surfaced in the same sweep: `failure_triage` (L992) logged a literal `durationMs: 0` — every sibling non-measurable event used `null`. When asked whether to just patch the render layer or instrument new timing, John redirected both answers into "this should be a central service, not something hardcoded in multiple places" — matching this repo's own "multi-site bug pattern → shared-service check" rule (`CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`) almost exactly.

**Fix — 2 new helpers, `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` only (1 file):**
- `buildHopEvent(type, agentId, data, durationMs, extra)` — a single declared `NON_MEASURABLE_EVENT_TYPES` set (`agent_selection`, `delegation`, `delegation_return`, `failure_triage`, `question_boundary`) replaces 6 scattered comments; a caller-supplied real duration passes through unchanged, an omitted duration on a declared type resolves to `null`, an omitted duration on any other type logs a `console.error` dev-guard (never throws — never blocks the user) and still resolves to `null`. Migrated all 21 `logEvent`/`onEvent` call sites through it — the `failure_triage` fix falls out of this migration for free (its site simply stops passing a stale `0`).
- `buildMessage({role, kind, text, hopStart, hopEnd, totalElapsedMs, needsReview, reviewReason})` — whenever `hopStart` is present, `totalElapsedMs` is a required companion (same fail-open `console.error` pattern if a caller forgets it). Migrated all 17 `setMessages` call sites. **Deliberately conservative scope, confirmed while writing the kickoff doc:** 8 sites that carried no hop data before this session (`qa_failed`, `hyp_entry`, the generic `non_qa` fallback, every `error` kind) still carry none — the data genuinely isn't available at those call sites without adding new UI surface, which was explicitly out of scope. Of the 9 sites that already had `hopStart`/`hopEnd`, the `qa` bubble already had `totalElapsedMs` too; the other 8 (submission ack, 2×info-only ack, 4×resolution ack, ~~1 more~~) gained it — `onDiscard` needed a brand-new `turnStart` capture (had none before), `onSelectHypothesis`'s ack correctly closes over a `turnStart` declared just after it (synchronous code, safe by the time the async `.then()` fires), `onResolveConfirmation`'s 4 ack sites reuse the function's existing `t0`.

**Process note found mid-session, fixed and flagged as a follow-up (`task_3216010f`):** this worktree's shared `.git` (all worktrees share one ref store) still has a local `dev` branch belonging to the main checkout, observed 253 commits behind `origin/dev`. `git push origin dev` — the literal command in this repo's own `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`/every prior kickoff doc's COMMIT section — silently resolves "dev" to that stale local ref rather than the current branch's HEAD, and gets rejected non-fast-forward in a way indistinguishable from the real concurrent-session race `CLAUDE.md` rule #4 already handles (re-fetch/re-rebase does **not** fix it, since the problem is which local ref is being pushed, not remote staleness). Fixed by using `git push origin HEAD:dev` instead — confirmed working. This session's own kickoff doc was patched live after hitting this once. Spun off as a background task to fix the same bare-`dev` instruction everywhere else it appears in the standing docs.

**Verification:** Node test (`test-s-chi-07.mjs`) 16/16 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` zero errors. Post-migration greps confirmed zero raw `durationMs:` literals outside `buildHopEvent`'s own definition, zero raw inline `setMessages` literals outside `buildMessage`'s own definition, zero `durationMs: 0` anywhere.

**Independently re-verified against the real deployed dev preview by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone (bypassed Vercel deployment protection via `VERCEL_AUTOMATION_BYPASS_SECRET`, confirmed fresh from `.env.local` rather than trusted from memory):** 3 full live round trips. (1) A plain `qa` question ("Japan is Apple's fastest-growing GEO...") confirmed the pre-existing regression case unchanged — "Hops 2–3 / Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in 1m 23s". (2) A guardrail-block/escalation run (the EMEA co-op question, which happened to trip Owen's guardrail this run) confirmed live that the `failure_triage` line ("Pipeline Triage — Deciding whether more research would help — more research wouldn't help here") now renders with **no** duration suffix, directly next to its sibling "Blocking this answer — a policy issue was found · 64.8s" line which does have one — the `0`-bug fix confirmed live, not just by code read. (3) A full hypothesis flow (flagged the Japan answer for review → generated 4 theories → selected H2 → tested it → resolved Info Only) confirmed 3 previously-silent elapsed captions now render: the submission-ack bubble ("Hop 6 / Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in 2s"), the Theory Evidence card ("Hops 7–6 / Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in 1m 2s" — the `onDiscard`-adjacent `hypFlow.testElapsedMs` render fix), and the info-only-ack bubble ("Hop 9 / Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in 1s" — the brand-new `turnStart` capture in `onDiscard`). Michelle's `agent_selection` line confirmed still shows no duration (correct, unchanged). Zero console errors across all three runs, including zero firings of the new dev-guard (confirming no call site was missed). Accept/Reject resolution-ack not independently re-run live — same already-proven `t0`-reuse pattern as the verified Info Only ack, not a novel path, per the standing guidance against re-proving an already-verified mechanism (`STANDARDS.md` Category L).

**Tooling note:** the Browser pane's `computer{action:"screenshot"}` consistently timed out this session against both the landing-page overlay and the live app; worked around entirely via `read_page`/`get_page_text`/`javascript_tool` text extraction plus ref-based clicks, which worked reliably throughout. `resize_window` to the desktop preset didn't visually change the rendered layout (stayed in the mobile tabbed "Chat"/"Evidence & Interaction" view) — worked around by using the tab-switch buttons instead of relying on a 3-column desktop layout. Neither issue reflects anything in the app itself.

**Close-out:** `CHI-07` moved directly to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (skipped the "leave in main table one more cycle" precedent some prior sessions used, since this session immediately continues into `CHI-08` and a stale main-table row would only add confusion). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.34`; "In flight now" bullet updated (not removed — 4 more sessions queued in this same design session); "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `CHI-04`'s entry aged out (already fully captured above). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md`, `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.34-CHI-07-*.md` touched, plus the one `.jsx` file. Serverless function count unaffected (no `api/` file added, `AI PATTERN CHECK` N/A — no `api/` route touched).

## S-LOG-18-design / S-LOG-18-activity-delivery (v6.3.31, `c5857c6`, 2026-07-16, worktree `log-18-activity-delivery-0716`)

**Relocated from `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" rolling window** — this entry had aged past its 3-slot window without ever being moved here, the exact drift the file's own header rule warns against. Independently recovered by two concurrent sessions (`S-AI-53`'s close-out and `S-AUDITLOG-design`) at almost the same moment, both pulling the same still-present summary line from `CLAUDE-STATE.md` rather than reconstructing from memory — merged to one copy here, no content added or summarized beyond the original bullet.

`LOG-18` ✅ Done, archived: root-caused live (3rd independent observation, 100% reproduction) — `lib/activity-log.js`'s shared `logActivity()` is fire-and-forget (bare un-awaited `fetch()`), silently abandoned whenever it's the last async work before a handler responds, since Vercel tears down the function's execution context right after the response is sent. Confirmed platform-wide, not `reflect`/`synthesis`-specific. Fixed: wrapped the write in Vercel's `waitUntil()` (`@vercel/functions`, new dependency) inside `logActivity()` itself — one file, zero call-site changes. One flagged unknown (this codebase's classic `(req, res)` handler shape vs. `waitUntil()`'s documented `fetch(request)`-shape examples) resolved live: works correctly, no fallback needed. Independently re-verified by the design session against real Supabase data: the decisive test's `reflect`/`synthesis` rows (ids `11647`/`11646`) confirmed to exist; 2 regression calls (16 rows total, including a full multi-hop delegation chain) confirmed no breakage.

## S-AI-53-design / S-AI-53 (v6.3.33, `e15586b`, 2026-07-16, worktree `log-19-teach-metadata-fix-0716`)

**Context:** `AI-53` (`docs/FEATURES.md`) found `SERVICE_CATALOG`'s `document-extraction` entry describing two operationally distinct things under one feature string — a real Claude Sonnet 4.5 call in `api/brief.js` generating structured training-doc metadata, and `api/extract.js`'s deterministic zero-cost PDF/DOCX/TXT parser — plus `api/extract.js`'s own two actions (parse vs. CSV upload) both hardcoding `ai_type: 'deterministic'`, indistinguishable from each other in By-Service. Root cause traced deeper than a naming fix during design: `api/extract.js`/`api/brief.js` (Vercel backend) had no shared source to pull a real service slug from — `SERVICE_CATALOG` lived only in `useAIActivity.js` (Vite frontend, which the backend cannot import), so each route hardcoded its own literal. Second, related finding: `knowledge-reinforcement`'s catalog entry claimed `['Embeddings','Structured Output']` but its real call site (`lib/knowledge-write.js`'s `embedAndUpsertEntry()`) only ever logs `patternsUsed: ['embeddings']` — a stale hand-maintained claim. Third, surfaced by a mid-design rebase: `src/aiPatterns.js`'s `AI_PAT.KNOWLEDGE_REINFORCEMENT` (used by 8 screens for step/task badges) hardcoded the identical stale claim — a concurrent session (`LOG-20`, already in `dev`) had changed this file's sourcing but explicitly preserved the content byte-identical, since correcting it wasn't `LOG-20`'s scope. Folded in as Task 5 (John's explicit call, a file-count exception to the normal 3-file cap) to avoid leaving a new Category M cross-reference contradiction between the two files.

**Fix — 5 files:** moved `SERVICE_CATALOG` into `shared/ai-patterns.js` (same treatment `AA-190b` already gave `PATTERN_CATALOG`), with the `document-extraction` entry removed and replaced by three: `document-metadata-generation` (the real AI call), `document-parsing`, `csv-upload` (both deterministic, `serviceType: 'logic'`) — 35 entries total (33 − 1 + 3). Added a derived `SERVICE_SLUG` export (`Object.fromEntries` over the catalog's own slugs) so `api/extract.js`/`api/brief.js` import their slugs instead of hardcoding literals — structurally prevents a future collision rather than just detecting one after the fact. `knowledge-reinforcement`'s patterns corrected to `['Embeddings']` only. `useAIActivity.js`: now imports `SERVICE_CATALOG` from the shared file (local 33-entry array deleted); `AI_TYPE_TO_SERVICE.extraction` repointed to `document-metadata-generation`; `byService`'s `patterns` field now self-derives from each service's real logged `patternsUsed` (via a new `PATTERN_NAME_BY_SLUG` lookup), falling back to the static catalog hint only when a service has zero real calls yet — closes this staleness bug class for all ~35 services, not just these two instances. `api/extract.js`/`api/brief.js` import `SERVICE_SLUG` and use it at both `logActivity()` call sites instead of string literals. `src/aiPatterns.js`'s `AI_PAT.KNOWLEDGE_REINFORCEMENT` corrected to `names('embeddings')` only.

**Mid-session rebase:** after this session's own push (`e15586b`), a concurrent session (`LOG-21`) landed on top of it (`01184f6`, moving `classifyRow()`/`buildActivitySummary()`/`percentile()` into `useAIActivity.js` from `useAgents.js`). A `git fetch` + `git rebase origin/dev` immediately before close-out found `e15586b` already an ancestor of `origin/dev`'s tip — the rebase fast-forwarded with nothing to replay, and a full re-read of `useAIActivity.js` post-rebase confirmed this session's `byService`/`PATTERN_NAME_BY_SLUG`/`SERVICE_CATALOG` changes were layered correctly under `LOG-21`'s restructuring, no duplication or conflict. `npm run build` re-run clean after the rebase to confirm.

**Verification:** Node test (`test-ai-53.mjs`) 9/9 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` zero errors (both before and after the mid-session rebase). `node scripts/check-ai-logging-coverage.js` — 3 CRITICAL findings, all pre-existing and unrelated to this session's slugs (`lib/agent-run.js`, `lib/vector-search.js`, `AIReviewTab.jsx` — none of `document-parsing`/`csv-upload`/`document-metadata-generation` appear in any finding, confirming a clean result as the kickoff predicted).

**Independently live-verified against the real dev deployment (`x-vercel-protection-bypass` header confirmed live against a real 401→204 OPTIONS check, not assumed from memory):** (1) `POST /api/extract` (plain text file) — 200, unchanged `{text, wordCount, preview}` shape; Supabase confirmed new row `feature='document-parsing'`, `ai_type='document-parsing'`. (2) `POST /api/extract` with `action:'upload-csv'` — 200 `{success:true, path}`; Supabase confirmed new row `feature='csv-upload'`, `ai_type='csv-upload'` (taskId column turned out to be `integer`, not UUID — used a numeric placeholder id). (3) `POST /api/brief` with `ai_type:'extraction'`, matching `TeachScreen.jsx`'s real payload shape (`messages`/`agent_id:'chloe'`/`tenant_id:'global'`/`skipRag:true`) — real Claude Sonnet 4.5 call returned a real JSON metadata object; Supabase confirmed new row `feature='document-metadata-generation'`, `model='claude-sonnet-4-5'`. (4) AI Audit panel on the live dev preview (bypass cookie set via `?x-vercel-protection-bypass=...&x-vercel-set-bypass-cookie=true`) confirmed `SERVICES ACTIVE 34/35` (catalog total 35, up from 33, matching the kickoff's predicted math) with "Document Metadata Generation" (20 calls, historical + this session's live test)/"Document Parsing" (1 call)/"CSV Upload" (1 call) all rendering as distinct Logic/AI services, no merged "Document Extraction" bucket remaining, and "Knowledge Reinforcement"'s subtitle reading "Embeddings" only. Zero console errors on a fresh page load.

**Close-out:** `AI-53`'s `docs/FEATURES.md` row updated to reflect instances 1+2 shipped and QA'd (status stays 🔶 Partial — instance 3, `lib/librarian.js`'s missing `patternsUsed`, intentionally deferred, unchanged from the kickoff's own scope call). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "In flight now" bullet removed; "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated (this entry added, `S-LOG-18` aged out to this file per the note above); "Version in dev" left at `v6.3.34` (`S-CHI-07`) since it is still the highest closed-out version — this session's `v6.3.33` does not move it forward. Worktree `log-19-teach-metadata-fix-0716` removed and its branch deleted per `CLAUDE.md` rule #7, session fully merged into `dev`.
