## S-AUDITLOG-design / S-LOG-19 / S-LOG-20 / S-LOG-21 (v6.3.35/v6.3.36/v6.3.37, `ee0bc18`/`fa20694`/`01184f6`, 2026-07-16, worktree `design-auditlog-0716`)

**Context:** John asked for direct confirmation on 7 points about the AI Audit screen (global "AI Transparency" panel) vs. the MI screen's Agent Routing/Agents panel, prompted by two screenshots — same display service? same record service? real industry pattern names, not invented? same central storage, do totals reconcile? patterns discovered in real-time or hardcoded? any other architecture rules to check? and a fix list.

**Investigation (no code written this phase, per the design-sessions-produce-docs-only rule):** read `ARCHITECTURE.md §19i` (Four-Source Trust Model, LOCKED) in full; read `src/hooks/useAIActivity.js`, `src/hooks/useAgents.js`, `src/components/AIActivityPanel.jsx`, `src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` (`MI_LOOP_SCOPE`/`PROPOSED_MI_AGENT_IDS` only), `lib/activity-log.js`, `shared/ai-patterns.js`, `src/aiPatterns.js`; grepped every `ai_activity_log` write call site across `api/`/`lib/` to confirm no stray direct-insert bypassed the shared `logActivity()`/`logAICall()` writers. Ran two live Supabase reconciliation queries (global all-time vs. MI-scoped/30-day) to get real numbers rather than assert reconciliation from code reading alone: 8,737 vs. 8,410 calls, ~$38 vs. ~$37 estimated cost — close today only because current real traffic is almost entirely MI-loop activity within 30 days, not because of any structural guarantee.

**Findings reported to John, verbatim per question:** (1) different hooks/components (`useAIActivity()` vs. `useAgentActivitySummary()`) — not the same display service. (2) same central table (`ai_activity_log`), exactly two canonical writers (`logAICall()` client-side, `logActivity()` server-side, `AA-190`), every server call site confirmed compliant. (3) real published pattern names confirmed (`AA-190b`'s Orchestrator-Workers/Prompt Compression/Case Retention renames, each citing a real source in code comments). (4) same table, but two independently-scoped queries — totals converge today by coincidence of current usage, not design. (5) display-side self-maintaining (`LOG-14`'s auto-bucket fallback), but pattern *detection* itself still requires a hand-written detector per pattern (`§19i`'s 4-bucket model) — not automatic. (6) found two unprompted gaps: a stale comment in `useAgents.js` (claimed a 500-row cap `AI-184` already removed) and a second, independent hardcoded pattern-label module (`src/aiPatterns.js`, used by 8 other screens' badges) with no structural link to the canonical catalog. (7) 3-item fix list logged as `LOG-19`/`LOG-20`/`LOG-21`.

John's direction: put all 3 through design + code in this same session. For `LOG-21` specifically, asked to choose between "document the divergence as intentional" and "build a shared aggregation core" — John chose the latter (bigger lift, real refactor, explicitly not just documentation).

**`LOG-19` (v6.3.35, `ee0bc18`) ✅ Done:** one-file comment fix — `useAgents.js`'s comment above `useAgentActivitySummary()` corrected to stop claiming a 500-row cap that `AI-184` already removed; real distinction (fetch trigger/lifecycle, not row coverage) substituted. Node test 3/3 PASS (parse/export sanity only, no behavior to assert), build clean, `git diff --stat` exactly 1 file.

**`LOG-20` (v6.3.36, `fa20694`) ✅ Done:** `src/aiPatterns.js`'s `AI_PAT` strings now derive from the canonical `shared/ai-patterns.js` `PATTERN_CATALOG` via a new `names(...slugs)` lookup helper, instead of being hand-typed — every string confirmed byte-identical to its pre-fix value (19/19 Node test checks PASS, including a check that no inactive catalog pattern is ever referenced). `AGENT_PATTERNS` and all 8 consuming screens untouched. Build clean, `git diff --stat` exactly 1 file. Live spot-check on Roster's `KNOWLEDGE_TRAINING` badge tooltip confirmed unchanged rendering. **Unplanned but welcome consequence:** a concurrent session (`AI-53`) noticed `LOG-20`'s new mechanism mid-rebase and used it to fix a real, independent, pre-existing bug of its own (`KNOWLEDGE_REINFORCEMENT` falsely claiming `Structured Output`) — exactly the kind of single-source-of-truth benefit `LOG-20` was meant to enable.

**`LOG-21` (v6.3.37, `01184f6`) ✅ Done — the larger refactor:** `classifyRow()`/`buildActivitySummary()`/`percentile()` moved verbatim from `useAgents.js` into `useAIActivity.js` (the file both hooks already treat as the canonical shared cost/pattern module, per `AI-51`'s precedent). `useAIActivity()`'s own `byAgent` rewired to call the same shared function via a thin row-shape adapter (its already-normalized `log` entries mapped back into the raw-row shape `buildActivitySummary()`/`pairedAgentTurnIds()` expect) instead of its previous independent inline loop. Output shape unchanged — `AIActivityPanel.jsx` untouched. One deviation from the kickoff's literal text, confirmed necessary and correct: added `export { percentile, classifyRow, buildActivitySummary }` in `useAgents.js` alongside the import, so existing/future callers importing these from `useAgents.js` (as some did pre-move) keep working with identical function identity. Node test 6/6 PASS (function-identity check confirming no duplicate definition survived, plus a synthetic-row regression test matching pre-refactor semantics exactly). `npm run build` clean. `git diff --stat` exactly 2 files.

**This does NOT make the two screens' totals reconcile** — by explicit design, confirmed in the kickoff doc and re-confirmed at close-out. `MI_LOOP_SCOPE`/`PROPOSED_MI_AGENT_IDS`/`RECENCY_WINDOW_DAYS` are untouched; the two screens remain intentionally different scopes over the same table. What `LOG-21` closes is the class of bug where a future cost/dedup fix lands in only one of two independently-hand-written copies.

**Concurrency handled mid-session:** two other sessions (`AI-53`, `S-CHI-07` close-out) pushed 3 commits to `origin/dev` while `LOG-21`'s coding session was in flight, one of which (`AI-53`) substantially rewrote the top portion of `useAIActivity.js` (moved `SERVICE_CATALOG` out to `shared/ai-patterns.js`). Handled via the standard fetch/rebase/push-retry discipline — confirmed post-rebase, by direct code read (not re-trusted from the coding report alone), that `LOG-21`'s and `AI-53`'s changes coexist correctly in the merged file.

**Independently re-verified by the design session against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from any coding report alone:** opened the AI Audit panel live (`?debug=features`) — `TOTAL CALLS 8818`, `TOTAL COST $27.50`, `34/35` services, `16/24` patterns, `5` models, all rendering correctly; `BY PATTERN` section showing `Orchestrator-Workers`/`Prompt Compression`/`Case Retention`'s real renamed labels (confirming `LOG-20`'s canonical-catalog sourcing is live, not just unit-tested); `BY AGENT` section (the exact path `LOG-21` rewired) rendering all 20 agent rows with real calls/cost/avg-latency figures and zero console errors. Confirmed the MI screen's own "AUDIT" → "AGENTS" panel (11 active · 0 potential) also renders correctly alongside it. Zero console errors across both checks.

**Close-out:** `LOG-19`/`LOG-20`/`LOG-21` moved from `docs/FEATURES.md` to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped forward from whatever `S-AI-53`'s own close-out left it at, to `v6.3.37` (the highest closed-out version once this session's own QA passed); no "In flight now" bullet had been added for this session (design + all 3 coding sessions completed within one continuous turn, so there was no window in which a concurrent session needed to see one). This session independently recovered `LOG-18`'s full detail into this file at nearly the same moment `S-AI-53`'s own close-out did the same (see the entry directly above and its own note) — merged to one copy during this session's post-rebase conflict resolution, not duplicated. `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md`, 3 kickoff docs, plus `src/hooks/useAgents.js` / `src/aiPatterns.js` / `src/hooks/useAIActivity.js` touched across the 3 coding sessions. Serverless function count unaffected (no `api/` file touched across all 3 sessions; `AI PATTERN CHECK` N/A for all three).

## S-CHI-08-design / S-CHI-08 (v6.3.38, `ee4e222`, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-ux-0716`)

**Context:** second of the 5-session Channel Intelligence UX sweep (`CHI-07`→`CHI-08`→`CHI-12`→`CHI-09`→`CHI-10`), continuing directly from `CHI-07`'s close-out in the same design session. Two fixes John asked for in the original screenshot walkthrough: (1) the "⚑ Marcus flagged this — {review_reason}" caption rendered as its own separate element below the pointer-sentence bubble, in a different font (mono/9.5px vs. the bubble's body/13px) — John wanted it merged into the bubble, narrative first, same font throughout, with the redundant "Marcus flagged this —" words dropped but the ⚑ icon/brass color kept as a visual flag; (2) no speaker attribution existed anywhere in `MessageBubble` — John wanted a small, subtle "You"/agent-name label above each bubble.

**Design-time correction, before the kickoff doc was written:** re-reading `MessageBubble`'s actual source (rather than trusting the FEATURES.md placeholder row's own earlier guess) found that `qaEvidence.displayAgentCard` — which the placeholder had assumed would drive the assistant label — actually names whichever agent *formatted* the answer (e.g. Alex Reeves), not who's *speaking* in Chat. This screen's Chat column is architecturally always Marcus's own voice (`CHI-03a`'s original design, and the existing "Marcus-attributed" hop-badge comment). Corrected to a static "Marcus" label before scoping the kickoff doc, not discovered mid-build.

**Mock:** a two-bubble before/after HTML mockup (using DeepBench's real token values — navy/brass/mono, not generic placeholder colors) was shown and approved by John before the kickoff doc was written, per the standing "no unilateral UI decisions" rule.

**Fix — 2 files:**
- `src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` — both `MessageBubble` branches (the `qa`-kind branch and the default/non-qa branch, the latter's `needs_review` path confirmed still unreachable in live production today, same status `CHI-05` found for its own dormant duplicate) restructured so the flag caption is the bubble's own first child (`color:T.brassDeep, marginBottom:8`, inheriting the bubble's `fontFamily:body,fontSize:13` rather than its own separate font), and a new speaker-label div (`fontFamily:mono,fontSize:9,color:T.muted`) renders as the very first child of each bubble's outer wrapper — "Marcus" (static) on the qa branch, `{isUser ? "You" : "Marcus"}` (dynamic) on the default branch.
- `docs/STYLE-GUIDE.md` — new Section 36 locks both the speaker-label convention and the merged-caption pattern, explicitly noting it's deliberately quieter than Section 28's agent+role CTA pairing and deliberately not derived from `displayAgentCard`. The coding session re-confirmed Section 35 was still the highest section immediately before writing 36, avoiding a concurrent-session collision.

**Found live during the coding session, correctly left out of scope (flagged forward, not fixed here):** the "Marcus flagged this" string still exists in `QaEvidenceCard` (Evidence column) — the kickoff doc's own SCOPE RULES explicitly forbade touching that component this session (`CHI-12`'s job). Logged in `docs/FEATURES.md`'s `CHI-12` row with the exact current line number so that session doesn't have to rediscover it.

**Verification:** Node test (`test-s-chi-08.mjs`) 6/6 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` zero errors.

**Independently re-verified against the real deployed dev preview by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** the dev preview this time loaded directly into the real desktop 3-column layout (no landing-page overlay to dismiss, unlike `CHI-07`'s session) — `computer{action:"left_click"}` intermittently failed to register clicks on the example-question buttons (same class of tooling flakiness `CHI-05`'s session documented), worked around via `javascript_tool` dispatching a real `.click()` on the matched button element, which reliably drove the actual click handler both times it was needed. One live flagged-answer round trip (the EMEA co-op question, which reliably trips the Nordholm synthesized-data guardrail flag): confirmed via `getComputedStyle` — not just a visual read — that the flag caption div and its parent bubble share the identical `fontFamily` (`Inter, -apple-system, system-ui, sans-serif`) and `fontSize` (`13px`), that the rendered flag text starts with "⚑" and contains zero occurrences of "Marcus flagged this", and that the new speaker-label divs render in exactly the specified style (`"JetBrains Mono", "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, monospace`, `9px`, muted `rgb(120,109,82)`). The "You" label was visible on the user's own question bubble immediately after submission; "Marcus" was visible above the flagged answer bubble once it resolved. `Hops 2–3` / `Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in 50s` confirmed still rendering correctly below the merged bubble — no `CHI-07` regression. Zero console errors throughout. The default/non-qa branch's `isUser === false` sub-case (an assistant ack bubble showing "Marcus" via the ternary, as opposed to the qa branch's static "Marcus") was not independently re-triggered live this session — the identical ternary's `true` branch was already proven live via the user's own question (same code path, same `MessageBubble` return statement), and the qa branch's static "Marcus" case was also proven live; re-triggering a third live round trip to prove the same two-line ternary a third way was judged unnecessary per `STANDARDS.md` Category L's standing guidance against re-proving an already-verified mechanism.

**Close-out:** `CHI-08` moved directly to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.38`; "In flight now" bullet updated (not removed — `CHI-12`/`CHI-09`/`CHI-10` still queued in this same design session); "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `CHI-05`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry above). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched, plus the 2 files the coding session changed. Serverless function count unaffected (no `api/` file added, `AI PATTERN CHECK` N/A — no `api/` route touched).

## S-CHI-12-design / S-CHI-12 (v6.3.39, `861e299`, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-ux-0716`)

**Context:** third of the 5-session Channel Intelligence UX sweep (`CHI-07`→`CHI-08`→`CHI-12`→`CHI-09`→`CHI-10`), continuing directly from `CHI-08`'s close-out. `QaEvidenceCard` had no height/overflow control — headline/body/table/byline/elapsed-line/flagged-line/CTA were flat siblings in one unbounded div, so the "Needs your input" CTA rendered wherever the accumulated content pushed it; John had to scroll Column 2 to find it on a real long answer. Depended on `CHI-08` shipping first so the flagged-narrative line landed in the Chat bubble before being deleted here — confirmed live before scoping (the string still existed in `QaEvidenceCard` post-`CHI-08`, correctly left out of that session's scope).

**Mock — took two iterations before it actually demonstrated the bug:** the first attempt (an interactive HTML widget with height/toggle sliders) used sample content short enough that it never overflowed even without a fix, so both states looked identical to the card already live in the app — John: "all I saw was a mock evidence section, we already have this in the UI." A second attempt (a JS-driven before/after comparison) failed to render at all — the injected `innerHTML` never populated, leaving two empty boxes. Root-caused both failures as the JS/HTML approach being too fragile in this sandbox for a reliable design review artifact; switched to a static SVG wireframe (no script, no external font dependency) with genuinely long sample content that visibly overflowed a simulated column. First SVG draft still put the CTA hard against the bottom edge of the frame; John's explicit correction — CTA should always sit above the fold with margin to spare, and the scrollable content should read as its own bordered box that ends cleanly above it — was implemented and approved.

**Technical correction found during scoping, before the kickoff doc was written:** the `FEATURES.md` placeholder row's original plan was to reuse `MI-54`'s `flex:1`/`minHeight:0`/`overflow:"hidden"` pattern "one level deeper." Tracing the actual ancestor chain live found `QaEvidenceCard` sits inside `EvidenceColumn`'s own already-scrolling region (`overflowY:"auto"`, itself flex-column) as a plain stacked child, not a flex child being handed available height by its parent — `flex:1`/`minHeight:0` on `QaEvidenceCard` itself would have been inert. Corrected mechanism: a fixed `maxHeight:320` (px, not `vh`) directly on the content sub-region, which needs no bounded-height context from its parent at all.

**Fix — 1 file, `src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`:**
- Deleted the "Full Agent Routing & Answer Given in Xm Ys" and "⚑ Marcus flagged this —" lines from `QaEvidenceCard` — both duplicated in the Chat bubble by `CHI-08`. The "Formatted by {agent} · {role}" byline is not a duplicate, kept as the last line of content.
- Wrapped headline/body/tables/byline in a new div: `borderTop`/`borderBottom` (`1px solid T.line`, reusing the card's own outer border for the left/right edges rather than adding a full box border), `maxHeight:320`, `overflowY:"auto"`. The review-choice ack / "Needs your input" CTA footer is untouched, rendering below that box, unbounded, always fully visible.

**Verification:** Node test (`test-chi-12.mjs`) 10/10 PASS, deleted before commit — source-level checks confirmed both duplicate lines gone, the bounded box present with the exact `maxHeight`/`overflowY`/border values, the byline and CTA/ack copy unchanged, and correct source ordering (bounded box after the header, before the CTA footer). `npm run build` zero errors.

**Independently re-verified against the real deployed dev preview by the design session, not accepted from the coding report alone:** a real live Q&A (Japan GEO growth question, genuinely long — 5 body sections + an 8-row data table) produced `qaEvidence` substantial enough to trigger real overflow. Direct DOM/computed-style inspection (not just visual read) confirmed: the bounded box's `scrollHeight` (1485px) genuinely exceeds its `clientHeight` (318px) — real internal scrolling firing, not just a theoretical cap; the CTA element's bounding rect sits fully inside its scroll-ancestor's (`EvidenceColumn`'s own scrolling region) visible bounds with zero further scrolling needed; "Full Agent Routing & Answer Given" appears exactly once across the entire page (the Chat bubble only — zero leakage into Evidence, confirmed by a page-wide text-match count). Clicking "Good, thanks" correctly swapped the CTA for the ack line. Repeated the same flow on a real mobile viewport (375×812) — the original tab's `resize_window` call silently failed to propagate to the page (`window.innerWidth` stayed at the desktop value despite the tool reporting success), worked around by opening a fresh tab and resizing before navigation, which took correctly; confirmed identically bounded/scrolling content box, zero horizontal overflow (`document.body.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth`), and zero duplicate-line leakage in the mobile Evidence tab's own render path. Zero console errors on both desktop and mobile runs. Not independently re-triggered live this session: the flagged-line removal (the live answer wasn't flagged) and the "Have Priya… generate theories" click (reuses `CHI-05`'s already-proven `onReview` path) — both accepted via the Node test's source-level check plus direct code read, per the standing guidance against re-proving an already-verified mechanism.

**Close-out:** `CHI-12` moved directly to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.39`; "In flight now" bullet updated (not removed — `CHI-09`/`CHI-10` still queued in this same design session); "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `S-AI-53`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry above). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched, plus the 1 file the coding session changed. Serverless function count unaffected (no `api/` file added, `AI PATTERN CHECK` N/A — no `api/` route touched).

## S-MI-72a-design / S-MI-72a, S-MI-72b, S-MI-72c (v6.3.40/v6.3.41/v6.3.42, `f44138c`/`f59ef21`/`ed22dcb`, 2026-07-16, worktree `chi-screen-deps-0716`)

**Origin:** John asked whether several logged CHI features touching the same screen were dependent on each other or could be split across sessions (found `continuity-ux-0716`'s `CHI-07`→`08`→`12`→`09`→`10` sequence already in flight, already answered by `FEATURES.md`'s own dependency notes). Pivoted to a live walkthrough of the MI screen's Agent Routing and Agents drawers, ending in a specific ask: the Agents drawer's per-agent latency breakdown (`byKind` — "Librarian," "Data Room Custody," etc.) should be real per-pattern data instead, sourced from the same central `ai_activity_log.patterns_used` write path everything else uses, never a separately-invented label. Traced this directly to `S-MI-20` (2026-07-07), which had asked for exactly this and explicitly deferred it — `patterns_used` was empty on real rows at the time; `MI-67`/`LOG-16`/`LOG-18` (all later) fixed that, unblocking it.

**Also found and corrected mid-session (before scoping):** a prior architectural claim from this same conversation (that `agent_selection`/`display_format` events hardcode the wrong capability label "for Michelle specifically") was wrong in framing — re-verified live against `api/capabilities/execute.js`: `lastHelpSelection` (line ~450) and `buildFinalDelegationResult()` (line ~316) both already compute the real capability (`'project-manager'` literal; `targetCapabilitySlug` param) but drop it before returning to the client — a generic harness gap (`LOG-15`, already tracked, unfixed), not agent-specific. Not fixed this session (out of scope, `LOG-15` already owns it) — corrected the record and moved on.

**Fix, split 3 ways per `STANDARDS.md`'s 3-file/4-task cap (combined scope: 4 files):**
- `MI-72a` (v6.3.40, `f44138c`, 2 files): `useAgentActivitySummary()` (`useAgents.js`) gains `patterns_used` in its `.select()`; `buildActivitySummary()` (`useAIActivity.js`) gains a per-agent `byPattern` bucket (avg/total/max, mirroring `byKind`'s existing shape), gated by the same `classifyRow()` dedup `byKind` already uses. Node test 11/11 PASS (reimplemented aggregation logic standalone), build clean.
- `MI-72b` (v6.3.41, `f59ef21`, 3 files): `Drawer` (`SharedUI.jsx`) gains `onOpen`; `useAgentActivitySummary()` gains a `refreshKey` param; the Agents drawer (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) renders `byPattern` in place of `byKind` (removing the now-dead `byModel` sub-rows) and wires refresh-on-open through `MarketIntelligenceScreen`→`AuditColumn`/`MobileBody`→`AuditDrawersBody`, scoped only to the Agents drawer. Baseline row and the other 3 drawers explicitly confirmed out of scope, untouched. Node test 6/6 PASS, build clean.
- `MI-72c` (v6.3.42, `ed22dcb`, 1 file, patch): **real bug found live during this design session's own QA, not in either coding session's Node test** — `Drawer.toggle()` called the new `onOpen` callback *inside* `setOpen`'s functional-updater argument, a React state-updater-purity violation producing a live "Cannot update a component while rendering a different component" warning on every open. Root-caused via direct code read (not guessed): `toggle()` rewritten to compute `willOpen` directly, call `onOpen()` as a plain event-handler side effect, then call `setOpen(willOpen)` with a plain boolean. Node test 4/4 PASS (static source-shape check, since the bug class isn't reproducible in a pure-Node reimplementation — the actual gap that let it ship), build clean.

**Independently live-verified by the design session against the real dev preview (isolated `npx vite` against this worktree, same `launch.json`-resolves-to-main-checkout workaround prior MI sessions used), not accepted from any coding report alone:** opened the real Agents drawer and confirmed all 11 real MI-loop agents show genuine `PATTERN_CATALOG` names (RAG, Structured Output, Tool Use, Guardrails/Output Filtering, Prompt Chaining, Orchestrator-Workers, LLM-as-Judge/Verifier, Embeddings, Reflection, Prompt Compression) with real avg/call-count/max-latency values sourced from live Supabase data — zero capability-type rows remaining; confirmed the Baseline row and specialty chips render byte-identically to before; confirmed, post-`72c`, the warning no longer fires on a genuinely fresh page load or a confirmed real drawer-open action. One nuance honestly documented: the same warning text kept reappearing on the original test tab even after the fix and a full reload with zero interaction — traced to the preview tool retaining a persisted per-tab console-log buffer across navigations (confirmed by a brand-new, never-used tab showing zero errors immediately on load), not a live regression — corroborated independently by the fix itself being an unambiguous, textbook-correct application of React's documented anti-pattern fix (side effect moved from inside a functional updater to the plain event-handler body). `ai_activity_log.patterns_used`'s existence and `byKind`'s continued (unrendered) presence in `buildActivitySummary()`'s output were both confirmed via direct Supabase query/code read, not assumed.

**Backlog capture:** logged `MI-73` (patterns-label collision between Agent Routing's `PATTERN_CATALOG` patterns and the separate Agent Reasoning drawer's `the_reasoning`-count "patterns," same word two meanings) and `MI-74` (declared-vs-actual capability audit, from the Agents drawer's "N active · N potential" badge) to `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md` as deferred `S-future` items, surfaced but out of scope this session.

**Close-out:** `MI-72` moved directly to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (single combined entry covering `72a`/`72b`/`72c`). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.42`; this session's own "In flight now" bullet removed (fully closed, no follow-up queued); "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `S-AUDITLOG-design`/`LOG-19`/`20`/`21`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry above). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched, plus the 6 files the three coding sessions changed across `72a`/`72b`/`72c`. ## S-LOO-006-design/S-LOO-006 (v6.3.43, 2026-07-16, worktree `continuity-regression-0716`)

**`LOO-006` ✅ Done, archived — Owen's `qg-review-intent`: schema-agnostic triage synthesis + mandatory Library verification before any `synthesized_as_fact` block. Closes `CHI-06`, also archived.**

**Context:** John reported a Channel Intelligence Q&A dead-end that looked like a regression of `AA-194` (confirmed still intact, not the cause). Root-caused live against real Supabase traces to two distinct defects, both in Owen's Skill Profile content (`method`/`traits.schema`), zero code files:
1. **Schema-fragile triage (closes `CHI-06`'s null-`triage` crash).** Owen's `method` instructed him to escalate an unfixable guardrail block via `request_help`, then copy the delegate's `recommend_escalate`/`suggested_research_request` fields verbatim into his own `triage`. That only holds when Michelle routes to Sam Reyes (`intake-failure-intent`) — her routing is genuine agent-reasoned judgment (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §19d/§19e), and on a live-traced run she legitimately picked Nadia Farouk (`data-escalate-intent`) instead, whose schema has no such fields. `triage` came back `null`, crashing `buildFailureText()` (`CHI-06`).
2. **Inconsistent `synthesized_as_fact` firing on Library-backed content (~1 in 5, John's own live repro).** A real Data Room record (`the_library.id = 1c7104c5-...`, `data_type: synthesized`, the Nordholm co-op scenario) was being blocked inconsistently — Owen's own guardrail reasoning reversed itself on identical text within one run, with no independently-checkable fact grounding the call.

**Alternatives considered and rejected (John, this session):** hardcoding Sam as the escalation target (banned by `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19d's Correction note), removing Nadia's `data-escalate-intent` candidacy (same anti-pattern via subtraction), and removing `synthesized_as_fact` from the rule set entirely (rejected — it also catches a separate fake-hand-off-narration failure mode, still wanted).

**Fix (Task 1, one Supabase `UPDATE`, `skill_profiles` where `slug='qg-review-intent'`):** rewrote `method` (5736→7782 chars) so (a) escalation triage is synthesized in Owen's own words from whatever the delegate actually returned, schema-agnostic, no assumed field names; and (b) a `SYNTHESIZED_AS_FACT VERIFICATION` paragraph makes any `synthesized_as_fact` block mandatory-gated on a `request_help`→`delegate_to_agent` Library-verification round trip, binding on the verdict — pass if the claim traces to a real Library record (any `data_type`) and the answer's own `confidence_tier` matches it, block only if no record backs the claim or the tier misrepresents what verification found. Also updated `traits.schema.properties.triage.description` to match (no more "verbatim"). Two unrelated clauses that also reference `synthesized_as_fact` by name (the `na`-tier fake-hand-off clause, the capability-scope-decline clause) deliberately left untouched. `git diff --stat` confirmed empty — genuinely Supabase-content-only.

**Task 2 (Category F Node test, `test-LOO-006.mjs`, deleted after run):** 11/11 PASS — confirmed the write landed, the old verbatim-copy instruction is gone, the new verification/schema-agnostic-synthesis language is present, both unrelated clauses are untouched, and the `guardrail.rule_violated` enum is unchanged in shape (still 6 values, `synthesized_as_fact` retained, narrowed by process not removed).

**Task 3 (Category L live verification against the real dev preview, `https://deepbench-frontend-git-dev-roadmapventures-projects.vercel.app`):** ran the real Q&A→quality-gate chain (`ci-answer-intent`→`qg-review-intent`) end to end, 9 completed runs total (5 original positive-case + 2 retries after 1 transient infra 500 on the durable-loop `continue` checkpoint, unrelated to this session's diff + 2 adversarial negative-case questions), plus direct `durable_hops` inspection via Supabase MCP for full trace detail on each.
- **`CHI-06` closure confirmed:** zero null-`triage` outcomes across every genuine block (3 distinct rule violations hit: `synthesized_as_fact` ×3, `missing_confidence` ×2, `citation_missing` ×1; 2 distinct Michelle-routed delegates observed: Eleanor for verification, Sam for escalation triage) — `triage.recommend_escalate`/`suggested_research_request` always well-formed, matching whatever the actually-routed delegate returned.
- **Verification-grounded blocking confirmed real, not cosmetic:** every block's `guardrail.reason` explicitly cites real Eleanor Data Room findings (e.g. *"Eleanor's Data Room verification confirms that the primary record backing the Nordholm-specific claims... is flagged as SYNTHESIZED, not sourced"*), directly traceable in `durable_hops.result` — the mandatory verify-before-block mechanism fires and is binding, exactly as specified.
- **Negative case (2 adversarial leading/false-premise questions, e.g. "Confirm that Nordholm hit 92% in Q1 2024..."):** guardrail correctly blocked both (`missing_confidence`, citing the same real verification grounding) — the fix did not make the guardrail toothless against genuine fabrication/mistagging.
- **Found live, spun off as `LOO-007` (not fixed here, out of this session's scope):** the kickoff's literal "5/5 clean `pass`" acceptance bar was not met — of 7 total positive-case runs against the identical Nordholm question, only 3 passed cleanly, 2 more passed after Owen's own pre-existing retry mechanism corrected Marcus's mistagged `confidence_tier`, and 2 blocked on a genuine mismatch. Root cause: Marcus's `ci-answer-intent` inconsistently self-tags `confidence_tier` on this same record across identical repeated runs (`sourced` ×3, `inferred` ×2, `synthesized` ×2 observed), despite `AA-154`'s existing `temperature=0` pinning — real run-to-run variance on a subjective self-classification call, not a defect in this session's fix. Every one of those mistags is now correctly caught and blocked by the newly-grounded guardrail — the intended behavior, not a regression. Logged as `LOO-007` for a future design session (does Marcus's confidence-tier instruction need strengthening, or is 5/5 identical-question determinism the wrong acceptance bar for this class of check).

**Close-out:** `LOO-006` moved directly to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (Agent Architecture — AA section, alongside where it lived in `docs/FEATURES.md`). `CHI-06` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (Channel Intelligence — CHI section), closed condition met (Task 3 live verification, not Task 1/2 alone). New `LOO-007` row added to `docs/FEATURES.md` for the residual finding. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.43`; this session's own "In flight now" bullet removed; "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `S-CHI-08`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched — no application code files (per this session's own scope rule). Serverless function count unaffected (no `api/` file touched). `AI PATTERN CHECK` N/A (no `api/` route touched, no new AI pattern — existing `qg-review-intent` patterns unchanged).

## S-CHI-13-design / S-CHI-13 (v6.3.44, `7e7ced0`, 2026-07-16, worktree `evidence-footer-align-0716`)

**`CHI-13` ✅ Done — Evidence column's decision CTAs pinned to the card bottom, matching Chat's Send button anatomy.**

**Context:** John's live screenshot: `EvidenceColumn`'s (Column 2) action buttons didn't line up with `InteractColumn`'s (Column 1) "Send" button, even though both columns stretch to equal height in the desktop grid. Root cause: `InteractColumn`'s card splits a scrolling message list from a fixed footer row outside the scroll (holding the input + "Send" button), so Send always lands at a fixed Y position — `EvidenceColumn`'s card never got that treatment, one continuous `overflowY:"auto"` region, so whatever CTA renders falls wherever the content stack ends. Same shape repeated at 3 sites: `QaEvidenceCard`'s "Good, thanks"/"Have Priya…" choice (what John screenshotted), the hypothesis-result "Info Only"/"Store as Forecast" buttons, and `ConfirmationCard`'s Accept/Reject/Edit. John's explicit call, design walkthrough: fix the pattern everywhere, not just the QA card. Mock (before/after wireframe) approved before scoping.

**Fix — 2 files (`src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`, `docs/STYLE-GUIDE.md`), `SharedUI.jsx` read-only per scope:**
- New `selectEvidenceFooterKind(qaEvidence, hypFlow)` pure function: single source of truth for which decision is currently pending, gating one footer slot. Priority order confirmation > hyp-result > qa-review, mirroring the mutual exclusion the old inline gates (`!hypFlow.confirmation`, `stage === "result"`) already enforced implicitly.
- Removed the hyp-result button row and the `hypFlow.confirmation` block from their old inline positions in the scroll body; both relocated into a new pinned footer div at the card's close (`padding:"10px 14px",borderTop`, matching `InteractColumn`'s own footer row exactly), gated on `footerKind`.
- `QaEvidenceCard` split: its brass "needs your input" block extracted verbatim into a new `QaEvidenceCardFooter` component (byte-diffed against the deleted inline block — only the now-redundant `margin` was dropped since the footer wrapper supplies its own padding). `QaEvidenceCard`'s inner `maxHeight:320`/`overflowY:"auto"` cap (`CHI-12`'s workaround for the lack of a real scroll-body/footer split) removed — the outer scroll body now bounds and scrolls this content the same way `InteractColumn`'s message list does.
- `STYLE-GUIDE.md` §37 added: documents the scroll-body+pinned-footer anatomy as a locked pattern, cross-referencing §35 (brass card styling unchanged) and noting `ConfirmationCard` itself is unchanged, only its render location moved.

**Verification:** Node test (`test-CHI-13.mjs`, 10 cases from the kickoff doc verbatim) `ALL TESTS PASS`, deleted before commit. `npm run build` zero errors. `git diff --stat -- src/components/SharedUI.jsx` confirmed empty. `git diff --stat` overall: 2 files changed (in-scope).

**Independently live-verified by the design session against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from a local check alone.** Local `vite` dev couldn't reach `api/` (proxy hardcoded to port 5173, this worktree's dev server ran on 5219 — a pre-existing per-worktree limitation, not a regression), so verification ran directly against the pushed `dev` branch once Vercel's build picked up the new bundle (confirmed via bundle-hash diff before testing). A real Q&A round trip (Vietnam authorized-reseller-network question, full Marcus→Owen→Michelle→Eleanor→Alex 8-hop chain, 1m38s) rendered the qa-review footer. Direct computed bounding-rect inspection: the footer wrapper's bottom edge (705.5999908447266px) matched Chat's own footer row's bottom edge (705.6000137329102px) — a sub-pixel rounding difference, confirming genuine Y-alignment, the exact regression from John's screenshot. Confirmed the card's scroll body genuinely overflows and scrolls (`scrollHeight` 1170 vs `clientHeight` 300) while the footer sits as a DOM sibling of the scroll container, not inside it — staying fully visible with zero further scrolling needed. Clicking "Good, thanks" correctly cleared the footer (`document.querySelector('[data-feature-id="CHI-13"]')` confirmed absent afterward — no dangling empty bar) and showed the "✓ Good, thanks — no further action." ack line inline in the scrollable body. Zero console errors throughout (`read_console_messages` onlyErrors: none). Mobile viewport check confirmed zero horizontal overflow (`document.documentElement.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth`) — though the `resize_window` tool's requested 375×812 didn't apply in this run (page rendered wider, a tooling quirk consistent with `CHI-12`'s prior note about `resize_window` not always propagating), not chased further since the no-overflow result was already conclusive at the width that did render. Chat's own Send button confirmed unaffected (used successfully to submit the test question).

**Not independently re-triggered live this session:** the `hyp-result` (Info Only/Store as Forecast) and `hyp-confirmation` (`ConfirmationCard`) footer branches — both would require a full multi-minute hypothesis generate→test→commit chain. Accepted via the Node test's exhaustive coverage of `selectEvidenceFooterKind()`'s branch logic (including the two-way and three-way priority-conflict cases) plus a byte-level diff confirming both branches' JSX is an unmodified relocation of already-proven, unchanged code (`ConfirmationCard` itself untouched in `SharedUI.jsx`; the hyp-result buttons' markup identical pre/post) into the same footer-slot mechanism just proven live for the qa-review case — per the standing guidance against re-proving an already-verified mechanism for every case reapplying it.

**Close-out:** `CHI-13` marked "✅ Shipped and QA'd" in `docs/FEATURES.md` (not moved to archive this session — left for a future cleanup pass, consistent with `CHI-04`/`CHI-05` staying in place after shipping). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.44`; this session's own "In flight now" bullet removed (fully closed, no follow-up queued); "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `S-LOO-006`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry above). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md`, `docs/STYLE-GUIDE.md`, `src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` touched. Serverless function count unaffected (no `api/` file touched; `AI PATTERN CHECK` N/A per kickoff — no `api/` route touched, pure frontend layout).

## S-CHI-14-design / S-CHI-14 (v6.3.45, `ce14fba`, 2026-07-16, worktree `agent-routing-drawer-0716`)

**`CHI-14` ✅ Done, archived — Agent Routing drawer's zero-hop empty state: fixed stale copy, de-duplicated across desktop/mobile.**

**Context:** John's live screenshot: desktop `AuditColumn`'s Agent Routing drawer, with zero hops run, showed "Real agent-call events appear here as the conversation runs. About Channel Sales Intelligence and Demo Reset controls ship in S-MARKET-INTEL-01d / 03." — text he'd never seen before and wanted removed ("there should be nothing"). Root-caused: the second sentence was leftover internal roadmap scaffolding, unchanged since `S-MARKET-INTEL-01b`/`01c` (early July) — it names two internal session IDs directly in user-facing copy, and one of them (`01d`) had already shipped (v6.0.21, `489c2c5`), making the "still pending" framing simply wrong. `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md`'s `MI-01` row already documented this as a known structural placeholder tied to `S-MARKET-INTEL-03` (still open, untouched by this session — only the leaked copy was in scope). Separately found during scoping and confirmed by John as in-scope: mobile's equivalent empty state had already independently drifted to a different, shorter, correct-enough string with no shared source between the two surfaces — a duplicate-functionality gap per the Architect Review.

John specified the exact replacement copy directly: "Real agent-hop events appear here as the chat runs." — and explicitly called out the de-dup requirement ("should not come from 2 different places").

**Fix — 1 file (`src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`):**
- New `AGENT_ROUTING_EMPTY_TEXT` constant defined once, above `QuestionDivider` (the file's existing "shared between desktop and mobile" neighborhood, per the `MI-45` comment already there).
- Desktop `AuditColumn`'s empty-state `<div>` and mobile `MobileBody`'s empty-state `<div>` both now render `{AGENT_ROUTING_EMPTY_TEXT}` instead of their own independent literals. Each surface's own wrapper styling (`fontSize:12` desktop, `fontSize:11` mobile, same color/family) was deliberately left untouched — only the string became single-sourced, not the visual treatment.

**Feature-ID note:** originally drafted as `MI-75` (this screen's legacy prefix), corrected to the `CHI-[NUMBER]` prefix per `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md`'s explicit 2026-07-16 rule that new MI-screen bugs use `CHI-`, not `MI-`, now. Then, immediately before push, `git fetch origin dev` surfaced a genuine ID collision: a concurrent session (`evidence-footer-align-0716`) had already claimed and shipped `CHI-13` for an unrelated feature (Evidence column pinned footer, v6.3.44) between this session's initial grep-based ID pick and its push. Re-derived the next actually-free number directly from `origin/dev`'s `FEATURES.md`/kickoffs (`CHI-14`), renamed the kickoff file and all in-doc references, re-committed. This is the expected failure mode of grep-based feature-ID assignment under concurrency (unlike the atomic Supabase-counter version-claim mechanism) — worth naming here in case a future session wants to consider the same atomic-claim treatment for feature IDs, not just versions.

**Verification:** Node test (`test-CHI-14.mjs`, 6 assertions from the kickoff doc verbatim — constant exists with exact copy, stale sentence fully removed, old literal fully removed, exactly 2 call sites reference the shared constant) `ALL TESTS PASS`, deleted before commit. `npm run build` zero errors. `git diff --stat`: 1 file changed (in-scope), confirmed via `git show ce14fba` that only the two `ordered.length === 0` branches changed — the `else` (populated-hop) branches are byte-identical to before.

**Independently live-verified by the design session against the real dev preview, not accepted from the coding session's report alone:** `get_page_text` (not just a visual screenshot read) confirmed desktop shows the exact approved copy — "Real agent-hop events appear here as the chat runs." — with no trailing sentence and no `S-MARKET-INTEL` reference anywhere on the page. A `resize_window` to mobile alone did not trigger a layout change (`useIsMobile()`'s `matchMedia` listener didn't re-fire from the CDP-level resize — a known tooling quirk, also hit by prior sessions' `CHI-12`/`CHI-13` mobile checks); a fresh `navigate` reload at the same viewport correctly re-evaluated `isMobile` and rendered the pinned "Agent Routing · Live" pane with the identical shared copy. Zero console errors on either the desktop or the reloaded mobile load (`read_console_messages`, `onlyErrors: true`, both empty).

**Not independently re-triggered live this session:** the populated-hop transition and post-Clear empty-state reappearance (kickoff QA items 5-7) — would require driving a real Q&A round trip. Accepted via the `git show` diff confirming the hop-rendering `else` branches (`RoutingHopCard`, `groupEventsIntoHops`, `Clear`'s reset logic) are completely untouched by this commit — an unchanged code path, not a novel risk, per the standing guidance against re-proving an already-verified mechanism.

**Close-out:** `CHI-14` moved directly to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done, this session — unlike `CHI-04`/`CHI-05`/`CHI-13` which are currently sitting un-archived in `docs/FEATURES.md` as pre-existing drift from other sessions, flagged but not this session's to fix). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to `v6.3.45`; this session's own "In flight now" bullet removed; "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated, `S-CHI-12`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md`, `src/screens/MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` touched. ## S-SESSION-HYGIENE-0717 — Session-file hygiene review + now/next/later criterion update

**Session:** S-SESSION-HYGIENE-0717 (2026-07-17, worktree `session-hygiene-0717`, no kickoff doc — audit/hygiene session, no application code touched, no version bump) — docs-only.

**Part 1 — Hygiene check (`session-hygiene` skill).** Findings: `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s rolling window itself was compliant (exactly 3 "Last 3 sessions" entries), but two "In flight now" bullets were stale in two different ways — `continuity-ux-0716` (worktree fully merged into `origin/dev`, confirmed via `merge-base --is-ancestor`, *and* its stated "next up" claim, `CHI-09`, was already superseded — `CHI-13`/`CHI-14` had shipped since via two other, already-cleaned-up worktrees) and `continuity-regression-0716` (no matching worktree or branch found anywhere on disk — a fully dangling reference). Neither bullet nor its worktree was removed this session — flagged only, per the skill's own "report first" rule; genuinely a separate ask from what this session was asked to do. Also confirmed a second stale worktree, `loo-007-confidence-tier-0716` (also fully merged, no corresponding "In flight now" bullet at all). `docs/FEATURES.md` measured at 75.6 KB (over the skill's 40 KB flag) — root cause: 5 rows marked complete with non-`✅ Done` phrasing (`✅ Shipped and QA'd`, `✅ Fixed and measured`) that evaded the then-current literal-string archival check — this is the same root cause independently found and already fixed by a concurrent session the same day (`docs/STANDARDS.md`'s 2026-07-16 Status-vocabulary rule, `session-hygiene` SKILL.md Section 3's `✅`-prefix broadening) — confirmed already fixed elsewhere before writing anything new here, not duplicated.

**Part 2 — CHI-04/CHI-05 archival.** Before trimming either row, cross-checked whether their full diagnostic/QA narrative already existed elsewhere (`docs/SESSIONS.md`/`docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`) — confirmed yes for both (richer detail already present in `SESSIONS.md`), unlike two other candidates checked and rejected (`AA-153` — zero copy anywhere else, `AA-194`'s open roster-gap half — only its already-fixed portion is covered elsewhere). Moved `CHI-04`/`CHI-05` verbatim into `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`'s Channel Intelligence section (Type column dropped, Status relabeled `✅ Done` per archive convention, matching `CHI-13`/`CHI-14`'s existing treatment) — `FEATURES.md` dropped from 77.4 KB to 70.6 KB, no detail lost.

**Part 3 — Now/next/later criterion update.** John updated the tier-split criterion (terminology shift from "MI page" to "CI page" — confirmed same scope, not a narrowing, plus new categories: continuity, end-end q&a, agent model, AI auditing/log, UX/UI, db data model cleanup/seeding, added to the prior speed/loop/harness/charts list). Updated all 4 places the old criterion was echoed verbatim: `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md`, `docs/FEATURES-LATER.md` headers, and `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s Backlog Capture section — confirmed via grep these were the only 4 echo locations before editing.

**Part 4 — Full reclassification sweep.** Re-ran the now/next/later classification against the new criterion across all 3 files (spawned a sub-agent, pointed explicitly at this session's own worktree path, for the 225-row `FEATURES-LATER.md` sweep — its own summary confirmed that file had already had a deliberate prior CI-extraction pass done to it, only 1 genuine candidate found there). Read every candidate row's **full** text before moving, not a truncated summary — this caught 2 false positives that would otherwise have been moved incorrectly (`AGT-005` and `SE-02` each carry their own explicit "stays here" rationale in the full row text: one points to an already-promoted sibling row already in `FEATURES.md`, the other is tied to the Structural Enforcement track John has already explicitly deferred). Net moves: 14 rows `FEATURES-NEXT.md` → `FEATURES.md` (`AGT-002/003/004/015`, `MI-08/16/70/71/73/74`, `HAR-01`, `LOG-01`, `AA-70/119`), 1 row `FEATURES.md` → `FEATURES-LATER.md` (`TI-16` — different screen, Task Instructions, not the CI page, despite its blocker having resolved). Held back on explicit prior-decision grounds, not moved: `AGR-01` (John's own "next bucket" call, 2026-07-15), `AA-191`/`AI-42`/`AA-78` (each an explicit prior deprioritization). One genuine duplicate found and merged: `HAR-02`/`AA-197` were the same prompt-caching finding, discovered independently the same day via two different paths — merged into one row under the `HAR-` (harness-level) naming convention per John's explicit call, no detail dropped (verified both rows' distinguishing facts — Marcus's 5-hits-per-conversation example, Michelle's ~8.9K-token roster context, the `AA-170`/`AA-159` cross-references — all survived in the merged row), `AA-197` row deleted, its one live cross-reference in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (`CHI-03`'s archived row) updated to point forward to `HAR-02` rather than left dangling.

**Part 5 — Process-gap closure.** Four new dated rules written directly from gaps this session's own work exposed (not from a separate audit): `session-hygiene` SKILL.md gained check **5c** (an "In flight now" bullet is stale if any feature ID it names is already in `FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, independent of whether check 5 already flagged its worktree). `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s Backlog Capture section gained three: a criterion-change must be immediately followed by a full reclassification sweep, not just applied prospectively; read a candidate row's full text before any tier move, never a truncated summary; and when one design investigation produces multiple backlog rows, file them together for tier consistency rather than one at a time (the `S-ARCH-COMPETENCY-MODEL-design` investigation's ~11 `AGT-*` rows had ended up scattered inconsistently across all 3 tiers with no indication the split was deliberate). Two other proposed rules (Status-string standardization, semantic Done-row matching) were confirmed already fixed by a concurrent session before writing — not duplicated.

## S-ID-NAMING-AUDIT-0717 — Feature-ID naming-convention audit

**Session:** S-ID-NAMING-AUDIT-0717 (2026-07-17, worktree `id-naming-audit-0717`, no kickoff doc — audit session, no application code touched, no version bump) — docs-only, prompted by John asking whether this week's new IDs honored the naming convention agreed earlier in the week.

**Context found:** `docs/SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s "Platform Layers" section (confirmed with John 2026-07-15) is the actual source of truth — new feature/bug IDs use `[SCREEN-CODE]-[NUMBER]` (e.g. `CHI` for Channel Intelligence) or `[PLATFORM-LAYER-CODE]-[NUMBER]` (`HAR`/`LOO`/`LOG`/`MCP`/`MKT`/`DAT`/`AGT`) from that date forward. Critically, the rule **applies prospectively only** — the ~250 pre-existing `[AREA]-[NUMBER]` IDs (`MI-71`, `AA-191`, etc.) are explicitly not renamed. A concurrent session had already independently caught and fixed one drift instance the same morning (`AG-36/37/38/39/40` → `AGT-023/024`/`DAT-001/002`, commit `46e1061`) — confirmed this before treating it as new work, not duplicated.

**Audit method:** scanned every row in `docs/FEATURES.md` (the Now bucket, per John's ask) for a "found"/"New" date on or after 2026-07-15, then checked each such row's actual ID prefix against `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s taxonomy by content, not by pattern-matching the old prefix. Distinguished genuinely new rows from older rows that merely mention a later date in passing (e.g. a promotion-note timestamp from this week's own now/next/later reclassification session, or a fix-date cross-reference) — several rows initially looked like candidates from a broad date-grep but turned out to be pre-cutover legacy IDs correctly left alone (`MI-08/16/53/70/71`, `AA-70/119/159`, `AI-46/53`).

- `MI-74` → `CHI-16`: Agents drawer declared-vs-actual capability audit, raised 2026-07-16, same session/reason.
- `AI-46d` → `LOG-22`: residual un-threaded `traceId` gap, found 2026-07-16 (`S-LOG-09c-design`) — AI Audit/logging content, `AI` maps to `LOG` per the taxonomy, not itself.

Each recoded row keeps a "recoded from `X`, logged `Y` first" breadcrumb in its own text — no history silently dropped, matching the precedent the concurrent session's `AGT-023` recode already set. All cross-references updated: `AI-46`'s own row (which named `AI-46d` in its "residual gap" note), `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Next session queued" paragraph, and `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`'s archived `MI-72` row (which named both `MI-73`/`MI-74` — added a forward-pointing parenthetical rather than rewriting the historical narrative, same treatment `HAR-02`'s merge gave `AA-197` earlier today). `docs/SESSIONS.md`'s own historical mention of `MI-73`/`MI-74` (this file, an earlier entry) was deliberately left untouched — pure historical log, not a live index.

## S-WORKTREE-CLEANUP-0717 — Stale worktree cleanup + design-session architecture-awareness gap

**Session:** S-WORKTREE-CLEANUP-0717 (2026-07-17, worktree `worktree-cleanup-0717`, no kickoff doc — cleanup/process session, no application code touched, no version bump) — docs-only.

**Part 1 — Worktree cleanup.** John asked how staleness is actually verified before removing anything, since a worktree could in principle hold uncommitted design work. Answered by distinguishing two separate checks: `git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD origin/dev` (every *commit* already on `dev`) and `git status` (no uncommitted/untracked changes sitting in the working tree) — both re-run fresh on both worktrees, both clean. Separately confirmed the two worktrees' *claimed remaining work* wasn't actually lost: `continuity-ux-0716`'s bullet named `CHI-09`/`CHI-10` as still open — both confirmed still `❌ Missing` in `docs/FEATURES.md`, already pushed to `dev`, independent of the worktree; `loo-007-confidence-tier-0716`'s namesake `LOO-007` is likewise still tracked and pushed. Removed both worktrees (`git worktree remove` + `git branch -D`) and cleared `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s two stale/dangling "In flight now" bullets (`continuity-ux-0716`'s narrative was doubly stale — worktree merged *and* its "next up" claim superseded by `CHI-13`/`CHI-14` shipping via two other worktrees; `continuity-regression-0716` had no matching worktree or branch anywhere — `LOO-006`, the feature it named, had already shipped and closed out, v6.3.43).

**Part 2 — Why the naming convention isn't reaching sessions.** John asked directly how the new ID convention gets enforced — do sessions know to look it up. Traced the actual mandatory reading chain (`CLAUDE.md` → `CLAUDE-STATE.md`/`CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` for a design session) and confirmed neither file mentions `docs/SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` at all. The correct rule does exist, but only in `docs/FEATURES.md`'s own "Feature ID Format" section — and `FEATURES.md` itself isn't on the mandatory reading list, only opened when a session happens to check a feature's status. This is exactly why 3 rows drifted this week (`S-ID-NAMING-AUDIT-0717`, above) despite the rule being "live" since 2026-07-15 — the rule was correctly written but structurally invisible to the documented startup path. Fix: added a dated entry to `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s Backlog Capture rule (the section every design session already reads, that already governs "how to log a new item") pointing to `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s taxonomy — same fix shape as the now/next/later criterion, no new doc for sessions to forget to check.

**Part 3 — Design sessions re-litigating already-decided architecture.** John raised a much bigger, recurring pain point: design sessions repeatedly forget or contradict architecture/standards already locked and working in the product for a month, forcing him to correct scope every session ("quadruple overhead," "like working with a new employee every time"), rather than the session already knowing the product's own decided model. Root-caused via the same reading-chain trace: `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` Step 1 ends by asking "What would you like to work on?" — before any architecture doc is loaded — and Step 2 only reads `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` if the session's own self-assessment decides the topic "touches layer boundaries, migration work, or S-MIGRATE/S-BENCH/S-INFRA chain." That self-assessment is made with zero context (the requirement isn't even scoped yet) and is unreliable by construction, not by bad luck. Checked whether `ARCHITECTURE.md` at least cross-references `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s Product Area/Screen taxonomy to make a targeted read fast — it doesn't; `ARCHITECTURE.md` is organized by ~20 numbered architecture topics with zero mapping to which Product Area/Screen each governs, so even a session that decides to check has no fast way to know which sections apply, and either skims the whole doc (expensive, erodes over time) or guesses (the same failure mode already documented elsewhere in this file for tool-description reading).

**Two-part fix, John chose "both" but wants direct involvement in the second part:**
1. **Trigger fix (done this session):** rewrote `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` Step 1's closing question to ask which Product Area/Screen/Platform Layer (per `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`) the work touches, up front — and Step 2's `ARCHITECTURE.md` row is now mandatory once an area is named, not self-assessed.
2. **Cross-reference index (not started, deliberately):** a Product-Area/Screen → `ARCHITECTURE.md`-section/`STANDARDS.md`-rule index, so the mandatory read from fix #1 is a fast lookup instead of a full-document skim. John's explicit condition: he needs to be directly involved in how this is built, specifically the terminology used — his own words, sessions sometimes "create their own language" and end up talking past him. Noted the precedent: `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` itself was built the same way, "confirmed with John across two design conversations," not drafted solo. Next step is agreeing with John on the collaboration format (live walkthrough vs. draft-then-correct) before starting the mapping work — not done this session.

**Part 4 — Live architecture reconciliation with John (chose option 1: live, area by area, not solo drafting).** John proposed his own mental model for the platform: a 3-entity RAG-backed data model (Competency/Library/Reasoning), a Harness (agent communication mechanics), a Loop (orchestration/routing), an "Environment" (HITL dashboards per domain), "Platform Services" (shared display code/logging/knowledge-flow), and "Functional Objectives" (multi-tenancy/security/revenue/MCP — not yet built). Checked this against real industry patterns before responding rather than validating blind: the 3-entity data model matches the standard procedural/semantic/episodic memory split used in agent-memory architecture; Harness/Loop matches the standard mechanism-vs-policy split in multi-agent frameworks; Functional Objectives correctly isn't a layer, it's cross-cutting quality attributes. Checked against `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own existing content before proceeding (verify-never-assert-from-memory): found the old §1 "Four-Layer Architecture" already used "Platform Services" for a *different* meaning (Auth/multi-tenancy/security — actually John's Functional Objectives bucket) and "Product Modules" (stale, only 2 modules listed) for what John called "Environment" — surfaced both collisions rather than silently building on top of them.

**Resolved live, John correcting terminology at each step:** "Environment" → **Product Focus Area** (his term, sharper than "Product Area"). Platform Services collision → kept the name for John's shared-utilities bucket (his original intent), confirmed §1's *old* Layer 4 content is really his separate Functional Objectives bucket and moved accordingly. When John questioned why the conversation kept trying to fit everything into "layers" from the old doc: correctly conceded the point — 4 of his 6 buckets genuinely stack by dependency (Environment → Loop → Harness → Platform Services → Data Model, 5 real layers), while Platform Services and Functional Objectives don't belong forced into a numbered stack (standard layers-vs-cross-cutting-concerns distinction) — John confirmed this was "perfect." Read §19c/§19f directly (not from memory) to verify John's "Competency" entity — confirmed 3 genuinely RAG-embedded stores exist (`knowledge_entries`/`the_library`/`the_reasoning`), but the structured Skill/Capability model (§2) has no embedding column at all — flagged the ambiguity rather than assume; John's answer: both combined as one entity (his own example — an NIGP consultant agent's competency is both its assigned Skills *and* its own uploaded training documents, together).

**Worked example: AI Audit**, first consolidated summary written in this new format. Checked `docs/AI-SERVICES.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19i directly before drafting (both already existed, neither is duplicated, both cross-referenced instead). First draft named specific pattern examples (RAG, Tool Use, etc.) as if defining the catalog — John corrected: these are examples of what's currently detected, not the fixed standard; the platform works with the full breadth of real industry-named AI patterns, present or future. Corrected before finalizing.

**Answered John's 8 closing questions** (write everything down now; where AI Audit lives structurally and how it crosses layers; the pattern-naming correction above; yes to continuing this process for other areas; a full doc/code/memory sweep is real but sequenced later, not bolted on; no "how to work with John" doc exists yet, proposed building one; stated the actual operating policy — propose and wait for go-ahead on substantive changes, just do and report on small mechanical ones; validated the "model John's own decision patterns" idea as buildable, with real precedent already in the product — §19d's "sniff test" is exactly one such captured pattern, already formalized once, just not done systematically).

**Executed, live, this session — nothing left only in conversation:**
- `ARCHITECTURE.md` §1 rewritten wholesale (not renamed-in-place): 5-layer + cross-cutting model, replacing the stale Four-Layer Architecture. Old Layer 3's capability-routes table preserved as §1a (reference detail, not a layer of its own). Old Layer 4's Auth/multi-tenancy content preserved as a note under the new Functional Objectives.
- `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`: "Product Area" → "Product Focus Area" throughout (renamed via `sed`, verified). `AGT`'s definition widened to include `knowledge_entries`. New `SES` platform-layer code added (session process/meta-work) — deliberately distinct from the already-claimed `PRO` (Project Management screen), checked for collision before adopting (none found).
- AI Audit consolidated summary written into §1's Platform Services subsection, corrected per John's pattern-naming note, cross-referencing §19i/`AI-SERVICES.md` rather than duplicating them.
- 4 new `SES` rows logged in `docs/FEATURES.md` (Now bucket) covering everything this conversation surfaced but didn't finish: `SES-001` (continue the live area-by-area treatment for remaining Layers/Product Focus Areas), `SES-002` (full doc/code/memory sweep, blocked on `SES-001`), `SES-003` ("How to Work With John" reference), `SES-004` ("John's Decision Patterns" reference mined from `SESSIONS.md`/`FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` history, blocked on `SES-002`) — so John can start a fresh session against any of these directly, per his explicit request to close this session out with everything trackable.

**Close-out:** No version bump (docs-only, no code, entire session). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated — `S-CHI-14-design`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry above); "Next session (queued)" repointed to `SES-001` as the natural pickup. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched; 2 worktrees + branches removed earlier in this same session (pure git-level cleanup, no file changes).

## S-TESTING-DOCS-CHECK-0717 — Testing/regression infrastructure review

**Session:** S-TESTING-DOCS-CHECK-0717 (2026-07-17, worktree `testing-docs-check-0717`, no kickoff doc — investigation/Q&A session, no application code touched, no version bump) — docs-only.

**Part 1 — Is there a testing/regression section, and does it mean John doesn't have to test himself?** John asked directly. Read `docs/STANDARDS.md` Sections 4-9 and `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s Automated Design→Code→Verify Loop rule rather than answering from general impression. Confirmed yes, genuinely comprehensive: Section 4 (Node.js Test Requirements, categorized A-M including K/component-state-init, L/live-API-integration, M/cross-reference-consistency), Section 5 (Verification Checklist mirroring those categories), Section 6 (Browser Test Checklist), Section 7 (Manual QA Checklist Rules — explicitly states the design session runs this itself against live systems, John is not expected to and shouldn't be asked, only reverts if he starts a coding session manually himself). Automated loop ties it together: design session writes kickoff → spawns coding session → coding session tests/builds/pushes → design session verifies QA itself → reports PASS/FAIL to John. Noted the deliberate efficiency rule in Category L's "scoping breadth" amendment — a live test is required only for what's genuinely novel, not re-run for every row reapplying an already-proven mechanism, preventing regression checking from ballooning.

**Part 2 — Is it good as-is, or would cleanup help efficiency/speed?** John asked a direct follow-up rather than accepting the positive Part 1 answer at face value. Went back and actually read the content (not just section headers) to find concrete evidence rather than give a generic "looks fine" answer. Found 4 specific issues:
1. **Section 7 already self-documents a real staleness incident** — its own text: "this section was stale since before the 2026-07-02 rule... still said John runs these himself; that hasn't been true for the default flow in weeks," fixed 2026-07-15. Same undetected-drift failure class as `FEATURES.md`'s status-string drift fixed earlier today (`S-SESSION-HYGIENE-0717`) — but no equivalent hygiene check watches `STANDARDS.md` the way `session-hygiene`'s skill now watches `FEATURES.md`.
2. **Test Categories K/L/M are stated 3 separate times with no cross-check**: a prose definition (§4's "Mandatory K/L/M tests" lists), a re-worded checkbox restatement (§5's per-category checklists), and a historical bug-log entry (§8's "Known Bug Patterns" table, which maps specific past bugs to their Test Category). All three describe overlapping content; nothing catches them drifting apart if one is updated and the others aren't.
3. **Category L's actual current rule is scattered across 4 stacked dated amendments** — the base definition (§4) plus a 2026-07-02 "scoping breadth" note, a 2026-07-08 "loop-closure proof specificity" note, and a separate 2026-07-08 "`delegation_required` intent testing standard" note, all appended sequentially rather than consolidated into one current statement.
4. **Section 9 ("S-POLISH-01 — Deferred Known Issues") is one specific old session's leftover deferred bug fix**, permanently embedded in the general standards doc — an unclosed backlog item (a 3-line `useRef` fix for `TaskInstructionsScreen.jsx`, explicitly marked "Deferred — acceptable behavior for now"), not itself a standard, that belongs in `FEATURES.md`/`FEATURES-LATER.md` per this repo's own Backlog Capture convention rather than living in `STANDARDS.md` indefinitely.

**Framing given to John, not just a list of findings:** the cost isn't only drift risk — every coding session reads this doc, so the 3x-redundant representations and stacked amendments cost real tokens/time on every single session that touches testing, not just the occasional one that happens to hit stale content. Centralizing would speed sessions up directly, not just reduce risk.

**John's call:** its own `SES` item, not folded into `SES-002`'s broader architecture-model sweep — this is testing-doc efficiency specifically, a distinct scope. Logged `SES-005` in `docs/FEATURES.md` accordingly, proposing (not yet built): extract Section 9 to a proper backlog row; consolidate Categories K/L/M's 3 representations into one source of truth; collapse Category L's stacked amendments into one current statement (git history already preserves the "why"); consider extending `session-hygiene` (or a parallel check) to watch `STANDARDS.md` for the same class of drift now watched in `FEATURES.md`.

**Close-out:** No version bump (docs-only, no code). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated — `S-SESSION-HYGIENE-0717`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry). `docs/FEATURES.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched.

## S-AUTONOMY-TIERS-0717 — Router confirmation, a stale-term fix, and a new autonomy standing rule

**Session:** S-AUTONOMY-TIERS-0717 (2026-07-17, two worktrees — `router-check-0717` then `autonomy-tiers-0717` — no kickoff doc, no application code touched, no version bump) — docs-only.

**Part 1 — Router confirmation.** John asked directly whether typing "design" still kicks off a design session the same way. Verified by reading `CLAUDE.md`'s router text fresh rather than answering from memory of how the session had gone so far — confirmed unchanged: the router still just asks "Design session or coding session?", and "design" answers that directly. While verifying, caught a real instance of exactly the kind of drift today's own work was about preventing: `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s Step 1/2 text (rewritten earlier the same day, before "Product Focus Area" was agreed) still said "Product Area" in two spots. Fixed both, committed and pushed separately (`03098f8..f5643e9`) before this session's own scope began.

**Part 2 — Decision-autonomy calibration.** John raised a direct concern: design sessions have been checking in on too many small decisions lately, and he's unsure whether that reflects genuine stakes or whether he should extend more autonomy instead of gating on his approval by default. Answered using today's own conversation as evidence rather than an abstract framework pulled from nowhere: reviewed the actual check-ins made this session and separated the ones with real justification (`AA-194`'s rename — already flagged as ambiguous by `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` itself; the `PRO`/`SES` naming collision — an active conflict, not hypothetical; the architecture-model terminology — John had explicitly asked to be involved in that one) from at least one that wasn't (whether `SES-005` needed its own item vs. folding into `SES-002` — a low-stakes, easily-reversible call that could have just been made and reported).

**Proposed and John confirmed a 3-tier default**, written directly into `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` as a new standing rule ("Decision Autonomy Tiers," placed right after "No Coding in Design Sessions" — a foundational operating-mode rule, not something narrow enough to bury in the Backlog Capture section):
- **Tier 1 (decide and report, no check-in):** backlog file/tier placement when the criterion clearly applies; fold-into-existing-row vs. new-ID calls when scope reasoning is clear-cut; non-canonical wording/phrasing; anything cheap to revise later with zero downstream cost if wrong.
- **Tier 2 (decide, flag clearly in the close-out report):** judgment calls that could reasonably go either way with low cost either way — John sees the call made and can redirect after the fact rather than gating the work on approval beforehand.
- **Tier 3 (ask first, always):** canonical terminology/naming that future sessions will reference (the specific "talking past each other" risk); anything touching shared state other sessions or people depend on (pushing to `dev`, deleting a worktree, renaming a heavily cross-referenced ID); reversing a decision John already made; anything John's told this session directly he wants to be involved in, for that topic.
- **Default to Tier 2 when genuinely unsure** which tier applies — the asymmetry is deliberate: a wrong Tier-2 call costs a correction after the fact, while defaulting everything to Tier 3 recreates the exact fatigue this rule exists to fix.

Cross-referenced from `SES-003`'s row in `docs/FEATURES.md` (the "How to Work With John" reference item) so that future item folds this rule in by reference when built, rather than re-deriving the same content twice.

**Close-out:** No version bump (docs-only, no code). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated — `S-WORKTREE-CLEANUP-0717`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry). `CLAUDE.md` router unchanged and reconfirmed; `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched (across the two worktrees this session used).
