## S-RULES-CHECK-0717 — Sub-agent/coding-session awareness gaps, checked before writing anything

**Session:** S-RULES-CHECK-0717 (2026-07-17, worktree `rules-check-0717`, no kickoff doc, no application code touched, no version bump) — docs-only.

**Part 1 — Are "sub-session," "sub-agent," and "worktree session" the same thing?** John asked directly. Answered precisely rather than treating them as synonyms: a **session** is a full independent conversation that always sets up its own worktree by hard rule ("worktree session" isn't a fourth category, just naming the mechanism); a **sub-agent** (the `Agent`/Task tool) is an ephemeral, prompt-scoped helper spawned from within a session that does *not* get its own worktree and starts with zero memory of the parent conversation; "sub-session" isn't formally distinct in this repo's docs, loosely covering either the spawned coding session or a versioned split (`72a`/`72b`/`72c`), both of which are actually full sessions.

**Part 2 — Coverage check, not assumption.** Full design/coding sessions are already covered by today's fixes (`CLAUDE.md`'s router is mandatory for every session, routes design sessions straight to `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`). Coding sessions deliberately don't read `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` at all — by design, they execute kickoff docs rather than making architectural calls themselves — so that's not a gap. The real gap: ad-hoc research/sweep sub-agents (like today's `FEATURES-LATER.md` 225-row sweep) get a hand-written prompt with no automatic path to `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`/`SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` — the exact same failure shape `CLAUDE.md` rule #3 already fixed for worktree paths (2026-07-16), just not yet generalized past "tell it where to work" to "tell it what's currently true."

**Part 3 — Does this also help a coding session avoid writing rogue/duplicate code?** John's follow-up. Honest answer, not a reflexive yes: no, not directly — coding sessions don't read `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md` at all, so this specific fix (sub-agent bootstrapping) doesn't reach them. The actual mechanism for catching duplication has to be the design session, upstream, before the kickoff doc exists — coding sessions only have a weaker downstream backstop via `CLAUDE-RULES.md` (the one file they read "if they hit a pattern question mid-session").

Checked `CLAUDE-RULES.md` directly rather than assuming its content (verify-never-assert-from-memory): confirmed zero duplication/shared-service-check guidance anywhere in it — Section 7 ("Critical Code Patterns") is bug-prevention patterns only, the "Before Every Commit" checklist (Section 9) has nothing like it either. Also found, while checking, that Section 9's "Manual QA gate" text was stale — still said "Wait for John to report PASS/FAIL," directly contradicting the automated Design→Code→Verify loop already confirmed live earlier today (`STANDARDS.md` Section 7, `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`). A coding session reading this literally would incorrectly halt waiting for John.

**Before adding a design-session-level duplication check, checked whether one already existed** rather than assuming the gap extended there too: `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` Step 4's "Architect Review" already has both a "Duplicate functionality" check (grep before building a parallel implementation) and a "Multi-site bug pattern → shared-service check" (added 2026-07-16, with a real incident — `AA-192`, 3 call sites sharing one unmigrated route, collapsed to 1 fix instead of 3). No new rule needed there — would have been a redundant duplicate of an already-good check, caught before writing it.

**Shipped, 3 fixes, all confirmed by John first:**
1. `CLAUDE.md` new rule #3c — for any sub-agent whose task touches naming/architecture/backlog classification, tell it to read the current `CLAUDE.md`/`CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`/`SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` as part of its own bootstrap, rather than hand-enumerating today's decisions into every prompt (which would itself go stale). Narrowly-scoped, fully self-contained tasks can still just be given what they need directly.
2. `CLAUDE-RULES.md`'s "Before Every Commit" checklist gained a duplication-check backstop item — explicitly framed as secondary to the design session's own Architect Review, not a replacement for it; if this item catches something, that's a design-session gap worth flagging back, not silently patching around.
3. `CLAUDE-RULES.md`'s Manual QA gate text rewritten to match the actual current process (design session self-verifies, doesn't wait for John, per the already-live automated loop) — the same John-doesn't-manually-test correction already made elsewhere, just reaching one more file that had been missed.

**Close-out:** No version bump (docs-only, no code). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated — `S-AUTONOMY-TIERS-0717`'s entry aged out (already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry). `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE-RULES.md`, `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `docs/SESSIONS.md` touched.

## S-AGT-023 / S-LOO-007-design / S-LOO-007 — Continuity RCA chain: schema gap, then Marcus's confidence_tier

**Session:** worktree `loo-007-confidence-tier-0716` (2026-07-16/17), continuing directly from `LOO-006`'s own design session (`continuity-regression-0716`). v6.3.46 (`AGT-023`, `77d9771`) + v6.3.47 (`LOO-007`, Supabase-content-only, no commit hash).

**Origin:** John shared a live screenshot of a Channel Intelligence dead-end — "how is this happening? I thought we fixed continuity" — believing it might be a regression of `AA-194`. It wasn't (`AA-194`'s fix confirmed intact). This session is the second half of that RCA thread, picking up from `LOO-006`'s own residual finding.

**`LOO-006` recap (prior session, for context):** Owen — The Proofreader's guardrail was made verification-grounded and schema-agnostic. Its own live testing found the fix worked correctly but couldn't hit its stated 5/5 clean-pass bar — 3 of 7 identical Nordholm-question re-runs still blocked, traced to Marcus — GEO CSO Expert's own `confidence_tier` self-tagging being inconsistent (`sourced`/`inferred`/`synthesized` all observed on the same repeated question). Spun off as `LOO-007`, not fixed there.

**This session, Part 1 — root-causing `LOO-007` itself.** Not a `temperature=0` determinism ceiling: checked `ci-answer-intent`'s full `method` and its schema's `confidence_tier` description directly — neither anywhere defines how to resolve `confidence_tier` when an answer's citations span more than one `data_type`. The Nordholm answer genuinely blends a `sourced` citation (industry benchmark) and a `synthesized` one (the Nordholm scenario record itself), with nothing to anchor a consistent choice.

**John asked for the underlying `the_library`/`the_reasoning` data model to be reviewed, to see if it explained the gap.** It did, and surfaced more than expected — full sweep (30 `the_library` rows, all 22 `the_reasoning` rows, not sampled) found:
- 9 rows of test/session-run residue mistaken for nothing (S-LIBRARIAN-04 write-capability tests, `AA-162`-era live testing) — fingerprinted 100% precisely by `category = 'Compliance'` (logged `DAT-001`)
- `the_reasoning.source_chunk_ids` empty on 21/22 rows — `reasoning-write.js` silently defaults to `[]` with no enforcement (logged `DAT-002`)
- Eleanor Voss — The Librarian's `library-write-intent` write schema had `data_type: 'learned'` (illegal on `the_library` per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19f, reserved for `knowledge_entries`) and was missing `'consolidated'` (the value the whole opinion-to-fact promotion mechanism depends on) — confirmed via a real promoted row (a Vitrine Tech correction) landing tagged `synthesized` instead of `consolidated`, with no lineage recorded (logged `AGT-023`, blocking `LOO-007`)

**A process correction, mid-session:** attempted to spawn `AGT-023`'s coding session immediately after committing its kickoff, without first walking John through the specific proposal conversationally — he interrupted, correctly: general agreement on the session's goal is not the same as sign-off on a specific kickoff's content. Reverted to conversational walkthrough-first for the remainder of the session (`AGT-023`, then `LOO-007` itself), each confirmed piece by piece before writing anything.

**`AGT-023` (shipped, v6.3.46):** fixed Eleanor's `data_type` enum (`learned` → `consolidated`) and — a second, independently-confirmed defect found by reading the actual insert code before scoping, not assumed from the schema alone — wired `promoted_from_reasoning_id` through `lib/librarian.js`'s `writeTheLibraryEntry()`, which had its own separate explicit param/payload list that would have silently dropped the field even with the schema fixed. Also fixed a stale `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19e doc note. 3 files. Independently re-verified from scratch by a second coding-session pass (unexplained sequencing oddity: the fix was already live on `dev` before that verification pass started — flagged to John, no clean explanation found, but every claim was independently re-checked against real Supabase/code state regardless, including catching a typo in the kickoff's own live-test instructions and self-correcting against real data).

**Two more findings surfaced during `AGT-023`'s own design discussion, confirmed non-blocking and logged separately, not built this session:**
- `AGT-024` — Nadia Farouk — Data Analyst's `data-patch-intent`/`data-patch-execute-intent` promote flow has no field referencing which specific `the_reasoning` row it's promoting (only `chunk_id`, the `the_library` row being superseded) — so even with `AGT-023` shipped, there's nothing to put in `promoted_from_reasoning_id`. John's design for the eventual fix: on promote, the source `the_reasoning` row should go quiet from normal retrieval (`status: 'superseded'` — confirmed the real RPC, `match_the_reasoning`, already filters on this, no new plumbing needed) while keeping a visible marker of its own history (`promoted_to_library_id`, mirroring `AGT-023`'s new field).
- `DAT-003` — John's own idea: `the_library` has no way to record who/what confirmed a promoted fact. Every promotion through Nadia's flow is already gated by `requires_human_confirmation: true` today, but that fact never lands on the row itself — unverifiable independently, and not structurally guaranteed going forward. Framed by John as a real showcase feature (AI-confirmed vs. human-confirmed facts, visibly distinguishable), not just an integrity fix.

**`LOO-007` itself (shipped, v6.3.47):** a "weakest-link" resolution rule added to Marcus's `ci-answer-intent.method` — when citations span more than one `data_type`, `confidence_tier` takes the weakest one; a single `synthesized` citation makes the whole answer `synthesized`, never averaged up by a stronger source. Supabase-content-only, one Skill, zero code files (Marcus already receives each chunk's real `data_type` inline via `AA-155`'s existing tagging — no new plumbing needed, unlike `AGT-023`).

**Along the way, John caught a real terminology collision** while reviewing the fix: `data_type: synthesized` likely isn't the right term for fabricated/hypothetical scenario content — `shared/ai-patterns.js` shows this platform already renamed a different item away from "Intelligent Synthesis" for exactly this reason (`AA-190b`), and "synthesizes" is still correctly used elsewhere to mean *combining real information*, the opposite of what a `synthesized`-tagged row actually is. The affected rows already carry better organic vocabulary (`category: 'partner_scenario'`, titles prefixed `"Scenario — ..."`). Confirmed live this does **not** block `LOO-007` — the resolution rule works identically regardless of the label — and logged separately as `AGT-025`, platform-wide in scope (touches `AGT-023`'s enum, `LOO-006`'s `synthesized_as_fact` guardrail rule name, and Marcus's own `confidence_tier` enum, not just one Skill).

**The actual deliverable — Category L live verification, per John's explicit request to close the loop back to `LOO-006`'s original unreached bar:** 5/5 re-runs of the exact original Nordholm question (`confidence_tier: synthesized`, `guardrail.result: pass`, every single run) plus 1/1 differentiating sourced-only case (correctly stayed `sourced`, not dragged down). John separately asked why each run took ~55s instead of the expected <1 minute total — confirmed live: `LOO-006`'s mandatory Library-verification hop is now firing consistently on every run, precisely because `LOO-007` made the input to it (Marcus's `confidence_tier`) reliable enough to trigger it the same way every time. Not a regression — the grounding mechanism working as designed, every run, for the first time.

**Also this session:** two new persistent memory corrections from John, both about *how* to work with him going forward, not this session's technical content — agent references must be `FirstName — Role` only (no surname, no agent code, and no "same exchange" carve-out, since that carve-out is exactly what let a prior version of this same rule lapse), and DeepBench mechanics must be explained in the platform's own vocabulary (Skill Profile, schema, `enum`), never invented layperson analogies. Also confirmed and reinforced: John needs proposals broken into one concrete use case at a time, never bundled.

**Close-out:** `AGT-023` and `LOO-007` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done). `AGT-024`, `AGT-025`, `DAT-001`, `DAT-002`, `DAT-003` remain open in `docs/FEATURES.md`, none blocking. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to v6.3.47, "Last 3 sessions" updated. Worktree `loo-007-confidence-tier-0716` and its branch removed per `CLAUDE.md` rule #7, this session's goal (deliver `LOO-007`) fully met.

## S-CHI-09-design/S-CHI-09 — Agent Routing drawer: within-hop chronological event order (v6.3.48)

**Session:** S-CHI-09-design (worktree `chi-tickets-0717`, 2026-07-17), coding session `S-CHI-09` spawned automatically per the Design→Code→Verify Loop. v6.3.48.

John asked to continue the open `CHI` backlog. Presented the 5 open tickets (`CHI-09`, `CHI-10`, `CHI-11`, `CHI-15`, `CHI-16`); John chose to split `CHI-09` (which had bundled two unrelated fixes) into two issues and work only the first.

**Split:** `CHI-09` kept the within-hop event order bug; the second fix (Michelle's unsummarized `agent_selection` raw-reasoning line) became `CHI-17` (next free ID — `CHI-16` was the highest in use across all 4 backlog files), filed `❌ Missing`, deferred.

**Root cause (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `groupEventsIntoHops()`, L796-817):** fed `ordered = [...events].reverse()` (newest-first, correct for hop-card stacking), but when merging consecutive same-agent events into one hop it just `push`ed each event onto that hop's own `events` array in iteration order — so *within* a hop, events inherited the same newest-first order the outer list uses, when a human reading one hop's own activity expects chronological (oldest-first) order. One shared function, called from both the desktop drawer and the mobile pinned feed — fixing it here fixes both call sites, no per-site patch (the repo's own "multi-site bug pattern → shared-service check" applied correctly from the start, since it was already one shared function, not duplicated).

**Fix:** one line added to the function's closing `hops.map` — `events: [...h.events].reverse()` on the non-boundary branch.

**Two side effects found while tracing this, confirmed in scope with John before writing the kickoff doc (not separate work):**
1. `RoutingHopCard`'s left-border color reads `hop.events[hop.events.length - 1]` (`lastColor`) — under the bug that was actually the *oldest* event; after the fix it's the true last/most-recent event, matching what the variable name implied was always intended.
2. `RoutingHopCard`'s React `key` is `hop.events[0].id` — under the bug that changed every time a new event streamed into a growing hop (forcing an unneeded remount); after the fix it's the oldest/first event, stable for the hop's whole lifetime.

**Coding session:** single file (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`), single task, exactly as specced. Node test (`test-CHI-09.mjs`, Category B) — the coding session found and fixed two pre-existing defects in the kickoff doc's own T2 test fixture (a miscounted hop-collapse total, and Owen's two sub-events listed in the wrong relative order) — confirmed these were fixture-only errors, not touching the actual shipped diff, before accepting the fix. 14/14 tests passed after correction. `npm run build` succeeded. `git diff --stat` — exactly 1 file, 6 insertions/1 deletion. Committed `416d173`, pushed via `git push origin HEAD:dev`.

**Design session's own independent live verification against the real dev preview** (Vercel deployment-protection bypass header appended as a query param to get past the SSO interstitial — see `docs/ENV-VARS.md`/memory `reference-vercel-bypass`): ran the live EMEA co-op-budget question end to end (6 hops, 1m 30s). Confirmed via direct `getComputedStyle` border-left-color reads, not just a visual glance — Marcus's hop 1 (`intent_routing` 3.6s → `qa_answer` 12.4s, `needs_review: true`) showed border color `rgb(182,135,58)` (`T.brass`, the `qa_answer`/needs_review color) rather than `T.navyMid` (`intent_routing`'s color) — proof the border now reads the *last* event in the hop, not the first, confirming side effect 1 live. Within-hop order for Marcus's 2-event hop displayed intent-routing before answer-generation — the only order that makes causal sense, and the order the pre-fix code could not have produced (it would have shown the answer before the routing decision). Owen's hop 4 (`delegation` "routing this to Eleanor" → `proofreader` "reviewing... 45.9s") showed border color `rgb(90,117,56)` (`T.moss`, matching `proofreader`'s pass-result color) — also consistent with the fix reading the true last event. Repeated the same check after resizing to a 375×812 mobile viewport — identical hop content and ordering from the shared function's second call site. Zero console errors on both. Side effect 2 (React key stability) not independently re-proven live — already deterministically covered by the Node test's T-side-effect-2 case (simulating a hop's growth step by step), per the standing guidance against re-proving an already-verified mechanism live when a pure-logic unit test already covers it.

Kickoff: `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.48-CHI-09-agent-routing-within-hop-chronological-order.md`.

**Close-out:** `CHI-09` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` as ✅ Done. `CHI-17` stays in `docs/FEATURES.md` as ❌ Missing, unaffected. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Version in dev" bumped to v6.3.48, "In flight now" bullet removed, "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated (`S-STEP3-FIX-0717` aged out, already fully captured in its own `docs/SESSIONS.md` entry, confirmed before dropping). Worktree `chi-tickets-0717` and branch `session/chi-tickets-0717` removed per `CLAUDE.md` rule #7 after this close-out commit is pushed.

## S-MOB-RULE-0717 — Desktop/mobile parity check + `MOB` ID series (docs-only, no version bump)

**Session:** S-MOB-RULE-0717 (worktree `mob-rule-0717`, 2026-07-17). Docs-only, no coding-session hand-off, no version bump.

John asked whether a rule was needed for desktop/mobile parity: he fixes desktop first, wants to know if the same issue affects mobile, wants trivial mobile fixes folded into the same session, and wants anything requiring real design work logged automatically rather than dropped.

**Design question raised and resolved by explicit override:** the natural fit per `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s own stated principle ("ID = where, Type = what") would be a `Type: Mobile` tag on existing rows, not a new ID prefix — mobile isn't a Screen or a Platform Layer, it's a device context cutting across all of them. Recommended the `Type` tag on that basis. John explicitly overrode this: *"i want you to write it in, but for now everything is given the MOB id. It helps me prioritize - i consider fixes for it as a single entitiy, and then each one i can flag the bucket."* Accepted directly — a legitimate prioritization workflow the ID-purity argument doesn't account for (batch-viewing all open mobile work as one queue, independent of which screen/layer each item's root cause lives in).

**Collision check before adopting `MOB`:** `grep -orh "MOB-[0-9]\+"` plus a broader `\bMOB\b` across `docs/FEATURES.md`, `docs/FEATURES-NEXT.md`, `docs/FEATURES-LATER.md`, `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `docs/SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` — zero hits, confirmed clear.

**Shipped:**
1. `docs/SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` — new `MOB` row in the Platform Layers table, directly after the `SES` row (added by an earlier same-day session), same 4-column format. Row text explicitly calls out `MOB` as a deliberate exception to the taxonomy's own principle, quotes John's rationale, and points to the new Architect Review check as the row's populating mechanism.
2. `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` Step 4's Architect Review — new bullet, **Desktop/mobile parity check (added 2026-07-17):** if a session's fix touches a UI element existing on both desktop and mobile, check the mobile counterpart. Trivial fix → fold into the same session. Real design work needed → log immediately under `MOB`, never drop silently.
3. **Incidental staleness fix, same section:** the Architect Review's intro line still read "Check all four:" despite already listing 7 checks before this addition (Duplicate functionality, Multi-site bug pattern, Cross-reference integrity, Layer violations, Schema alignment, Delegation legitimacy, Locked-section staleness) — corrected to "Check all of the following:" so the count doesn't need editing again on the next addition. Tier 1 (Decision Autonomy Tiers) call — non-canonical wording, cheap, obviously correct, no need to check in.

**Close-out:** No `FEATURES.md` row needed — this was a rule/taxonomy change, not a tracked feature. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" rolling window updated (`S-CHI-09-design/S-CHI-09` aged out, already fully captured above, confirmed before dropping). ## S-SES003-TSR-design / S-HAR-04 (v6.3.49, `5422f94`/`627e0be`, 2026-07-17, worktree `ses003-design-0717`)

**Session:** started as a check-in on whether John and Claude were reading "Task Success Rate" (formerly "Continuity") the same way, then pivoted live into fixing the top TSR issue for completion rate, with John's explicit bar: "if we tested it, it's a 5/5 success."

**Part 1 — Terminology rename.** John: "is that where we're updating definitions?" then confirmed a rename to "Task Success Rate," abbreviated **TSR** — the old name "Continuity" collided with an unrelated "Data continuity rule" heading in `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s NIGP-migration section. Checked first, not assumed: the canonical definition already existed and was well-formed (`docs/FEATURES.md`'s Type Taxonomy, tightened 2026-07-16 in `S-AA194-design`) — not loosely defined or contradictory, just sharing a word with an unrelated concept. Renamed consistently across `FEATURES.md` (definition + 16 rows + criterion quote), `FEATURES-NEXT.md` (cross-ref + 1 row + criterion echo), `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` (19 rows), `CLAUDE-STATE.md`, `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`, and the `session-hygiene` skill — 6 files, over the usual 3-file cap, justified as one atomic terminology decision (a partial rename would have recreated the exact ambiguity being fixed). Left historical records (`SESSIONS.md`, `FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, `docs/kickoffs/*`, worktree/session names) untouched per the existing prospective-only rename precedent. John's follow-up requirement — TSR must explicitly span all 5 architecture layers, so "top TSR issues" surfaces everything, not just Loop bugs — folded directly into the Type Taxonomy definition, citing `ARCHITECTURE.md` §1. Pushed `050b285`; hit a real rebase conflict mid-push (a concurrent session, `S-LOO-007`, closed out and archived the exact `LOO-007` row while this session was working) — resolved by taking `dev`'s already-archived state, not the stale pre-archival copy.

**Part 2 — Picking and scoping the top TSR fix.** Live-pulled the current open TSR-tagged backlog (~19 rows) and picked `AA-69` (Prompt Service chain timeout risk) as the strongest completion-rate candidate — a real, already-observed "Intermittent 504s under load" symptom, not just a lab repro. **John's scoping question caught a real gap before any code was scoped:** "is `plan.js` not used in the CI TSR? If not, move to later — or does this fix the harness for CI?" Read `plan.js`, `request-receivable.js`, and `execute.js` directly (not from the backlog note) to answer precisely: `plan.js` itself (Project Management's "Create Work Order" pipeline) is genuinely not CI-relevant — it runs DB Assembly → AI Enrichment → Request & Receivable with zero inter-step budget checking, unlike `execute.js`'s `runLoop()`. But `request-receivable.js`'s `callModel()`/`sendRequest()` — the functions actually making the risky sequential Anthropic calls (main call + parse-failure retry, each an independent fixed `AbortSignal.timeout()`, plus a guardrails check) — are shared Harness code `execute.js`'s CI loop also calls, and its existing hop-budget check (`AA-196`, p99-based) doesn't cover the tail case where an internal retry doubles a hop's call time. **Split, per John's direction:** `AA-69` narrowed to `plan.js`'s own PM-specific missing-checkpoint gap (stays in `FEATURES-LATER.md`); new row `HAR-04` (`FEATURES.md`, Task Success Rate) carries the CI-relevant shared-Harness half.

**Part 3 — Kickoff, execution, and independent verification.** John asked for a plain-language, use-case framing before the kickoff was written (problem/fix/expectation/real example) rather than architecture prose — provided directly, confirmed, then wrote `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.49-HAR-04-callmodel-deadline-aware-timeouts.md` (version claimed atomically, `dev_version_counter`). Fix reuses `execute.js`'s existing `_deadline` pattern (`AA-139`) rather than inventing a new budget mechanic: `callModel()`/`sendRequest()` gain an optional `deadline` param (byte-identical when omitted), each internal Anthropic call bounds its own timeout to real remaining time, and fails fast below a minimum-viable-call floor instead of risking a silent Vercel kill. Spawned the coding session per the automated design→code→verify loop; it shipped `5422f94` (2 files, `request-receivable.js`/`execute.js`), ran Category B (7/7 mocked) plus live Category L against the real dev deployment — 5/5 forced parse-failure-retry scenario, 3/3 ordinary-Q&A regression check, 1/1 `plan.js`-unaffected confirmation. **Design session's own re-verification caught one real miss:** the coding session's Node test file (`test-har-04-deadline-timeouts.mjs`) was never deleted before committing, violating `STANDARDS.md` Section 4's explicit rule — flagged back to the same coding agent (resumed via `SendMessage`, not respawned), which fixed it in a clean follow-up commit (`627e0be`, confirmed via `git show --stat` to touch only the stray test file). Then ran its own fresh live spot-check (one real `channel-intelligence`/`ci-answer-intent`/Marcus call against the deployed endpoint — clean 200, real content, 11.6s), consistent with the coding session's own reported results — satisfying the "one quick spot-check, not a full re-run" bar for a backend-only session whose Category L test already exercised the real pipeline.

**Close-out:** `HAR-04` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done, new "HARNESS — HAR" section — first row under that code). `AA-69` remains open in `FEATURES-LATER.md`, narrowed to its PM-specific remainder, unscheduled. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`: version bumped to `v6.3.49`, "In flight now" bullet cleared, this entry added to the rolling window (`S-RULES-CHECK-0717` aged out, already fully captured in its own entry above).

## S-CHI-10-design / S-CHI-10 (v6.3.50, `cc31e7f`, 2026-07-17, worktree `chi-ux-queue-check-0717`)

**Session:** John asked to work `CHI-10` off the CHI/UX-UI backlog survey done earlier the same session. Mandatory `session-hygiene` checks 1-3/5 ran first per `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` Step 1: `CLAUDE-STATE.md` (19.5 KB, over the ~10 KB baseline) and `docs/FEATURES.md` (100 KB, over the ~40 KB flag threshold) both flagged, not fixed (tripwire only, reported not restructured). Check 5 found 3 fully-merged stale worktrees (`chi-tickets-0717`, `ses-efficiency-review-0717`, `ses003-design-0717`) and check 5b found 2 orphaned never-registered directories (`agent-routing-drawer-0716`, `chi-screen-deps-0716`) — flagged, not cleaned up (John didn't ask for that pass this session). **Real finding, not just doc bloat:** `chi-tickets-0717`'s own commit log showed `CHI-09`'s fix already merged into `origin/dev` (`416d173`), while `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "In flight now" bullet still described it as pending — flagged to John mid-session; resolved at this session's own close-out (see below), once a concurrent session's own cleanup of `docs/FEATURES.md`'s side of the same drift was confirmed via a fresh read at close-out time, not assumed.

**Scoping.** `CHI-10`'s original find (`continuity-ux-0716`) proposed two fixes: bump the ETA estimate's percentile (p75→p90) and fix a "'<'/'>' wording inconsistency" between the immediate ceiling (`"expect < 2m"`) and the later real estimate (`"expect > Xm"`). Reading `STYLE-GUIDE.md` §5a directly (not from memory) found that pairing is the documented, LOCKED, deliberate two-stage design (`S-MI-49`, 2026-07-13) — not a bug. Surfaced this to John before proposing scope; John confirmed: keep the two-stage ceiling→floor pattern and its exact copy, drop the wording-fix half entirely. John also asked two direct questions, answered by reading the code fresh rather than recalling architecture from memory: (1) whether actual run time is logged for future accuracy — yes, `lib/activity-log.js`'s `logActivity()` (`AA-190`) is the single shared server-side writer, already capturing real `latency_ms` for every AI call; (2) whether the calculator/storage/display are each a central service — yes to all three: `percentile()` in `useAIActivity.js` (centralized by `LOG-21` specifically to prevent this exact math from diverging), and `AgentWorkingIndicator` (locked `STYLE-GUIDE.md` §5a as the one canonical "agent is working" component platform-wide). No new plumbing needed — this session is a single-parameter change on an already-centralized pipeline. Grep-verified before scoping: `.p75`/`p75:` appears at exactly 4 locations in `src/` (1 write site, 3 read sites), no other file reads it — safe to rename in place, no multi-site duplicate-fix risk.

**Also corrected a real misunderstanding before finalizing scope:** John's framing ("propose a time, then increase it by 90%") wasn't how percentiles work — explained directly that p75→p90 means reading a higher point on the same historical latency distribution (90% of past runs finished by this time, vs. 75%), not a +90% multiplier on the estimate itself.

**Kickoff and execution.** Version `v6.3.50` claimed atomically (`dev_version_counter`). Wrote `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.50-CHI-10-eta-percentile-accuracy-fix.md` — 3 files (`useAIActivity.js`, `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`, `useAgents.js`), no `STYLE-GUIDE.md` touch (explicitly no visual/copy change). Hit a real rebase conflict pushing the kickoff commit — a concurrent session (`S-HAR-04`) had just closed out, bumping `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s version to `v6.3.49` and clearing "In flight now" to "None," conflicting with this session's own 2-bullet addition (`CHI-09`'s stale flag + `CHI-10`'s own bullet); resolved by keeping both of this session's bullets per `CLAUDE.md` rule #6 (adjacency conflict, not a real disagreement — their "None" was simply true before this session's own addition landed). Spawned the coding session per the automated design→code→verify loop; it shipped `cc31e7f` (3 files exactly as scoped), Node test 9/9 PASS (percentile math at p90, field-rename confirmation, an old-shape `p75`-only bucket correctly falling back to `null` rather than silently reading stale data, multi-hop contract unchanged), `npm run build` clean, grep confirmed zero remaining `.p75` references.

**Design session's own independent live verification against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the coding report alone:** submitted the same EMEA co-op-budget question `MI-58`'s predecessor session used, for a direct before/after comparison point. Confirmed the initial `"expect < 2m"` ceiling rendered unchanged on submit; confirmed the badge upgraded to `"expect > 1m 25s"` once routing resolved — meaningfully higher than `MI-58`'s own p75-era projection (~51s) for the same intent shape, direct live proof the p90 bump is real. The run itself (8 real hops: Marcus→Owen→Michelle→Eleanor→Marcus→Owen→Marcus→Alex, real Nordholm co-op-budget answer rendered) took 1m 44s end to end, genuinely exceeding even the p90 estimate by ~19s — expected, honest behavior for a percentile floor (still exceeded by design on ~10% of real runs), not a regression, and a useful live data point that the fix isn't over-promising. Opened the Agents drawer (Column 3) and confirmed all 11 agents' `byKind` avg/max/call-count rows render exactly as before — zero regression from the `byDepth` field rename. Zero console errors across the full run (checked before submit and after completion).

**Close-out:** `CHI-10` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done, `CHANNEL INTELLIGENCE — CHI` section). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`: version bumped to `v6.3.50`, both "In flight now" bullets cleared (`CHI-10` shipped; `CHI-09`'s stale bullet corrected — its `docs/FEATURES.md` row had already been removed by a concurrent session by the time of this close-out, confirmed via a fresh read, not assumed — only the `CLAUDE-STATE.md` bullet itself needed clearing), this entry added to the rolling window (`S-MOB-RULE-0717` aged out, already fully captured in its own entry above). Stale-worktree/orphaned-directory findings from this session's hygiene check remain unfixed, flagged for a future cleanup pass — not blocking, John didn't ask for that work this session.

## S-MOB-001-audit / S-MOB-001 (v6.3.52, `42188d3`, 2026-07-17, worktree `mobile-ui-audit-0717`)

**John's ask:** run a full mobile UI test and report any issues worth logging — an audit/investigation session, not a pre-scoped design session (`CLAUDE.md`'s "neither design nor coding" branch).

**Method:** full mobile-viewport sweep (375×812, `resize_window` mobile preset) against the real deployed dev preview, covering every screen in `docs/SCREEN-INVENTORY.md` — Channel Intelligence, Project Management, Create Work Order, Task Instructions, Spend Analysis, Agent Roster, Add Agent, Personnel File, Teach, Test My Team, AI Audit panel, About panel, hamburger menu. Screenshot/zoom tools were broken in this session's browser instance (consistently timed out); pivoted to `javascript_tool`-based DOM instrumentation (horizontal-overflow detection excluding intentional `overflowX:auto` scroll strips, tiny-font detection, tap-target-size detection) plus `read_page`/`get_page_text`/console-error checks — a viable substitute, not a downgrade, since it caught real bugs a screenshot alone wouldn't have (e.g. the exact pixel dimensions of the broken Clear button).

**Session-hygiene findings, flagged not fixed (not this session's scope):** `CLAUDE-STATE.md` 18KB (~2x baseline), `FEATURES.md` 97.5KB (~2.4x baseline), `FEATURES-NEXT.md` modestly over baseline, one stale fully-merged worktree (`design-chi-ux-0717`), two orphaned never-registered worktree directories (`agent-routing-drawer-0716`, `chi-screen-deps-0716`).

**5 findings, bundled into `MOB-001` at John's explicit direction across the conversation** (each folded in one at a time, per the one-issue-at-a-time communication pattern):
1. Mobile header subtitle ("AI Workforce Platform") rendered at `fontSize:5.5` (`AppShell.jsx:120`) — illegible, measured via `getComputedStyle()`. **Initially misjudged as a typo** — before scoping, `STYLE-GUIDE.md` Section 24 (LOCKED, `S-MOBILE-NAV-01-design`) was found to document 5.5px as a deliberate, John-confirmed value, never checked against real rendering. Surfaced the contradiction live per the locked-section-staleness-check rule instead of silently patching; John's call was to revise it (7.5px) and amend the locked section in the same session.
2. Mobile chat "Clear" button measured 36×13px live (`getBoundingClientRect()`) — desktop's mouse-oriented `MI-57` padding reused verbatim, no touch-target sizing ever applied for mobile.
3. Task Instructions' "Work Dashboard" breadcrumb (`TaskInstructionsScreen.jsx:756`) called `navigate("/")`, landing on Channel Intelligence instead of the real Work Dashboard (`/work`) — stale from before Channel Intelligence took `/`; not mobile-specific, folded in at John's direction anyway.
4. About panel version (`APP_VERSION`, `config.js`) hardcoded to `"5.1.32"`, over a full major version stale against the real `dev_version_counter`.
5. About panel's "By the Numbers" grid — 9 hand-typed numbers, investigated live at John's follow-up question ("make sure those get updated... I guess it will always be a db update at code session end?"). 3 of 9 checked against real state and found badly stale: Session specs 61 vs. 269 real `docs/kickoffs/` files, Bench agents 13 vs. 21 real roster agents, AI Services 19 vs. 35 — the last one directly contradicting the AI Audit panel, one click away on the same product. Answered John's actual question: no update mechanism exists at all, not even at session end — these are typed once and never revisited.

## S-CHI-19-design / S-CHI-19 (v6.3.53, Supabase-only, no code commit, 2026-07-17, worktree `design-chi-tickets-review-0717`)

**John's ask:** work `CHI-19` from the open backlog list this session surfaced (Marcus's Q&A answers dive into partner specifics without ever naming which real-world entity a generic question reference resolved to — e.g. "our EMEA retail partner" never gets connected to "Nordholm").

**Target correction, before the kickoff doc was written:** `CHI-19`'s own row named `qa-answer-format` (`method`/`output_desc`) as the fix location. Live Supabase check (per the row's own "verify live against Supabase first" instruction) showed this was wrong on two counts: `qa-answer-format` belongs to `screen-controls`/Alex Reeves, not Marcus's `channel-intelligence`, and its own `method` text explicitly forbids content judgment ("never invent, add, or omit facts... never content judgment") — it only reformats whatever raw answer text it's handed. The real content-generation Skill is `ci-answer-intent`, confirmed genuinely attached to `channel-intelligence` and assigned to Marcus alone. Also confirmed `output_desc` is never read into the model prompt at all for Intent-type Skills (`db-assembly.js`'s `typeSlug === "intent"` branch reads only `objective`/`method`/`traits.analysis_instructions`) — same shape as the unrelated `AA-167` finding, one Skill type over. `method` was the only viable target.

**Iteration on the fix itself, driven by John's own follow-up questions during the design walkthrough — not a single-shot design:**
1. First draft ("Our EMEA retail partner is Nordholm Retail Group, which is using 55%...") named the entity but John caught that it didn't establish the actual relationship — a bare name swap. Checked the real Data Room record (`the_library`, id `1c7104c5-9bc1-4ee7-9b61-d6070dc5aba8`, `data_type: synthesized`): Nordholm is described as "one of Apple's larger EMEA authorized resellers operating in Germany and the Netherlands." Revised the instruction to require pulling the entity's relationship/role from whatever the retrieved record actually says each time, not a hardcoded fact (a future partner will have a different relationship).
2. John then asked what "EMEA" itself is — confirmed it's a standard geographic/business-region abbreviation (Europe, the Middle East, and Africa), verified against `the_library`'s own `geo` column values (`Americas`, `Brazil`, `EMEA`, `EMEA Emerging`, `Europe`, `Greater China`, `India`, `Japan`, `Latin America`, `Southeast Asia` — a region list, not an organization list). John's resulting ask: spell out any abbreviation on first use per chat, abbreviate after — confirmed this is genuinely checkable, not just aspirational: `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `conversationContext()` really does join every prior chat message into a `PRIOR CONVERSATION` section injected before `CURRENT TASK` (`db-assembly.js` lines 509-527) on every call.

**Coding session:** spawned via `Agent` tool into this same worktree. Ran the kickoff's dollar-quoted Supabase `UPDATE` (append-only, `ci-answer-intent.method`) via MCP `execute_sql`, then the Category F Node test (`test-CHI-19.mjs`, live REST fetch against the real row) — 10/10 PASS, confirming `output_desc`/`objective`/`traits.schema.required`/`guardrails` byte-identical pre/post. Deleted before commit. `git status` empty — zero `src`/`api` diffs, confirmed Supabase-content-only as scoped.

**Design session's own independent live verification against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the Node test alone — a Category L check, since the coding session's own test never exercised the real pipeline end to end:** the real EMEA/Nordholm co-op question, run fresh each time. Run 1 (original append): entity resolution fired correctly ("Nordholm Retail Group, one of Apple's larger EMEA authorized resellers operating in Germany and the Netherlands, is using 55%...") — but the abbreviation was never spelled out, even on a brand-new chat with no `PRIOR CONVERSATION` section to justify skipping it. Root-caused: the instruction only said what to do when `PRIOR CONVERSATION` shows a prior definition, never what to do when there's no `PRIOR CONVERSATION` section at all (the first-ever question) — confirmed the instruction really was present, correctly worded, in the assembled prompt via a local `assemblePrompt()` reconstruction (no live LLM call needed for that check), ruling out a wiring bug.

**Run 2** (tightened wording — added "regardless of whether the question itself already used that abbreviation" + explicit no-`PRIOR CONVERSATION` handling): same failure, "EMEA" still not expanded. **Run 3** (escalated to "you MUST... unconditionally... even though EMEA is common terminology"): same failure. Entity resolution held correctly across all three runs. Three different wordings failing identically ruled out simple phrasing ambiguity as the cause.

**Diagnostic run 4 (John's own hypothesis — tested, not assumed):** rather than a fourth prose rewrite, tested whether Marcus was anchoring on the retrieved chunk's own unexpanded "EMEA" phrasing. Temporarily edited the live `the_library` record to pre-expand it ("...one of Apple's larger Europe, the Middle East, and Africa (EMEA) authorized resellers...") and re-ran the same question fresh. Result: Marcus's answer still compressed it back to bare "EMEA," even with the full expansion sitting directly in his retrieved context — ruling out source-anchoring as the mechanism. Reverted the diagnostic edit immediately after (`the_library` row confirmed byte-identical to its original via a `NOT LIKE`/`LIKE` round-trip check).

**Conclusion:** entity resolution is a genuine, reliable fix (4/4 live runs). Abbreviation-expansion-on-first-use is a real, repeatable model-default problem (0/4 across 3 prose wordings + 1 source-data test) that prose instructions — in either the Skill's `method` or the source data itself — don't reliably override. Also confirmed `guardrails.must`/`must_not` would not have helped even as an alternative lever: `request-receivable.js` shows those fields only drive a separate post-hoc Haiku validator that flags pass/fail after generation, never shaping what Marcus actually writes.

**Scope decision at close-out:** rather than ship a `method` field describing a capability that doesn't reliably work, trimmed the live text back to the entity-resolution instruction only (renamed "ENTITY RESOLUTION," dropped "& TERMINOLOGY"), with an inline note pointing to `CHI-22`. Filed `CHI-22` as a new backlog row scoped explicitly as "needs a non-prompt mechanism" (e.g. a deterministic post-processing/expansion pass in Alex's `screen-controls` formatting step, or a small known-abbreviation dictionary check) — a Layer 3/harness-level fix, not more Skill Profile content, whenever it's next picked up.

**Close-out:** `CHI-19` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done, `CHANNEL INTELLIGENCE — CHI` section). `CHI-22` added to `docs/FEATURES.md` (❌ Missing, Now tier — same track `CHI-19` was in). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`: version bumped to `v6.3.53`, "In flight now" bullet cleared, this entry added to the rolling window (`S-CHI-10-design` aged out — already fully captured in its own entry above). Session-hygiene findings from earlier in this same session (oversized `CLAUDE-STATE.md`/`FEATURES.md`, 4 stale merged worktrees, 2 orphaned directories) flagged, not fixed — not this session's scope.

## S-AI-AUDIT-TRIAGE-01-design / S-LOG-15 (v6.3.55, `484c9e8`, 2026-07-17, worktree `ai-audit-triage-0717`)

**John's ask:** full triage of AI Audit issues across the AI Audit screen, Channel Intelligence's Agent Routing drawer, and Agents drawer — what's legit vs. whack-a-mole, prioritized, and are the three views aligned. An investigation session, not pre-scoped.

**Part 1 — full backlog sweep.** Session-hygiene check first (mandatory Step 1): flagged `CLAUDE-STATE.md` (18KB, ~2x baseline) and `FEATURES.md` (99.8KB, over 2x its flag threshold) as oversized, plus 2 orphaned never-registered worktree directories and 2 likely-stale registered worktrees — reported, not auto-fixed. Read `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s consolidated AI Audit summary (§1) and §19h/§19i in full before scoping anything (Step 2's mandatory architecture read). Confirmed live in source, not from docs alone: `LOG-15` (Agent Routing drawer capability mislabeling) and `CHI-15` ("Agent Reasoning" drawer's "N patterns" badge colliding with real pattern data) both still reproduced in current `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`; `LOG-01`'s cost-as-$0.00-for-null-tokens claim also confirmed live in `useAIActivity.js`. Swept `DAT`/`HAR`/`LOO`/`AGT` platform layers plus the older `AI-`/`S-INFRA-01`/`S-AI-01`/`S11` legacy infrastructure family (`FEATURES-LATER.md`) — confirmed out of scope (predates the real `ai_activity_log` system, different screens). Found `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19h's own prose still described `AA-171` as "unfixed as of this session" despite it shipping and archiving 2026-07-08/09 — a real Locked-section-staleness finding, not yet corrected (carried forward, not fixed this session). `CHI-15` moved `FEATURES.md`→`FEATURES-NEXT.md` per John's direct call.

**Part 2 — "are these 3 areas aligned" deep-dive on `LOG-15`, redirected repeatedly by John into a much more precise investigation than the original walkthrough attempted.** Original proposed fix (thread the real capability slug through so the drawer shows the *correct* capability) was corrected multiple times in a row:
1. John: is "Channel Intelligence" being logged as if it were an AI Pattern? Verified: no — `channel-intelligence` exists only in `SERVICE_CATALOG`, never `PATTERN_CATALOG` — but the drawer's UI renders the Capability name and real Pattern names in one unlabeled line, a genuine display ambiguity even though the data underneath is correctly separated.
2. John: why isn't Michelle's routing work tagged as "Routing"/"Orchestration"? Verified: `PATTERN_CATALOG` (all 30 entries) has no "Routing" pattern at all, despite `agent-delegation`'s own description explicitly naming "agent routing (pre-call selection)" as a distinct real concept it doesn't cover — logged as `LOG-23`.
3. John, directly, after the design session proposed hand-adding a new `SERVICE_LABEL` entry: this repeats a 5-day-recurring hardcoding mistake — no persistent rule against it exists anywhere (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, `WORKING-WITH-JOHN.md`, `AI-SERVICES.md` all checked directly, none state it), and `useAIActivity.js` already solved "slug → label" generically via `LOG-14`'s self-maintaining fallback — `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`'s `SERVICE_LABEL` was a separate, older, never-migrated duplicate of exactly that anti-pattern. Logged as `LOG-24`, rule text not yet drafted (deferred).
4. John: `SERVICE_CATALOG`/`PATTERN_CATALOG` shouldn't be treated as golden — should be evaluated for retirement per "new rules of AI pattern assembly and naming." No existing documented direction found (`HAR-01`/`AI-35` are adjacent but different) — logged as `LOG-25`, explicitly waiting on John to explain the concept rather than guessed.
5. Full-repo verification of the "Channel Intelligence as fake pattern" question: swept every real file reference (`src/`, `api/`, `lib/`) — confirmed zero misuse anywhere except the one drawer line. Verified all 5 of `channel-intelligence`'s declared patterns are real, cited, industry-standard terms. **Queried real `ai_activity_log` data directly** — found the declared list itself is wrong in both directions: `Case-Based Reasoning`/`Streaming` declared but zero real occurrences ever (confirmed against `§19i`'s "not implemented" status); `tool-use` (1,120 real calls) and `guardrails`/`prompt-chaining` real but undeclared.
6. Same check repeated for the AI Audit screen's own `ServiceRow` — found a narrower, lower-severity version of the same instinct (pattern list embedded under the service name, no explicit label) but confirmed the underlying data is self-correcting for active services (`byService`'s `entries.length > 0 ? observedPatterns : svc.patterns` logic) — logged as `LOG-27`, cosmetic-consistency only.

**John's hard rule (from a live screenshot of the real drawer, 6 numbered corrections):** (1) no capability may ever display in the Agent Routing drawer, period; (2) the per-event line is real AI patterns only; (3) hops with no real pattern data are their own tracked issue, not a blocker; (4) line format `"AI patterns used: [names]"`; (5) pattern names should ultimately be reasoning-derived per hop, not catalog-matched (`LOG-26`, connects to `LOG-25`); (6) John's direct question on *why* 5 days of conflation — answered honestly: `describePipelineEvent()`'s own return shape and `SERVICE_CATALOG`'s own schema (bundling a capability's identity with a static declared `patterns` array in one record) structurally invited treating Capability and Pattern as two attributes of one thing rather than separate concepts; every fix proposed in the conversation had operated inside that frame instead of questioning it.

**John also caught a scope-tracking gap mid-session** — asked "would another session see this?" and the honest answer was no: `LOG-15`'s row hadn't been updated with any of the day's findings, and items 2/4/7 didn't exist as backlog rows anywhere, only in a findings doc nobody would open cold. Fixed immediately: `LOG-23`/`24`/`25`/`26`/`27` all given real IDs in `FEATURES.md`, cross-referenced bidirectionally with `docs/S-AI-AUDIT-TRIAGE-01-FINDINGS.md` (a new dedicated investigation-findings doc, updated live throughout rather than only at close-out, matching the existing `docs/S-ARCH-AGENT-VISIBILITY-01-FINDINGS.md` precedent). Checkpointed to `dev` twice mid-session (not held until close-out) specifically because John flagged losing context across sessions as "the bane of my frustration for 5 days."

**Full event-type audit, John's explicit ask ("check every event type") before allowing the fix to be scoped:** every `buildHopEvent()` call site in `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` checked individually against whether it carries real `patterns_used` at the level the renderer reads. 8 event types already safe (`intent_routing`, `qa_answer`, `proofreader`, `display_format`, `hypothesis_test`, `hypothesis_generation`, `memory_consolidation`, `patch_proposed` — all via the shared `callCapability()` wrapper, `MI-67`). 3 real, distinct root causes found for the rest — not 6+ symptom patches: `lastHelpSelection` (hand-assembled object built once in `execute.js`, reused 3 ways downstream — `last_help_selection` and `buildFinalDelegationResult()`'s `selection` field, confirmed via direct code read of line 323 — fixing it once fixes all 4 `agent_selection` call sites at once), `gate.triage` (a real field, but `patterns_used` lives one level up on `gate` itself — feeds `failure_triage`), and `{resolution, result}` (a wrapper object, `result.patterns_used` exists but isn't hoisted to the top level — feeds `patch_resolved`, 2 sites). Every fix traced back to the platform's one shared write-side authority, `request-receivable.js`'s `buildPatternsUsed()` (`§19i`'s documented mechanical model) — confirmed live, not asserted, that no pattern is ever declared on an agent profile and blindly counted.

**Kickoff doc:** `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.55-LOG-15-agent-routing-patterns-only.md`, 3 files (`execute.js`, `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`, `STYLE-GUIDE.md` §31 amendment), 3 tasks. `delegation`/`delegation_return` explicitly out of scope (blocked on `LOG-23`/`26`, separate session per John's direct call — "this session is `LOG-15` only, all other issues will be worked in a separate session").

**Coding session:** spawned via `Agent` tool into this same worktree. Deleted the now-dead `SERVICE_LABEL` dictionary (confirmed zero other references beforehand); removed `capability` from every `describePipelineEvent()` case; `RoutingActivityLine` now renders one generic pattern line, no per-event branching; fixed the 3 root-cause sites. Node test 11/11 PASS, live Category L test 4/4 against real Supabase/Anthropic (`runCapability('project-manager'/'agent-selection-intent')`, confirming `patterns_used` real and non-empty), both deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean. **One deliberate deviation the coding session caught on its own:** applying the capability-removal literally to the `delegation`/`delegation_return` cases would have produced a diff on lines the kickoff's own SCOPE RULES said must stay untouched — reverted those two lines to original text so the diff genuinely matched the stated constraint.

**Design session's own independent live verification against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the coding report alone:** a real 6-hop EMEA co-op-budget escalation chain (Marcus → Owen → Michelle → Owen → Michelle → Alex). Confirmed live: Michelle's pick (the original bug) — was "Channel Intelligence" + zero patterns, now no capability + real patterns (`Structured Output, Tool Use, Prompt Chaining, Guardrails / Output Filtering, RAG`). Alex's formatting (the other original bug) — was "Channel Intelligence," now no capability + real patterns. Owen's proofreader review (the "Quality Gate" case from John's screenshot) — also confirmed fixed live. Agent name/role unchanged at all 6 hops. `delegation`/`delegation_return` rows confirmed still blank, untouched. Zero console errors. `failure_triage`/`patch_resolved` not separately live-tested (different conversation flows — guardrail-block, hypothesis-commit) — covered instead by the Node test's exact same logic, not re-proven live a third/fourth time for an identical fix shape.

**Close-out:** `LOG-15` moved to `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (✅ Done). `CLAUDE-STATE.md`: version bumped to `v6.3.55`, this session's own "In flight now" bullet removed (a concurrent sibling session's bullet — `design-ai-audit-screen-0717`, which independently collided with and resolved around this session's `LOG-23`–`27` IDs — left untouched, not this session's to edit), this entry added to the rolling window (`S-CHI-21-design` aged out, already fully captured in its own entry above). `LOG-23`/`24`/`25`/`26`/`27` deliberately left open, John's explicit instruction — everything else gets worked in separate future sessions, pointed at `docs/S-AI-AUDIT-TRIAGE-01-FINDINGS.md` for full context so nothing has to be re-derived.

**`CHI-29` shipped broken 3 times in a row, each caught by this design session's own live verification, not by John or a later session:** a Rules-of-Hooks crash (hook called after an early return — fixed `S-CHI-26b`), the fade never appearing (deps-only recheck never re-fired for a single atomic answer-arrival — fixed `S-CHI-26c`, made the hook self-healing via `MutationObserver`+`document.fonts.ready`), and the fade rendering in the wrong place (`position:relative` on the wrong ancestor, shared with the footer — fixed `S-CHI-26d`, dedicated wrapper). Worth internalizing for future `EvidenceColumn`/`useScrollFadeHint` work: an early return before a hook call is invisible in a Node test and a build, only a live empty→populated transition catches it; a `position:relative` ancestor shared with unrelated siblings silently mis-anchors absolute children.

2 files total across all 4 patches, Node tests all passed (20+10+7+9), builds clean throughout.

**Design session's own exhaustive live re-verification after every patch, never accepted from a coding report alone:** 7+ full live Q&A runs across 5 different example questions, multiple legitimate Owen guardrail-blocks correctly distinguished from real bugs (same class as `AA-143`), precise `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement confirming the final fade position matches the scroll body's edge to the pixel. Zero console errors on every final run.

## S-CHI-34-design / S-CHI-34 (v6.3.68, 2026-07-19, worktree `design-chi-ux-0719`)

John's live screenshot walkthrough of Channel Intelligence's UX/UI produced 10 numbered items in one batch. **Shipped this session (`CHI-34`/`35`/`36`/`40`/`41`, all in `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`):** Column 2's title renamed "Evidence & Interaction"→"News & Evidence" in both places it renders (mobile tab + `EvidenceColumn`'s own header); the in-panel header suppressed on mobile only (new `bare` prop, same convention as `InteractColumn`'s); Marcus's flagged chat bubble reordered so the pointer sentence always renders first, with flag-aware wording leading into the ⚑ narrative below it instead of closing the thought; a mobile-only news-awareness status line above Send/Clear (loading → "Click News above..." → hidden-on-tab-open → reappears after Clear); and mobile auto-jumping back to Chat the instant a news card is clicked.

**Not worked this session, logged to `docs/FEATURES.md` for a future session:** `CHI-37` — root-caused live, not guessed: `fetchNewsCards()` (`CHI-33`'s background Jordan→Alex news-feed fetch) never fires the completion event that would resolve its own delegation placeholder, so hop 1 shows Jordan permanently stuck mid-handoff instead of Alex completing it, and the orphaned entry inflates the chat bubble's cumulative "Total hops" figure by 1. This session's own first-pass hypothesis (that fixing the orphan alone would also fix the count) was caught and corrected in-session by re-tracing `logEvent()`'s replace mechanism precisely — the entry occupies one slot in `pipelineEvents` either way, so the count needs its own distinct fix (tagging the hop as background/non-conversational, excluded from the cumulative count), not something that falls out for free. `CHI-38`/`CHI-39` — shimmer/motion loading-state effects for the news cards and the rotating example questions, likely sharing one visual primitive. **Item 4** (a chat answer's flag narrative telling the end user it "draws on synthesized partner scenarios") turned out to already be covered by `AA-143` (2026-07-07, still open) — updated in place with this session's live finding rather than recoded or duplicated, John's explicit call; also cross-references a second concurrent session (`CHI-31`) independently discussing the same underlying topic.

1 file, 4 tasks, Node test 6/6 PASS (deleted before commit), `npm run build` clean.

**Design session's own live verification against the real deployed dev preview, not accepted from the coding report alone:** confirmed desktop's 3-column header and mobile's tab both read "News & Evidence"; mobile's in-panel duplicate header confirmed gone on both the news view and the Q&A/hypothesis view; a real flagged answer (external-news channel-positioning question, triggering both Owen's revision cycle and Marcus's own `needs_review` self-flag) rendered the exact specified wording and order; the mobile status line's full lifecycle (loading text → "Click News above..." → disappears on tab open) confirmed via direct DOM state checks after the in-app browser's `computer`/screenshot tools began timing out mid-session (same recurring tooling instability noted in `CHI-33`'s own close-out) — verified via `read_page`/`javascript_tool` DOM inspection instead, a real dispatched click confirmed to produce the same state change a genuine click would; mobile auto-jump-to-Chat confirmed via the tab button's own active-state styling immediately after a real news-card click, before the answer arrived (the screen's pre-existing `MI-51` auto-switch-to-Evidence-on-answer-arrival then correctly took back over once the real answer landed, unrelated to and unaffected by this fix). ## S-CHI-37-design / S-CHI-37 (v6.3.70, 2026-07-20, worktree `design-chi-37-0720`)

Generic hop-completion mechanism (`callCapability` now requires a `hopEvents` declaration whenever a caller passes `onEvent`, structurally closing the gap that let `fetchNewsCards()`'s Jordan→Alex placeholder go permanently orphaned), migrated the 3 existing Q&A call sites onto it, then wired `fetchNewsCards()` via a new append-only `appendHopEvent`. `LOO-009` (Harness-layer version) logged separately, out of scope. 1 file, Node 11/11 PASS, build clean.

**Two real bugs found and fixed via this session's own live verification, neither accepted on code-read alone:** a live 23-question before/after diff against the real dev preview caught a 100%-reproducible hop-order swap on the Display call site (fixed, re-ran clean — 0 regressions/18 QA-routed, 5 non-qa skipped); loading the actual MI screen then showed hop 2 never rendering at all — root cause was a pre-existing `if (result.status) return result` early-return skipping the new hop-event block whenever a call resolves via internal delegation (`status: "final_delegation"`), a class of bug the 23-question script's standalone reimplementation of `callCapability` couldn't have caught. Fixed, then live-reconfirmed on a real preview deployment: fresh load shows Jordan (hop 1, unchanged) + Alex (hop 2) as two separate cards; a real question (8-hop Owen→Michelle→Eleanor escalation) starts its own hops at 3, chat's "8 hops total" matches the drawer's "8 hops" badge; Clear correctly refires the Jordan/Alex pair; zero console errors.

**Note (retroactively added 2026-07-20, `design-loo-010-0720`, while closing out the `LOO-009`–`011` track):** the `hopEvents` mechanism this session built was fully superseded and removed by `LOO-009b`/`LOO-010` once the harness gained a live completion event — see `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`'s `LOO-009`/`LOO-010`/`LOO-011` rows for the full follow-on story, including two real bugs this session's own "2 permanent hops" assumption masked (`LOO-009`'s Owen/Marcus pairing bug, `LOO-011`'s Jordan/Alex originator-credit gap).

## S-SES-010 (v6.3.95, pending commit hash, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-ses-010-0721`)

Full sweep of `CLAUDE.md`, `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`, `docs/WORKING-WITH-JOHN.md`, and `docs/STANDARDS.md` for standing rules whose compliance depends entirely on a session remembering to follow them, with nothing mechanical catching a miss — John's explicit call ("just fix it based on best practices, why do I need to be involved") after the initial scoping conversation. Found roughly 50 standing rules; ~15-18 had a plausible cheap mechanical check, a handful were already partially mechanized, the rest (Decision Autonomy Tiers, terminology discipline, approval gates, most of the Architect Review's semantic checks) are genuinely judgment-only and were left flagged, not force-fit into a script.

**Shipped, ranked by real-incident evidence first:**
- `scripts/check-session-docs.js` — mechanizes `session-hygiene` checks 1/2/3/3c/5/5b/5c into one script, plus a new check **5d**: cross-references every "In flight now" bullet against `git worktree list` in the *reverse* direction from check 5 (a bullet naming a worktree that no longer exists — the inverse of "a worktree with no bullet"). Found live twice this session (`design-loo-013-broker-fallback-0721`'s phantom bullet). Also caught a real staleness bug in its own first draft: checking `CLAUDE-STATE.md` from this session's own (branch-time-frozen) worktree copy produced false positives against worktrees other concurrent sessions had already correctly cleaned up — fixed by fetching `origin/dev` fresh for the cross-reference checks specifically (same pattern as `CLAUDE.md` rule 3b's fallback circuit-breaker), while checks 1/2/3/3c still read the session's own local draft.
- `scripts/check-api-deps.js` — mechanizes the `BUG-10` dependency-audit instruction (an `api/` import missing from `package.json` `dependencies`, invisible locally, crashes only on Vercel's clean install). Verified against a synthetic fixture (missing package, scoped-package resolution, dev-only-dependency warning, builtin exclusion) before trusting it against the real repo. Real repo currently clean.
- `scripts/check-model-ids.js` — mechanizes `STANDARDS.md` Section 12's canonical-model-id rule. First run against the real repo found live, pre-existing, **unfixed** violations: `api/brief.js:123,140` and `lib/agent-run.js:18` pass the short-form `"claude-sonnet-4-5"` as a real server-side Anthropic API call value (CRITICAL — a possible real call-failure risk, not just AI Audit cosmetics; `lib/agent-run.js`'s `SONNET_MODEL` feeds Brent's ReAct loop, possibly related to `LOO-008`'s existing note that Brent's harness "is not currently functioning even there"); `src/contexts/FetchContext.jsx:152`'s `logAICall()` call site uses `"claude-haiku-4-5"` (WARNING, client-side). Not fixed this session — logged `LOG-35`.
- 5 local Claude Code hooks in `C:/Projects/.claude/settings.json` + `C:/Projects/.claude/hooks/*.js` — **not part of this repo, not git-tracked, machine-local only** (real architectural finding this session: every session here runs with cwd `C:/Projects`, never inside the repo itself, per `CLAUDE.md`'s own router rule — so `deepbench-frontend/.claude/settings.json`'s existing hooks/permissions were never actually loaded for sessions structured this way; that file is likely dead weight, flagged but not touched). The 5 hooks: block `cd <path> && <command>` compounds (`PreToolUse:Bash`); block bare `git push origin dev` (`PreToolUse:Bash`); block Edit/Write to `src/`/`api/` when the current worktree's branch matches `session/design-*` (`PreToolUse:Edit|Write`) — the real enforcement behind "no coding in design sessions," previously discipline-only; block Read/Edit/Write/non-git-Bash targeting the shared checkout directly instead of a worktree path (`PreToolUse:Read|Edit|Write|Bash`) — mechanizes rule 3b's fallback circuit-breaker; auto-run `check-session-docs.js` after any `git worktree add` (`PostToolUse:Bash`). `jq` is not installed on this machine, so all 5 are Node scripts, not the jq/grep one-liners the `update-config` skill's own examples assume.

**Live-fire verified, not just pipe-tested:** all 5 hooks confirmed firing against real tool calls this same session (not just synthetic stdin) — the `cd &&` block caught this session's own accidental use of the pattern mid-session while re-running the hygiene check; the shared-checkout block fired on a real `Read` against `C:/Projects/deepbench-frontend/docs/FEATURES.md`; the design-session-code block fired on a real `Edit` attempt against this worktree's own `src/main.jsx` (deliberately made as a test, reverted by the block itself — no file was actually touched).

**Also found and corrected live:** `design-ses-006-0721`'s "In flight now" bullet was itself already phantom (worktree gone from `git worktree list` mid-session, confirming `SES-006` shipped via that session's own close-out but skipped removing its bullet) — removed as part of this close-out rather than left for a future hygiene pass to catch.

1 script/hook session — no `src/`/`api/` files touched (verified by the design-session-code hook itself refusing to let it happen). `npm run build` clean (912 modules, pre-existing chunk-size warning only, unrelated).

**Close-out:** `SES-010` addressed (mechanical-check candidates shipped; the local-hook enforcement portion is machine-local, not repo-portable — see caveat above). `LOG-35` (model-id violations found, not fixed) and the broader Tier-2/Tier-3 catalog (kickoff-doc section linter, serverless function count script `SE-05`, type-tag hard gate, new-ID prefix validation, Supabase schema-diff check, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` verification, deleted-test-file audit-trail gap folding into `SES-009`) remain open, not logged as individual new backlog rows per John's explicit call to stop asking about backlog-ID bookkeeping — available for a future session to pick up from this entry directly. **Follow-up, same day (`design-entry-length-gate-0721`) — `LOG-35` fixed, entry-length gate built.** After the sweep above closed out, John asked directly to fix `LOG-35`'s findings (see `S-LOG-35a`/`S-LOG-35b` entries below) and then, separately, to check which Tier 1 item from this sweep never actually got built. Answer: the `CLAUDE-STATE.md` per-entry character-count gate — check 1 only ever caught *whole-file* bloat, not one paragraph-length bullet hiding inside an otherwise-under-baseline file, which is the exact pattern behind the repeated 45KB→cleanup→bloat-again cycle. Tested against a real complete kickoff doc (0 missing) and a deliberately incomplete synthetic one (7/11 correctly flagged missing).
- `scripts/check-api-function-count.js` — mechanizes `SE-05` (Vercel Hobby's 12-function limit). **Found and fixed a real bug while testing:** first version counted every `.js` file under `api/` recursively (17), not accounting for Vercel's own routing convention that any path segment starting with `_` is excluded from routing — exactly why this repo's shared helpers live under `api/_lib/`, not `api/lib/` (confirmed via git history, a deliberate rename for this reason). Fixed to exclude underscore-prefixed segments; corrected count (12) now matches this repo's own kickoff docs, which have consistently stated "12 files in api/ today." **Real finding: the repo is at 12/12 right now, 0 remaining** — the next new `api/` route requires either merging two existing routes or moving the new capability to Railway, exactly the condition `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s kickoff-doc Step 9 already anticipates.

Fixed by diffing against the actual merge-base commit instead, isolating only this branch's own changes. Also caught fixing its own bug during testing: its regex initially only accepted the versioned `// DeepBench vX.X.X | ...` header shape, which would have flagged this session's own new scripts/ files — broadened to also accept the established unversioned `// DeepBench | ...` scripts/ convention (confirmed via `check-ai-logging-coverage.js`'s existing precedent), then added the missing `FEATURE:` comments this same check correctly caught missing from all 3 of this session's own new files.

**Remaining Tier 2 items, not started this pass:** extend `check-ai-logging-coverage.js` for `SERVICE_CATALOG.roadmap` flips, type-tag hard gate, new-ID prefix validation, Supabase schema-diff check, a local pre-commit hook (build+staged-test-file gate), and the deleted-test-file audit-trail gap (folds into `SES-009`, not its own item). **Item 7 (`eslint-plugin-react-hooks` verification) turned out not to be a simple toggle** — confirmed live (via `git show origin/dev:package.json`) that this repo has no ESLint configuration or dependency at all; enabling the rule means standing up an entire linting setup from scratch, likely surfacing a wave of pre-existing violations across the whole app. Flagged as its own decision, not attempted this pass.

One less item than the catalog assumed.

Shipped 2 more items:
- `scripts/check-new-id-prefixes.js` — mechanizes `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s new-ID prefix rule. Diffs `docs/FEATURES.md`/`FEATURES-NEXT.md`/`FEATURES-LATER.md` against the merge-base (same fix as `check-version-headers.js`'s own bug) to find genuinely *new* rows, then checks each one's prefix against a confirmed-active allowlist (`HAR`/`SCA`/`LOO`/`LOG`/`MCP`/`MKT`/`DAT`/`AGT`/`SES`/`MOB` from `SCREEN-INVENTORY.md`'s Platform Layers table, plus `CHI`/`PRO` — the only Screen codes actually observed in use in that doc's own Applied mapping table). Tested against synthetic rows (one deliberately-invalid prefix `XX-99`, one valid `CHI-999`) — correctly flagged only the first.
- Extended `check-ai-logging-coverage.js` with a `SERVICE_CATALOG.roadmap` staleness check — cross-references every non-`'now'` catalog slug against real `api/` file basenames on disk, flagging a same-named file as "confirm whether this should flip to `'now'`." This exact drift has already happened twice, caught by hand both times (`ai-patterns.js`'s own `BUG-22` comments: "prompt-assembly is live; move off roadmap", "ai-enrichment is live (16 calls); move off roadmap"). Imports `shared/ai-patterns.js` directly (confirmed zero-framework-dependency pure data file, safe in plain Node) rather than regex-parsing the catalog array as text. Hit and fixed a real Windows-specific bug while testing: dynamic `import()` of an absolute path fails on Windows without converting to a `file://` URL first (`pathToFileURL`) — fixed. Also fixed the same `process.cwd()` cwd-sensitivity bug this script already had (added `--worktree`, matching every other check-*.js script). Tested by temporarily flipping a known-live entry (`ai-enrichment`) to `roadmap: 'next'` and confirming the check caught it, then reverting. Real repo currently clean on this check (no genuine staleness right now).

Both scripts version-headered/`FEATURE:`-tagged per this session's own new `check-version-headers.js` (dogfooded immediately). Docs/tooling-only, no version bump.

**Follow-up, same day (`design-tier2-precommit-hook-0721`) — Tier 2 catalog, batch 3/3, final item.** Built the local pre-commit gate: `C:/Projects/.claude/hooks/block-unverified-commit.js`, a 6th local `PreToolUse:Bash` hook (same non-repo, machine-local caveat as `SES-010`'s original 5). Denies a `git commit` if a staged file matches `test-*.mjs`/`test-*.js` (`STANDARDS.md` §4's "deleted before committing" rule), and — only when the commit actually touches `src/`/`api/` — runs the real `npm run build` and denies on failure. Deliberately scoped to skip the build step entirely for docs/scripts-only commits (the majority of commits across 5-7 concurrent sessions on a given day) to avoid adding several seconds of friction to every commit regardless of relevance.

Hit and fixed a real Windows bug while testing: `execFileSync("npm", ...)` fails with `ENOENT` on Windows without `shell: true` (`npm` resolves to `npm.cmd`, not found via bare `PATH` lookup without shell resolution) — same class of platform gotcha as the `pathToFileURL` fix earlier this same day. Tested via 4 real scenarios in throwaway worktrees, not just synthetic stdin: staged test file → denied; passing build → silent; a deliberately broken `src/main.jsx` → denied; a docs-only staged change → silent, in 0.13s (confirms the build-skip actually skips, not just returns fast by luck). Also live-fire confirmed via an actual `git commit` call through the real Bash tool (not manual piping) — correctly blocked.

Updated `CLAUDE.md`'s mechanized-hooks note from "5 hooks" to "6 hooks, extended same day" to keep the count accurate.

**`SES-010`'s Tier 2 catalog is now fully worked through, 3 batches, same day:** all originally-listed items either shipped (this entry + the two before it) or explicitly resolved as already-done (type-tag gate) or out-of-scope-for-a-toggle (`eslint-plugin-react-hooks`, flagged separately, no ESLint exists in this repo at all). Remaining: the Supabase schema-diff check (acknowledged as the most complex item, scoping still pending) and the deleted-test-file audit-trail gap (folds into `SES-009`, not its own item, never intended to be built here). Docs-only from the repo's own perspective — the hook itself lives outside it.
