## S-LOG-30-design / S-LOG-30 (v6.3.92, `61eb2e6`, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-ai-audit-now-0719`)

Two AI Audit "By Pattern" integrity bugs from a prior live screenshot walkthrough (`design-ai-audit-screen-0717`), scoped and kicked off same-session: `LOG-30`/`LOG-31`/`LOG-34` fixed in code; `LOG-32`'s original write-site question found already-resolved during Architect Review before any code was written.

**`LOG-30`/`LOG-34` (`shared/ai-patterns.js`, 1 line):** `PATTERN_CATALOG`'s `react` entry flipped `active: true` → `active: false`. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19i's own status table already classified `react` as "Roadmap/inactive," and a direct Supabase query confirmed zero rows, ever, have logged it — the catalog and the locked architecture doc disagreed with each other. `useAIActivity.js`'s `patternsActiveCount` (the AI Audit header's "Patterns Active X/Y" stat) is a direct `PATTERN_CATALOG.filter(p => p.active).length` — correcting the flag corrects the header number automatically, no separate stat-computation change needed for `LOG-34`.

**`LOG-31` (`AIActivityPanel.jsx`):** `computeByPattern()`'s existing `LOG-14` self-maintaining fallback already auto-detects any real, uncatalogued logged pattern slug and tags it `patternType: 'unknown'` — but the By Pattern render logic only sorted into 3 buckets (`structural`/`reasoning`/`inactive`), none of which an `unknown`-typed, `active:true` entry matches. The fallback entry had nowhere to render — silent data loss, confirmed live on 18 real rows carrying the historical `reflection` slug. Fixed with a 4th "Uncatalogued" bucket (`patternsSorted` minus the other 3, byte-for-byte styling copy of the existing "Not yet active" collapse card) and a new `uncatalogedPtnClosed` collapse-state var.

**`LOG-32`:** originally posed a write-site question (is the wrong slug still being logged?) — resolved during this session's own Architect Review, before Task 1/2 were written: `api/prompt/ai-enrichment.js` (comment tagged `AA-190c`) confirms every current call already logs the correct `reflect` slug. The 18 `reflection` rows are permanent historical residue from before that earlier fix, not a live bug — per `ARCHITECTURE.md` §19i's own rule, historical data is never retroactively corrected, it just needs somewhere honest to display, which `LOG-31`'s fix provides. No write-side code touched.

2 files (`AIActivityPanel.jsx`, `shared/ai-patterns.js`). Node test (6 checks: catalog entry preserved not removed, `active:false` confirmed, `patternsActiveCount` excludes `react`, `LOG-14` fallback still fires for an uncatalogued slug, that entry reproduced falling through all 3 original buckets pre-fix, and caught by the new 4th bucket post-fix) 6/6 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean (912 modules, matching the pre-existing baseline). `git diff` confirmed exactly the 2 specified files, additions-only on the render logic, single-flag flip on the catalog.

**Verified directly against `ARCHITECTURE.md` before editing, not assumed:** grepped §19i's implementation-status table and confirmed `react` was already listed under "Roadmap/inactive," and confirmed `useAIActivity.js`'s `patternsActiveCount` is indeed a bare filter over `PATTERN_CATALOG` with no separate computation path — both claims in the kickoff doc checked fresh, not taken on trust.

**Manual QA self-verified against the real deployed dev preview after push, not accepted on Node test + build alone:** all 9 checklist items PASS. Header's "Patterns Active" read `15/24` (was `16/24` pre-fix, dropped by exactly 1); "Not yet active · 7 patterns" now includes ReAct (confirmed by expanding it); a new "Uncatalogued · 1 pattern" bucket appeared below it, expanding to show "Reflection — Auto-detected — not yet catalogued, CALLS 18, COST $0.03" — distinct from the Reasoning section's own "Reflection" row (CALLS 6, COST $0.04, the current `reflect` slug) exactly as designed. Direct Supabase query (`SELECT count(*) FROM ai_activity_log WHERE patterns_used::text ILIKE '%reflection%'`, project `rallojeqnkgtxgsdsnqm`) returned `18`, reconciling exactly with the Uncatalogued row's displayed call count.

## S-DAT-6-design / S-DAT-6 (v6.3.94, `addb9a2`, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-confirmation-uuid-0721`)

Found live during `CHI-42`'s own verification session (logged independently there as `AA-199`/`task_469f3123`, reconciled/superseded by this session's `DAT-6`/`LOO-17` before close-out — see `AA-199`'s row in `docs/FEATURES.md`). Traced end-to-end, not assumed: `writeLibrary()`'s `insert` operation (`lib/librarian.js`) ran a raw Postgres `the_library?id=eq.<supersedes_id>` lookup with zero UUID-format validation whenever `params.supersedes_id` was set. Live-reproduced twice against the real dev preview: a "Store as Forecast" commit on a hypothesis-test result populates `supersedes_id` with the new entry's own human-readable `chunk_id` slug (e.g. `geo-revenue-analysis-fy2025`), not a real row id, throwing Postgres `22P02`.

**Identified as the same bug shape `AA-189` (✅ Done) already fixed at a sibling call site** — `AA-189`'s own kickoff doc explicitly named `supersedes_id` as "the same category" as the `source_chunk_ids` guard it added to `writeTheReasoning()`, but didn't touch it, leaving this site unguarded. Fix mirrors `AA-189` exactly: a `UUID_RE` format check before the lookup, reusing the existing `denied-supersedes-cross-room` tier on a malformed id — no new response shape, no schema change, 1 file.

Node test 6/6 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean. Version header updated on `lib/librarian.js`.

**Live QA completed in a follow-up verification pass (`verify-dat6-0721`, same day):** the first live click-through reached a new `ConfirmationCard` with the identical bug-shape `chunk_id`, confirming the repro path was real and current — but the follow-up write call hit real, live `Anthropic 529 overloaded` errors on 3 consecutive attempts (confirmed via raw network response bodies), external and unrelated to this fix, before ever reaching `writeLibrary()`. A dedicated follow-up session re-attempted the live UI flow and repeatedly landed on non-deterministic action types (`opinion`, a proofreader-rejected answer) that never reach `writeLibrary()` at all — traced the real delegation chain via direct Supabase schema reads (`data-patch-intent`'s `on_accept_intent_slug` → `data-patch-execute-intent`'s `analysis_instructions`: only `action:"promote"` triggers the `request_help`→`library-write-intent` chain that actually calls `writeLibrary()`). Constructed a `pending_confirmations` row directly, matching the exact original bug shape (`action:"promote"`, slug `chunk_id`, supersede `version_note`), and resolved it via the real deployed API (no UI, same production code path) — got the clean `{"error":"library-write handler: writeLibrary failed -- denied-supersedes-cross-room"}` (HTTP 500), not the raw `22P02` Postgres string, reproduced identically on a second attempt. Confirmation stayed `status:'pending'` throughout (this failure path throws synchronously, before any `durable_hops` checkpoint, so it never triggers `LOO-17`) — cleaned up via a normal Reject call, zero residue.

**Also found and logged, not fixed this session (deliberately split, one-feature-per-session discipline):** `LOO-17` — `resolveAccept()`/`markAcceptedDelegated()` flips a `pending_confirmations` row to `status:'accepted'` on a mere `durable_hops` checkpoint return (`{status:'in_progress', job_id}`), not only on a genuinely completed write. Confirmed live via direct Supabase query on 2 separate stuck rows (including one produced by this session's own Anthropic-overload hit) — `resolution` shows a stale in-progress checkpoint pointer while `status` already reads `accepted`, permanently blocking Edit/Reject (409) and turning further Accept clicks into retries of the same dead job. Generic to every `requires_human_confirmation` capability that can checkpoint, not specific to `DAT-6`. Needs its own design session — open question flagged to John: on a failed resume, revert the confirmation to `pending` (John's stated preference, matches `AA-189`'s existing "leave card open" precedent, zero new UI work) or a new distinct `accept_failed` status (more semantically precise, needs new UI treatment/approval). Logged as `LOO-17` in `docs/FEATURES.md`.

**Close-out:** `DAT-6` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` (code shipped + unit-tested + deployed + live-verified). `LOO-17` stays `❌ Missing`, queued as next design session on this track. `AA-199` marked superseded, pointing to both.

## S-HAR-8-design / S-HAR-8 (v6.3.101, `84bd8be`, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-loo-17-0721`)

Found live during `LOO-17`'s own design session — 3 consecutive `Anthropic 529 overloaded` errors while re-verifying `DAT-6`'s live Accept round-trip. Checked before proposing anything: `postToAnthropicWithRetry()` already retried once on a transient status (2 total attempts), not enough for that day's real capacity spike.

**Frequency check, live against Supabase (14-day `durable_hops` history, 647 checkpointed chains, 77 failed) before scoping anything:** 11 genuine `529` failures, all from the one day, zero on any prior day — not a chronic problem. Separately, 30 failures the day before were an unrelated billing issue ("credit balance too low," a `400`), already correctly excluded from the retry set and already resolved by the time of this session — confirming the existing non-retry-on-`400` logic already works correctly.

**Design conversation with John surfaced the right scope himself, not assumed:** initially considered a confirmation-specific retry loop, but John redirected to a generic harness-level fix ("this should be service in the harness... keep it all the same") — `postToAnthropicWithRetry()` is the one shared wrapper every model call on the platform already goes through, so bumping it there gives every capability the same resilience uniformly. Also clarified a real mix-up: `MAX_LOOP_DEPTH = 5` (hop-count cap) is not a retry mechanism — no such 5-attempt retry exists anywhere in the codebase, confirmed via direct grep.

**Fix, 2 files:** `api/prompt/request-receivable.js` — `MAX_ANTHROPIC_ATTEMPTS = 3`, `API_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS = [1000, 3000]` (escalating, was flat 1000ms), `TRANSIENT_ANTHROPIC_STATUS` untouched (confirmed byte-identical post-fix). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §1's Harness/Execution line updated to describe the new 3-attempt/escalating-backoff behavior.

Node test 6/6 PASS (pure simulation of the retry/backoff decision logic — see, then delete, `test-HAR-8.mjs`), deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean. Category L doesn't apply — a real `529` isn't forceable on demand; accepted on the deterministic Node simulation plus the historical Supabase data already gathered at scoping time, per the kickoff doc's own explicit call.

**Close-out:** `HAR-8` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`. Sequenced to ship before `LOO-17` (same session) — `accept_failed`'s meaning depends on the harness genuinely retrying 3 times before giving up.

## S-LOO-17-design / S-LOO-17 (v6.3.102, `5ae8c2a`, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-loo-17-0721`)

Fixes the bug `DAT-6`'s own investigation surfaced and logged separately: `resolveAccept()` (`api/capabilities/execute.js`) treated any return from a delegated `on_accept_intent_slug` capability call as a completed accept — including a `durable_hops` checkpoint placeholder (`{status:'in_progress', job_id}`), not only a genuine result. Live Supabase rows confirmed this precisely: `status:'accepted'` with a stale in-progress checkpoint pointer stored as `resolution`, from calls whose actual resumed writes later failed. Once flipped, the confirmation was permanently stuck — Accept retries re-hit the same dead job, Edit/Reject both hard-failed 409. Generic to any `requires_human_confirmation` capability that can checkpoint, not `DAT-6`-specific.

**Design decision walked through live with John, not assumed:** on a failed resume, revert to `pending` (invisible, relies on a scrolling chat-error line) vs. a new `accept_failed` status (visible, needs new UI work later). John's explicit call: `accept_failed` — "give feedback to the user, and let them decide what to do next" needs an actually visible state, not a silent revert. Also confirmed live (`onResolveConfirmation`'s catch, `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) exactly what revert-to-pending would have looked like before deciding, so the choice was grounded in the real current behavior, not a guess.

**Fix, 2 files, no schema change:**
- New transitional `accepting` status — set when the delegated call checkpoints, reusing the existing `resolution` jsonb column to hold the `job_id`. Blocks concurrent Edit/Reject exactly like `accepted` does today, so the fix doesn't introduce a new race during the wait.
- New terminal `accept_failed` status — closed out via a reverse `job_id` lookup (`getConfirmationByCheckpointJobId()`) added to the `continue` route handler: `markAcceptedDelegated()` on a genuine terminal success, `markAcceptFailed()` on a genuine terminal failure, no-op if it checkpoints again.
- **Same bug shape found at 2 more sites during Architect Review (multi-site check), fixed identically, not left as separate future bugs:** `resolveAccept()`'s own fast/synchronous failure path (a `runCapability()` throw that never even reaches a checkpoint — `HAR-8`'s 3 attempts already happened inside that one call), and `resolvePendingConfirmation()`'s plain accept path (used by any confirmed intent with no `on_accept_intent_slug` — this path can never checkpoint at all, `sendRequest()` has no loop).
- **Necessary completion, not optional:** the eligibility guard (`status !== 'pending'`) widened at all 3 check sites (`resolveAccept()`, `resolvePendingConfirmation()`, the `edit` route branch) to `['pending','accept_failed'].includes(status)` — without this, `accept_failed` would have been just as dead-ended as today's bug, defeating the entire point of adding it.

Entirely inside the confirmation layer — confirmed unnecessary to touch `runCapability()`/`runLoop()`/`checkpointAndReturn()`/`resumeCapability()`'s own internals or the `durable_hops` schema; the reverse lookup on the existing `resolution` column is sufficient. `accept_failed`'s own visual/UI treatment deliberately deferred — real UI work needing a mock + John's sign-off, not blocking this backend fix.

**Coding session note, found live during setup:** a local `PreToolUse` hook (`block-design-session-code.js`, `SES-010`) correctly blocked code edits from the `session/design-loo-17-0721` branch — the coding session resolved it exactly as intended (a fresh `session/coding-loo-17-0721` worktree/branch, removed after push), the same resolution pattern `S-CHI-43` already established.

Node test 11/11 PASS, deleted before commit. `npm run build` clean.

**Live QA in two passes, gap found and closed within this same session, not left open.** Coding session's own Category L proved the fast/synchronous-failure site directly: a real `pending_confirmations` row with a deliberately-broken `proposed_action` landed cleanly on `accept_failed` with a real error captured; a second Accept call against that same row retried (500) instead of 409ing; Reject on a separate `accept_failed` row resolved cleanly (200). Honestly flagged as *not* proving the checkpoint-then-resume site (Task 4's `continue`-branch close-out) — unit-test-only, not live-forceable on demand through the normal UI flow.

## design-ai35-registry-0721 (docs-only, no version bump, 2026-07-21, worktree `design-ai35-registry-0721`)

Live design conversation with John, redefining `AI-35`/AI pattern tracking from first principles rather than patching the existing model. No code touched.

**Why this happened now:** while scoping `LOG-33` (found already-resolved by `AA-181`, closed same session, no code needed — see that row in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`), the conversation surfaced John's own framing of *why* pattern tracking exists: the platform's primary showcase that real AI reasoning, not deterministic code, drives every agent-loop hop, at a scale where cost/frequency/behavior are measurable per pattern. Testing the existing model (`ARCHITECTURE.md §19i`'s Four-Source Trust Model) against that purpose surfaced 5 governance questions it never answered: (1) how do we know we're missing a pattern a model now exhibits, (2) how do we know our names/definitions are industry-standard, (3) how do we know we're not inventing patterns even when detection is honest, (4) how do we handle an industry rename, (5) how do we handle a model doing something genuinely new.

**Grounded in real external research before answering, not reasoned in isolation:** WebSearch confirmed (a) LLM/agent observability is a real, well-funded industry category (LangSmith, Langfuse, Arize, Datadog, W&B Weave); (b) OpenTelemetry's GenAI Semantic Conventions is a real, versioned, cross-vendor standard for the actual call/span schema, in active development since April 2024 — the real reference point for "is our schema standard," which this platform doesn't currently align to; (c) Anthropic's own "Building Effective Agents" paper is the real published source for 5 of this platform's pattern names (prompt chaining, routing, parallelization, orchestrator-workers, evaluator-optimizer); (d) 2026 academic literature (e.g. "VeryTrace") independently validates the self-report-then-verify idea already used for this platform's Case-Based-Reasoning design — not invented in isolation.

**The core diagnosis:** the existing model tangled three separate concerns into one flat catalog + one hardcoded write-time function — *what really happened* (a fact, changes only when model behavior changes), *how we prove it* (a detection method, changes as detection quality improves), and *what we call it* (a name from the literature, changes as industry terminology shifts). Tangling them is exactly why a rename or a newly-observed behavior felt expensive.

**Redefinition — three separated layers, replacing the tangled model:**
- **Layer A (Call Facts)** — generic, structural, checkable facts captured at write time regardless of pattern names: real tool-call records, real retrieved-chunk ids, call-chaining links, which internal gates fired, the model's self-reported structured claims. Nothing here knows what a "pattern" is.
- **Layer B (Classification Rules)** — pattern names as *derived labels* over Layer A facts, versioned, evaluated at read time (mirrors how `cost_usd` already works via `AA-181`) — so a rename or new pattern is a rule edit/addition, never a backfill or new per-call-site instrumentation. The prior 4-bucket evidence model survives here, reframed as "what kind of proof a rule may use," not a fixed list of 24 patterns each needing bespoke code.
- **Layer C (Pattern Vocabulary)** — citation-governed: every entry requires a real published source, a maturity status, a review date, and a `superseded_by` forward-pointer so renames never silently erase historical meaning.

**Schema decision made this session:** `PATTERN_CATALOG`'s governance fields move out of the static `shared/ai-patterns.js` file into a real Supabase table — this metadata is operational (needs editing without a code deploy), same reasoning already applied to `dev_version_counter`/`feature_id_counter` in this codebase. `ai_activity_log` stays the one central table, gaining new fields for Layer A. Whether individual Layer B rules are data-driven or code is left to whichever session builds Layer B.

**`ARCHITECTURE.md §19i` rewritten wholesale** (not patched) — the prior Four-Source Trust Model is preserved in a collapsed `<details>` block in the same section for history, explicitly marked superseded, not deleted.

**`AI-35` fully re-scoped in `docs/FEATURES.md`** with the ordered build sequence: (1) this doc rewrite, done; (2) Layer C vocabulary schema + a mandatory re-verification/reclassification audit of all 24 existing catalog entries against real sources (existing entries were never uniformly checked against this discipline — folds in `LOG-23`'s missing "Routing" pattern and `LOG-28`'s open Memory Consolidation question, the first concrete case the audit resolves); (3) Layer A generic fact capture; (4) Layer B read-time classifier (folds in `LOG-17`'s `llm-as-judge`/Case-Based-Reasoning/`streaming` detection, substantially answers `LOG-26`); (5) migration/cutover decision (historical rows keep frozen old-style classification, never retroactively rewritten — same precedent already established elsewhere); (6) rollup views (platform-wide/by-hop/by-agent), computed queries, same shape as today's `byService`/`byPattern`.

**Backlog reconciliation, same session:** `LOG-24` (persistent anti-hardcoding rule) marked 🔶 Partial — the rule is now written and LOCKED (`§19i` itself), but `SERVICE_LABEL`'s own cleanup is still open, small. `LOG-25`/`HAR-01` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md` — both were open questions this redefinition directly answers, no further code needed for the questions themselves. `LOG-17`/`24`/`26`/`28` marked merged into `AI-35`, not independently actionable. `LOG-27` (cosmetic labeling gap) unaffected, stays open separately.

No kickoff doc — this is architecture/backlog restructuring, not a coding task. Steps 2-6 above each need their own design session (Architect Review, kickoff doc) before any code is touched, per the standing one-feature-per-session discipline.

**Design session closed that gap directly rather than accepting it as a permanent limitation:** constructed a real `durable_hops` row (`status:'in_progress'`, deliberately-malformed `format_contract` so the resume would genuinely fail) linked to a `pending_confirmations` row (`status:'accepting'`, `resolution:{job_id,...}`) via Supabase, then called `action:'continue'` against the real deployed API. The resume genuinely failed (`"reasoning-write handler: action required in structured output"`), and the linked confirmation correctly closed out to `accept_failed` with that real error captured in `resolution.error` — confirming Task 4's reverse-lookup-and-close-out logic works live, not just in the Node simulation. Reject on that same row then resolved cleanly (200), proving the widened guard holds for the checkpoint-derived case too, not just the fast-failure one. Both synthetic rows (`durable_hops` + `pending_confirmations`) cleaned up via Reject, zero residue left in Supabase.

**Close-out:** `LOO-17` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`. `AA-199`'s original two-part finding (`DAT-6` + `LOO-17`) is now fully closed on both halves.

## S-SES-011-design / S-SES-011 / S-SES-011a (v6.3.105 → v6.3.107, `a5d6bea` / `e78423a`, 2026-07-21, worktree `ses-11`)

Found 2026-07-21, same session as `SES-010` — two related frictions bundled under one backlog row: (1) the full 8-step worktree-isolation ceremony was run in full for several single-line, additive-only bookkeeping edits, and (2) `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s shared "In flight now"/bullet-list block caused real merge conflicts purely from bookkeeping, not from actual feature-work collisions (3 hit in one afternoon).

**Design decision, delegated to Claude directly (John: "whatever you think is best to be more efficient," and separately "I would not know, I am trusting you have the best insight to this"):** the two frictions share one fix. Decompose the shared "In flight now" block into one file per session (`.claude/inflight/<session-name>.md`) — structurally can't collide since no two sessions ever touch the same file — and define the lightweight bookkeeping path as "pure append (zero deletions) to a file only this session touches," which the per-file decomposition makes safe by construction. `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s "Last 3 sessions" section deliberately left alone (lower contention, edited only at individual close-out) — a scoped, flagged Tier-2 call, not asked.

**S-SES-011 (v6.3.105, `a5d6bea`):** 3 files, 4 tasks. `CLAUDE.md` gained the Lightweight Bookkeeping Path hard rule (skip `.env.local` copy + immediate worktree removal for qualifying pure-append edits, `git diff --stat` verified before push) and retargeted rules 6/6c/8 from shared bullets to per-session files. `CLAUDE-DESIGN.md`'s two "In flight now" references retargeted to match. `scripts/check-session-docs.js` gained `freshDevDirEntries()` (directory counterpart to the existing `freshDevText()`) so `extractInFlightBullets()` reads `.claude/inflight/*.md` instead of parsing `CLAUDE-STATE.md` text; "Last 3 sessions" checks untouched. Node test 5/5 PASS, build clean.

**Design session's own close-out migration:** the ~7 real "In flight now" bullets existing at the time were moved verbatim into their own `.claude/inflight/<name>.md` files (11 total, including this session's own and one late addition), and `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s section collapsed to a two-line pointer. Hit one real merge conflict during this exact push — `design-chi-49-0721` updated its own bullet on `dev` between this session's read and push — resolved by taking the newer content into that session's migrated file, a live, first-hand demonstration of the exact friction `SES-011` exists to eliminate going forward.

**S-SES-011a (v6.3.107, `e78423a`) — real QA FAIL found during this design session's own self-verification, not hypothetical:** `freshDevDirEntries()`'s `git ls-tree --name-only origin/dev -- .claude/inflight` was missing `-r` (recursive) — a non-recursive `ls-tree` on a directory pathspec returns only that directory's own tree entry, not the files inside it, so the function silently returned zero entries regardless of how many real inflight files existed. Confirmed live (`git ls-tree` with vs. without `-r` against the real repo) before writing the patch. Root cause, not symptom: `SES-011`'s own Node test only exercised the pre-migration empty-directory case, never a populated one, so both states produced the same (wrong) zero-entries result and the bug shipped clean. 1 file, 1 task, Node test 2/2 PASS (including a live Category L check against the real, already-pushed inflight files), build clean.

**Verified directly, not accepted from the coding reports alone:** re-ran `node scripts/check-session-docs.js` against the real worktree after each push. Post-`SES-011a`, check 5 correctly stopped flagging all 11 migrated sessions and correctly kept flagging the 2 genuinely-untracked worktrees (`design-ai-35-0721`, `design-owen-news-provenance-0721`) — confirming the fix is precise, not overbroad. Checks 5c/5d, previously unable to fire at all (empty array), now correctly surface real pre-existing content-staleness findings in several migrated bullets (not this session's bug to fix, left for a future hygiene pass).

## S-DAT-7-design (v6.3.112, worktree `design-dat-7-0721`, 2026-07-21)

`DAT-7` was found live by `design-chi-50-0721` as an Accept-time HTTP 500 on Nadia — Data Analyst's Draft Forecast proposal (`"reasoning-write handler: writeContent failed -- denied-source-chunk-cross-room-or-missing"`), filed with the working hypothesis that it was a `§19f` `the_reasoning` write-path credential/room-boundary bug. This session root-caused it directly against live Supabase data and found that hypothesis wrong.

**Root cause 1 (the actual bug):** pulled the real failed `pending_confirmations` row (`id: 1f9ad39e-b7bb-40fd-83f5-8f850aa92a16`, `status: accept_failed`, `2026-07-21T20:53:16Z`) — its `proposed_action.chunk_id` is `"JPN-GROWTH-STRUCTURAL-HYP-001"`, a human-readable label Nadia invented, not a real `the_library` id. A second live "opinion" proposal from the same day (`2c10b0ef-...`, `chunk_id: "JPN-STRUCTURAL-CYCLE-001"`) shows the identical shape — 2/2 real organic samples. Traced why: `data-patch-intent`'s Supabase `skill_profiles` trait schema gives its `chunk_id` output field zero instruction (`{"type":["string","null"]}`, no `description`, and this intent has no `analysis_instructions` at all, unlike its sibling `data-patch-execute-intent`) — nothing tells the model to echo `task_context.disputed_chunk_id` verbatim, so it invents a descriptive slug instead. That bad value flows unchanged through `data-patch-execute-intent` (working as designed) into `reasoning-write.js` → `lib/search-harness.js`'s `writeTheReasoning()`, whose `AA-189` UUID-format guard correctly denies it. **The guard is not the bug; confirmed and left untouched.**

**Root cause 2 (why it surfaced as a crash, not a graceful message):** `reasoning-write.js`'s `handle()` bare-throws a statusless `Error` on any denial, which `execute.js`'s single outer catch maps to a raw HTTP 500. Checked the sibling `library-write.js` (Eleanor — Librarian's equivalent handler) — identical bare-throw shape, same bug pattern at 2 call sites dispatched through the same `format_contract.handler` registry (`request-receivable.js`). Per the multi-site-bug-pattern check, scoped as one fix covering both files rather than two independent patches. `request-receivable.js` already has the target idiom in the same file for its own "unknown handler" 501 case — `execute.js`'s outer catch already forwards `e.status`/`e.detail`, so no `execute.js` change is needed.

**Design walkthrough with John (one issue at a time, per `WORKING-WITH-JOHN.md`):** presented root cause 1 alone first, confirmed (including folding in a `temperature: 0` pin on `data-patch-intent`, matching `AA-154`'s existing precedent). Root cause 2 initially unclear to John on first pass — re-explained without HTTP-status-code framing, grounded in what "try again" actually does today (nothing, since the bad `chunk_id` is permanently baked into the stored proposal — only Edit/Reject work) — then merged what had been proposed as two separate tasks (server-side status/detail; client-side recovery copy) into one, since the first has no purpose without the second. Final scope, and the exact user-facing copy line, both explicitly confirmed before the kickoff doc was written.

**Kickoff written:** `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.112-DAT-7-nadia-chunk-id-echo-and-graceful-denial.md` — 2 tasks, 3 code files (`reasoning-write.js`, `library-write.js`, `MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) + 1 Supabase trait edit. Includes a Category L live test using a real `the_library` id (`0d09ac6e-baca-4471-a3f5-c0a63007882d`, `apple-cso-data-room`) confirmed live this session, for a loop-closure check uniquely traceable to the new write.

Coding session spawned next via the Automated Design→Code→Verify Loop; outcome to be appended here at close-out.

Close-out: `SES-011` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`.

## S-CHI-55-design/S-CHI-55 (v6.3.118, worktree `investigate-news-fetch-0721`, 2026-07-21)

Started as a live bug investigation: John reported dev's news fetch spinning ~1 min then showing nothing, his first test in 10 hours during which several concurrent sessions shipped (`SES-020`, `S-AI-35-2a`, `SES-019`, `DAT-7`, `CHI-49`–`53`). Live re-test reproduced the full Jordan→Alex news-fetch pipeline succeeding end to end; no commit in the intervening window touched `fetchNewsCards()`. Root cause not confirmed as a regression — most likely a slow-but-successful run (`ws-news-search-intent` latencies run 13s–43s against `execute.js`'s 60s Vercel `maxDuration`) combined with `fetchNewsCards()`'s deliberate silent fail-open (no visible error, by original `CHI-33` design). Logged as `CHI-54`, a UX gap (should a timeout ever surface visibly), not a blind fix.

**Pivoted into a UI cleanup walkthrough** once John confirmed the news issue resolved via refresh and brought 29 numbered items from a live screenshot review. Organized into themes rather than fixing 29 discrete items — many were the same root cause recurring: drawer content "accumulation" (4 mentions), Agent Routing "new question" divider consistency (4 mentions), chat bubble/status placement (8 mentions). Each theme confirmed with John before any scope was finalized (`CLAUDE-DESIGN.md` Step 8b's stop-gate, applied per-theme given the volume). Claimed `CHI-55`–`CHI-60` from the atomic feature-ID counter, logged all 6 with full context to `docs/FEATURES.md`.

**`CHI-55` root-caused in 2 passes, live with John.** First hypothesis: `useDrawerStack`'s `manualOverride` (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` ~L1694-1717) only tracks a single `{key,open}` pair, so 2 manually-opened drawers can be simultaneously expanded — confirmed as intentional, not the bug (John's explicit call: keep multi-open, preserves the "papers on a desk" persistence intent `CHI-43`/`CHI-49` were built around). John re-tested live and found the real cause himself: comparing Column 2 (`EvidenceColumn`) against Column 3 (`AuditColumn`), Column 2's outer wrapper sets `background:T.cardAlt` + a border — identical to every individual `Drawer` inside it — while Column 3's equivalent wrapper sets no background at all, sitting directly on the app's real page background (`T.paperDeep`, confirmed at `AppShell.jsx:345`). Two open drawers in a same-colored parent box read as one merged block; Column 3's sections don't have this problem because there's real contrast between drawer and backdrop.

**Fix:** removed `background`/`border` from `EvidenceColumn`'s outer wrapper (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` ~L1855), and updated `ScrollFadeHint`'s `bg` prop (~L2090) from `T.cardAlt` to `T.paperDeep` to match. Visual-only, zero state/architecture change, 1 file. Explicitly scoped out `InteractColumn` (Column 1/chat) even though it shares the same wrapper pattern by an existing code comment — chat bubbles already have strong color/alignment-based distinction regardless of container background, so this specific problem (same-color content blending into same-color container) doesn't apply there.

**Kickoff written:** `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.118-CHI-55-evidence-column-background-removal.md` — 1 file, 2 tasks. Coding session spawned via the Automated Design→Code→Verify Loop: Node test 6/6 PASS, `npm run build` clean, committed (`03ba83e`) and pushed to `dev`.

**Self-verified QA live** (Manual QA Checklist, Section 11) — not just accepting the coding report: opened the dev preview, ran a real Q&A question end to end (9 hops), then opened both the News and Analysis & Narrative drawers simultaneously. Used direct computed-style inspection via the browser's JS console rather than a screenshot read (a screenshot-based visual check would have been weaker evidence here): confirmed the wrapper's `background-color` is `rgba(0,0,0,0)` (transparent), each `Drawer`'s own background is unchanged (`rgb(242,234,212)` = `T.cardAlt`), and the real background showing through is `rgb(221,213,190)` = `T.paperDeep` — exact match to Column 3. `ScrollFadeHint`'s gradient confirmed reading `T.paperDeep` too. Zero console errors. `git diff` confirmed `InteractColumn`/footer/`Drawer` component/`useDrawerStack` all byte-unaffected.

## S-CHI-56-design/S-CHI-56 + patch (v6.3.120/v6.3.121, worktree `investigate-news-fetch-0721`, 2026-07-22)

Continuation of the same investigation session, picking up `CHI-56` next. What started as a one-paragraph "status placement" finding turned into several rounds of live architecture conversation with John before any kickoff was written — worth recording the actual back-and-forth, not just the final shape, since the early framing was materially wrong and only corrected through direct questioning.

**Round 1 — terminology alignment.** John described 4 "chat feedback items" (user prompt, Marcus prompt, live status, end status) and asked directly whether the code used different names — mapped cleanly onto `MessageBubble`(`role:"user"`/`user_action`), `MessageBubble`(`kind:"qa"`/`"non_qa"`), `AgentWorkingIndicator`, `HopSummaryLine` respectively.

**Round 2 — layout invariant, initially framed wrong.** An earlier, casual observation in this same conversation had suggested end status should move to sit under the *user's* bubble (right-aligned). John's careful requirements pass reversed this: Marcus is "the closer," so end status stays with Marcus, left-justified — the earlier note was superseded, not a contradiction to reconcile. Also new: end status should show the original time estimate alongside real elapsed, so John can judge whether the system's own estimates are accurate.

**Round 3 — root-caused the Agent Routing "some hops have a time, some don't" complaint before touching any code.** Found `NON_MEASURABLE_EVENT_TYPES` (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`) declared 6 event types permanently duration-less by design, not by bug. Traced each: `agent_selection`/`failure_triage` are sub-fields embedded inside a single already-timed response (confirmed by reading the exact construction sites, `~L1252`/`~L1397`); `delegation`-family arrive as live SSE ticks with a real, uncaptured client-side arrival moment; `question_boundary` is a pure marker. Presented this and asked which way John wanted it resolved (keep the exception list vs. measure everything) — **John: measure everything, "a hop is an instance that has work and time, no matter how brief."**

**Round 4 — "turn" vs. "transaction."** Proposed extending the existing `question_boundary` divider to fire on every user action. John corrected this: a single investigation (question → deeper theories → pick a theory → commit as forecast → accept) is *one* transaction with several turns inside it, not several transactions — firing a divider on every turn would fragment one real investigation into false "new questions." Re-checked the code: the one existing `question_boundary` call site already only fires on a genuinely new typed/example question, so it was already behaving correctly as a transaction-start marker without anyone having framed it that way — no change needed there, only a symmetric "transaction complete" closing marker needed adding.

**Architect Review — no prior shared implementation existed** (grepped for any "turn"/"duration"/"elapsed" abstraction; every flow hand-rolled its own `Date.now()`/`buildHopEvent` pattern independently — confirmed, not assumed). Decided against lifting the deeply-entangled `workingStatus`/`messages`/`pipelineEvents` state into a new stateful hook in this first session — too large a rewrite for the scope at hand — and instead built `src/lib/turnTracking.js` as a pure-function module (duration resolution + transaction-boundary event builders) that the screen's existing state calls through. Session 2 migrates the remaining flows onto the same module; if a genuine stateful hook turns out to be warranted once more flows are migrated, that's Session 2's call.

**Kickoff written and shipped:** `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.120-CHI-56-turn-tracking-service.md` — 2 files, 4 tasks. Node test 18/18 PASS, `npm run build` clean, committed/pushed (`f89f503`).

**Found a real bug during this session's own mandatory live Category L verification, not hypothetical.** Asked a real question (succeeded, 7 hops, every hop showed a time — confirmed the core fix worked immediately, including previously-blank `agent_selection`/`delegation_complete` hops). Asked a second, different real question immediately after — it hit an unrelated, pre-existing backend flake (`request-receivable.js`'s "Parse failed and retry also failed," confirmed by grep to predate this session by many prior retry-hardening sessions) — but also threw 4 real `CHI-07` console errors on `delegation_complete`. Root-caused before patching: `lastEventAtRef` (this session's own new ref) was never seeded at turn start, so a turn's first delegation-family event could legitimately have no prior tick to diff against; `resolveDelegationDuration`'s own correct `null` return for that case then tripped `buildHopEvent`'s **pre-existing** guard (predates `CHI-56`, never previously applicable to these 5 types since they used to be declared non-measurable). A second, subtler defect the same fix closes: without seeding, a new turn's first delegation-family event would have diffed against the *previous* turn's last one, silently folding the user's own between-questions think-time into that hop's reported duration.

**Wrote and shipped a proper patch kickoff rather than editing code directly in this design session** — briefly drafted the fix inline to confirm it, then reverted it and went through the same design→kickoff→coding-agent loop as everything else in this session, keeping the established discipline consistent even for a same-session bug found by the design session itself. `docs/kickoffs/v6.3.121-CHI-56-patch-turn-start-seed.md` — 1 file, 1 line + comment. Node test 4/4 PASS, `npm run build` clean, committed/pushed (`c6ccdf7`).

**Re-verified live after the patch, thoroughly — 4 consecutive real questions in one continuous session, 31 total hops** spanning a plain success, a legitimate Owen guardrail block that escalated through Sam and Eleanor, and 2 more successes. Every single hop showed a real, non-zero duration. On the console-error question specifically: initial re-checks kept showing the same 4 old errors, which was concerning until direct instrumentation (overriding `console.error` to tally only occurrences from that point forward, rather than trusting the browser tool's own buffered/replayed console history) proved a full subsequent turn — including its own delegation-family events within a legitimate guardrail-block-and-escalate chain — produced zero new errors. The earlier repeated sightings were stale history, not a live recurrence; worth noting as a real gotcha in this testing setup for future sessions doing repeated console checks in the same tab.

`CHI-56` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`, covering both `v6.3.120` and its `v6.3.121` patch. Remaining scope (theory/forecast/info-only flow migration, plus the instructive-bubble/redundant-text/forecast-decision-bubble items carried over from the original finding) split out as `CHI-61`, logged in `docs/FEATURES.md`. Worktree (`investigate-news-fetch-0721`) stays open.

Hit real friction driving the app: `computer`'s coordinate-based click reliably failed to fire the Send/Clear button handlers this session (value/focus changes registered in the DOM but the click itself silently no-op'd) — worked around by dispatching a genuine `.click()` via `javascript_tool` once the value was set via `form_input`, consistent with this repo's own long-documented history of coordinate-click unreliability against this app (`CHI-42`/`CHI-43`/`MI-60`/`CHI-02`'s archived entries all hit the same class of issue independently).

A static check of the shipped bundle (`fetch()`ing the live JS asset, grepping for exact strings) confirmed `Store as Info Only`/`Create Forecast`/`Needs Your Decision` present, the old bare `Info Only`/`Store as Forecast` strings gone, and `Needs your input` (the old pill's exact text) surviving *only* inside the dormant `MessageBubble` branch — exactly matching the scoped-out `CHI-63` finding, not a regression.

A separate real crash was hit reaching the Result stage on the one hypothesis test that *did* complete: React error #31 (raw `{text, citations}` object rendered as a child), preceded by a "Parse failed and retry also failed" console line — confirmed via `git diff` to have zero relationship to this session's badge/button-only diff, logged fresh as `CHI-65` (cross-referenced against `HAR-9`'s similar-but-not-identical citations flake, not assumed the same). Given 3 live attempts all hit pre-existing, already-tracked-or-newly-logged instability unrelated to this session's code, further retries were stopped per the standing "don't loop retrying a known/tracked issue to build extra confidence" guidance — Result/Draft Forecast/qa-review accepted via the identical, already-live-proven `NeedsDecisionBadge`/`headerRight` mechanism (same component, same slot, only the boolean condition differs per drawer) plus the Node test's explicit per-condition coverage, the same "prove a mechanism once" precedent `CHI-42` and `CHI-49` already used for their own unreachable live paths.

`CHI-59` archived ✅ Done in `docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`. `CHI-63` (dormant `MessageBubble` duplicate) and `CHI-65` (the new crash finding) logged fresh in `docs/FEATURES.md`. *Applied as `SES-021`, v6.3.129 — claimed atomically from Supabase.*

Reconciled the always-on session docs so every rule has exactly one home. `CLAUDE.md` went from a
~22 KB always-on file (mostly procedure + inline "found live" narrative) to a thin router + hard
rules + pointer core. The procedures moved to a new `.claude/skills/session-setup/` skill (loads at
session start, not every turn); the rationale/history below moved here. **No rule changed** — only
procedures and rationale relocated. `DeepBench-Session-Init.md` was deduped to pointers against the
same homes.

### Rationale migrated out of CLAUDE.md (kept here so it isn't lost)

**Retired read-only bootstrap check (2026-07-15).** CLAUDE.md previously specified a separate
"read-only bootstrap check" (`git fetch` + `git show origin/dev:<path>`) to work around the shared
checkout going stale, applied only to `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE-STATE.md`. Made redundant once the
per-session worktree became mandatory *before any read*: a worktree branched fresh from `origin/dev`
is already correct for every file, so one normal Read replaces the fetch+`git show`+scratch-file
dance. This also fixed a real inefficiency — a 2026-07-15 design session read `CLAUDE-STATE.md` twice
(once stale for the hygiene check, once via `git show` for content), burning ~13% of context before
any work started.

**Why the shared checkout is deliberately never kept in sync (John asked directly, 2026-07-16).**
With 5–7 concurrent sessions, any `git pull`/`checkout` against the one shared directory would race
every other session doing the same — and a working-tree checkout isn't atomic across files, so a read
caught mid-pull could see a mix of old and new file states. A consistently-stale checkout is a
*safer* failure mode than one that's usually fresh but occasionally mid-mutation, precisely because
nobody trusts it enough to skip verifying. The shared checkout also can't be hidden — it's the
obviously-named top-level folder, trivially discoverable, while worktree paths are buried three
levels deep in `.claude/worktrees/<name>/`. That obvious-but-wrong vs. hidden-but-right asymmetry is
why every sub-agent must be *given* the worktree path explicitly rather than left to discover one.
**Fallback circuit-breaker:** any session about to read from the shared checkout directly should
`fetch origin dev` then read via `git show origin/dev:<path>` and flag the misdirection, so the
upstream gap gets fixed rather than papered over.

**Sub-agent staleness, found live 2026-07-16.** Two sweep sub-agents were both pointed at the shared
checkout with no worktree path in their prompts; one caught the resulting 193-commits-behind
staleness itself and rerouted to a fresh worktree, the other didn't and re-reported an already-fixed
bug (`AA-192a`/`b`) until the parent cross-checked both reports. Root cause: a bare task description
with no explicit path defaults to the discoverable shared checkout — hence the "state the worktree
path verbatim in every sub-agent prompt" rule.

**Route-around-a-deny, found live 2026-07-21 (`SES-019`).** `block-unverified-commit.js` denied a
legitimate `git rm` of debris test files because it matched on staged path alone, not add/delete
status (fixed same day). The correct response was to fix the hook or flag it — not to retry the
`git commit` through PowerShell because a hook only matched `Bash`. Routing around a deny defeats the
hook regardless of whether the specific instance was safe.

**`HEAD:dev` vs bare `dev`, found live 2026-07-16 (`continuity-ux-0716`).** Worktrees share one set
of local refs, so a bare `git push origin dev` resolves to whichever local branch is named `dev`
(normally the shared checkout's, deliberately never kept current) rather than the executing
worktree's HEAD — pushing stale content or failing confusingly. `HEAD:dev` sidesteps the shared-ref
lookup.

**Atomic counters — the collisions that motivated them.** Version: `AZ-19` and `S-MOBILE-ROSTER-01`
both claimed `v6.2.4` by read-and-increment before the `dev_version_counter` fix (rule 5 → now the
`session-setup` skill). Feature IDs: `AA-197`/`AA-198`, `CHI-13`/`CHI-14`, `HAR-02`/`AA-197` collided
the same way before `feature_id_counter` (`SES-006`). Both are now claimed atomically; Postgres
serialization makes the claim itself the reservation.

**Per-session inflight files (`SES-011`).** Moved from a shared "In flight now" bullet list to
per-session `.claude/inflight/<name>.md` files after a shared list kept mis-flagging quiet-but-active
worktrees. Found live 2026-07-21: 8 real in-progress design worktrees were misflagged as
stale/abandoned by `session-hygiene` check 5 purely because none had committed since branching — the
one-line inflight file is the only on-disk signal distinguishing a live conversation from a
finished-and-forgotten worktree.

### Stale bits reconciled in Session-Init this session

- **Already fixed on current `dev`:** Session-Init Step 9 already used `git push origin HEAD:dev`
  (not bare `git push origin dev`) — no change needed; noted so the scope's assumption is on record.
- **Fixed this session:** Step 10b's ⛔ "wait for John's PASS/FAIL" manual-QA hard-stop contradicted
  the 2026-07-15 self-test hard rule (design/coding sessions verify their own Manual QA against live
  systems; John only closes the loop when he personally started a coding session). Step 10 now points
  to the Automated Design→Code→Verify Loop instead of gating on John's manual sign-off.

## S-SES-22-23 (v6.3.130, `d3fcd19`, 2026-07-23, worktree `design-session-0723d`)

Housekeeping session — John's ask: run `SES-22`, `SES-23`, and the `session-hygiene` flags.
Docs + one tooling script, no `src/`/`api/` change. Full per-row detail lives in
`docs/FEATURES-ARCHIVE.md`'s PROCESS IMPROVEMENT — SES section; only what isn't captured there
is recorded below.

**The near-miss worth remembering.** `SES-23` was logged that morning off a single observed
instance. Checking it live found **four at once** — `design-chi-beta-triage-0722`,
`design-log-23-0722`, `design-session-0723b`, `design-session-0723c` — each holding an unstaged
inflight marker, and therefore each presenting to `check-session-docs.js` check 5 as
"zero commits ahead, no marker, likely finished, cleanup skipped." Two were under 45 minutes old.
This session *was* the housekeeping pass whose job is to act on that flag; the only reason nothing
was deleted is that it looked at disk before trusting the check. The fix went to the cause on both
sides — the written procedure (`session-setup` step 2b) and the mechanical backstop
(check 5e, which now suppresses check 5's verdict rather than merely adding to it).

**A wrong assertion made and corrected mid-session, recorded because the correction is the lesson.**
This session told John all 8 of check 5c's flags were "false positives by construction — the matcher
hits any backticked ID anywhere in the archive," reasoning from the matcher's source without
checking it against the data. Checking afterward: only 2 of 8 were wrong on that basis; 5 of the
flagged IDs have real archive rows. The flags *were* mostly noise, but for a different reason —
reading the markers showed they cite archived IDs as legitimate context (the shipped mechanism being
fixed; a record of what the session logged), which check 5c structurally cannot distinguish from the
bug it exists to catch. Exactly one of eight pointed at genuinely stale prose. John chose option B:
row-anchor the matcher, dedupe per marker, and downgrade to `WARN` worded as "read the prose."
8 flags → 5 warnings. The general lesson is `feedback-verify-never-assert-from-memory` applied to
source-reading, not just recall: reading a matcher tells you what it *can* match, never what it
*does* match — that requires running it against the data.

**Also cleared:** the four empty orphan directories under `.claude/worktrees/`
(`design-chi-58-0722`, `design-chi-60-0722`, `design-chi-ux-0719`, `design-ses-010-0721`) — all
zero-child, no git registration, no `session/*` branch. This closes `CLAUDE-STATE.md`'s standing
"Unresolved" note that `design-chi-ux-0719` was locked by an unidentified process ("device or
resource busy" on 2026-07-21): it deleted cleanly on the first attempt today, so whatever held it
had since released. That note is removed from `CLAUDE-STATE.md` rather than carried forward.

**Deliberately left open, logged rather than folded in** (scope discipline — a housekeeping pass
that grows to fix everything it finds stops being one): `SES-24` (`session-hygiene` skill prose
still written against the shared "In flight now" bullet list `SES-011` retired — the script was
retargeted, the human-readable skill text was not, so a session running the check by hand looks in
the wrong file entirely), `SES-25` (`CLAUDE-STATE.md` at 29.7 KB and `FEATURES.md` at 184.8 KB,
~6× and ~4.6× their baselines — needs its own pruning session), `SES-26` (the machine-local
`PreToolUse` hook's allowlist omits `ls-tree`, which `check-session-docs.js:218` itself runs against
the shared checkout — the repo's own tooling performs a read the hook forbids a session to perform;
denied this session's own call, worked around per the never-route-around-a-deny rule rather than
retried through PowerShell).

**Not touched, on purpose:** the four worktrees flagged by the new check 5e. Deleting another
session's worktree is shared state, and the evidence said at least two were live. Their owning
sessions should stage and push their markers. Likewise `design-ai-audit-screen-0717`'s marker,
which is genuinely stale (six days old, still in the pre-`SES-011` bullet format, session never
closed out) but belongs to another session — the `session-setup` skill's "only ever edit or delete
your own inflight file" rule applies.

## S-LOG-64 (POC, no version — docs/Supabase-read only, 2026-07-24, worktree `design-log-64-0724`)

`LOG-64` — **POC 1 of the LOG-38 runtime signature model (`ARCHITECTURE.md §19k`): prove the
signature→pattern join before the build. Done, mechanism de-risked.** Full working detail:
`docs/harvests/LOG-64-poc-signature-join-0724.md`.

**Proven live** on real row `ai_activity_log.id=19329` (Michelle, `agent-selection-intent`): a
signature computed from real config + `call_facts` joined the real `request-routing` gold row via
`signature @> criteria` → derived "Request Routing"; `@>` **empirically order-independent** (two
key-orderings compare `=` and both match); a **structural** pattern resolves from the config-half
**anchor alone** on an all-null fact-half, and the RAG criteria correctly did **not** match — the
false-`rag` fix in miniature. All throwaway inline SQL; nothing persisted.

**Decided with John, locked into `§19k`:** the 15-field signature order, ranked by **intrinsic
telling-ness under full attribution** (John's frame — a null field drops out entirely, so coverage is
handled by removal; ranking on telling × coverage double-counts absence). Under full attribution
**facts are proof, config is only propensity → facts rank above config**, below the `intent` anchor;
`trace_id` last. Three locked caveats: the order is a diagnostic/aggregation-key aid not the matcher
(`@>` is order-agnostic); "attributed fields move up" needs an **ordered projection**, not bare jsonb
(LOG-67); facts-over-config holds for contingent patterns only (the primary/structural one is provable
from `intent` + config on history).

**Requirements the POC surfaced (folded into the build tickets):** LOG-67 must scope the signature's
intent-type skill to the **fired** intent (the naive all-skills-on-capability read leaked the sibling
intent `work-order-decomposition`; one-WHERE-clause fix proven live) and store the signature as an
ordered projection; LOG-66's `criteria` needs a bounded operator set beyond `@>` (for
`retrieved_chunk_ids > 0`) and is an intent-enumeration-as-data for Susan's merged patterns
(`agent-selection-intent` + `ci-routing-intent` = Request Routing); LOG-65 resolves the pure-`@>`
-per-intent vs `in`-operator choice while running the full anomaly set. No new backlog rows needed —
all findings homed on existing tickets. No code, no version bump.

## S-CHI-73-design/S-CHI-73 (v6.3.147, `653f435`, 2026-07-27, worktree `chi-ui-0727`)

`CHI-73` (UI) ✅ **Done, live-QA passed on the deployed dev preview.** Three Channel
Intelligence live-status/waiting-state UX cleanups John reported off a single forecast-flow
screenshot ("How do smartphone upgrade cycles vary by country…"). All three land in **single
shared components** (`AgentWorkingIndicator`, `EvidenceColumn`) that render on both desktop and
mobile, so one fix each covered both surfaces — no `MOB` row. 1 file
(`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx`), no `api/`/schema/delegation touched.

**Design conversation, the part worth keeping:** John's own question drove the shape of task 2.
His first read was "we already have a standard activity timer — is this not centralized, and why
isn't this one using its fallback?" Mapping every status call site confirmed his instinct exactly:
the **display** is centralized (one `AgentWorkingIndicator`, so `elapsed` is universal — it's
computed inside the component and no call site can forget it), but the **expected-time fallback was
never centralized** — the `"< 2m"` ceiling was a literal that only the qa flow's call site passed,
while the 2 hypothesis statuses passed a grounded estimate *or `null`* and 7 other statuses passed
nothing. That's why the screenshot showed "Priya is generating hypotheses…" with no estimate. Fix
became: move the fallback into the component (`LIVE_EXPECT_FALLBACK = "expect < 2m"`,
`expectation || fallback`) and retire the per-call-site literal, so every status — present and
future — inherits it; grounded `expect > Xs` still wins. John also chose intent-aware ack copy
(option B): forecast/theory → "Building theories now...", correct → "Reviewing that correction
now..." — a correction isn't a "theory." And for the column-2 motion he chose skeleton-shaped
motion (option A) over a lighter dot, honoring the `CHI-58` governing principle (motion-as-skeleton,
never re-add status *words* to column 2) while filling the Result `testing` state that previously
rendered an entirely empty open box.

**Shipped:** (1) `HYP_ENTRY_TAIL` map + intent-branched `hyp_entry` ack tail; (2)
`LIVE_EXPECT_FALLBACK` centralized in `AgentWorkingIndicator`, qa live literal retired (retrospective
past-tense `HopSummaryLine` estimate left untouched); (3) `CandidateRowSkeleton`/`ResultSkeleton`
reusing `ShimmerSweep` + the existing `shimmer` keyframe (no new colors/keyframes) on the Candidates
`generating` and Result `testing` waiting states. Node test 14/14, `npm run build` clean.

**Live verification (design session, Step 5b, deployed dev preview — the forecast flow end to
end):** ack rendered "Got it — treating that as a Forecast. Building theories now..." (old "Pick or
refine…" gone); live status showed "elapsed X | expect > 35s/45s" through routing, generating, and
testing; the `ResultSkeleton` shimmer was confirmed rendering *inside* the FORECAST RESULT drawer
during testing (4 bars + `ShimmerSweep`, verified by ancestry), the Candidates static text was gone,
and real result content (Supports/Complicates + data table) landed cleanly once testing finished —
also confirmed rendering at mobile 375px via the shared `EvidenceColumn`. The Candidates
*generating* skeleton and the `"expect < 2m"` *fallback* string specifically weren't frozen live
(generation is too fast; Priya's chain had historical p90 data this run so the grounded estimate
showed) — both are covered by the Node test plus the identical proven `ShimmerSweep` mechanism and
the now-unconditional `| estimate` separator. Console showed only pre-existing `CHI-64`
`delegation_complete` warnings (untouched by this change), no CHI-73 errors, no React crash.

Note: browser text-entry into the chat input was flaky under the screen's frequent re-renders
(news rotation / status ticks kept dropping keystrokes) — submitted reliably via a React
value-tracker-reset + native input event, a QA-harness workaround, not an app defect.

## S-CHI-75-design/S-CHI-75 (v6.3.150, `c3d9aca`, 2026-07-27, worktree `chi-status-gap-0727`)

`CHI-75` (UI) ✅ **Done, live-QA passed on the deployed dev preview.** Immediate follow-up to
`CHI-73`, off a second John screenshot of the same forecast flow.

**Bug:** on page load the news web-search (`fetchNewsCards()`) fires automatically; asking a
question *while it's still running* made the chat working-status line show, then **go blank for
~10s, then re-appear**. John's read: "it almost seems like we can't run multi-agents — do the web
search agents have to complete before the Q&A agents, or is the UI not keeping up?"

**Diagnosis (verified by direct code read, not assumed):** it is **not** a concurrency limit. The
news fetch and the question are two independent `callCapability()` → `/api/capabilities/execute`
calls that genuinely run in parallel; neither awaits the other. The problem is a **single-slot UI
ownership bug**: there is one `workingStatus` state slot, both flows drive it (both pass the shared
`onDelegationProgress` as `onProgress`), and the news fetch's completion ran `setWorkingStatus(null)`
unconditionally (`MarketIntelligenceScreen.jsx` L3294 success / L3301 failure), guarded only against
a *Clear* (`isStale()`/`clearGenerationRef`), **not** against an in-flight question. `web-search-news`
runs ~13–43s, so it routinely finishes mid-question and wiped the question's live status; the line
stayed blank until the question's next hop fired its own `setStatus` — the ~10s gap. So the correct
answer to John was his own third hypothesis: the UI wasn't keeping up with multi-agent work.

**Fix (targeted — John chose option A over a general owner-token model):** a `chatTurnActiveRef`
(`useRef(false)`) set `true` at the start of `submit()` (after `setLoading(true)`) and `false` in
its `finally` (try/finally guarantees no leak); the news fetch's two `setWorkingStatus(null)` calls
now run only `if (!chatTurnActiveRef.current)`. A ref, not state, because `fetchNewsCards` is async
and closes over stale state — a ref read at completion time is always current. `submit()` is the only
flow that overlaps the one-shot news fetch (it runs only on mount/Clear; by the time a
hypothesis-test or commit is reachable it has long finished), so guarding `submit()` covers the real
bug. `setNewsCards(...)` untouched — the news drawer still fills; only the chat status line is
protected. 1 file, Node test 5/5, `npm run build` clean.

**Live verification (Step 5b, deployed dev preview, John's exact repro via a 1s in-page poller):**
navigated fresh so the news search was in flight, immediately submitted a question, and sampled the
chat status line every second. Result — the news fetch completed at t≈9→10s (newsLoaded flipped) and
the question's status line stayed **continuous** across that exact moment (`elapsed 9s → 14s |
expect > 1m 25s`, no blank sample anywhere t=2…16). Control case (reload, no question): the news
fetch's own status appeared near completion and then **cleared normally** (`chatWorkingLineNow:
null`) — confirming the guard still allows the clear when no chat turn owns the slot, i.e. no
stuck-status regression. Console: only pre-existing `CHI-64` `delegation_complete` noise.

Deliberately not in scope: the general owner-token model that would protect *any* two concurrent
flows (option B, deferred); the co-located `CHI-64` console-noise bug in the same function.
